### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: a `gitRepository` `gitRef` is a branch or tag name, and registration rejects a value matching `^[0-9a-f]{40}$` (SPEC-2 Edit (b); enforced by CODE-2 `validateSharedAssets` via `workspaceplan.IsCommitSHA` and a per-entry CEL rule, pinned by TEST-1 item 12) — BECAUSE a store fill sends no haves, so a 40-hex want takes SPEC-5's commit branch and gets a shallow pack, which `index-pack --strict` rejects without a `shallow` file, and which would break rule 3 deltas that exclude everything reachable from the store commit — ALTERNATIVES: keying SPEC-5's full-history branch on a store-fill request (needs a request-purpose field Decision 16 declines; rewrites SPEC-5, SPEC-7, proto, handler); a shallow store with a `shallow` file (rule 3 deltas then miss ancestors); wording only with no rejection (nothing enforces it, the whole pool fails warming).
WATCHOUT: do not align §14 `gitClone.ref` "(branch, tag, or commit SHA)" with the gitRepository rule; the divergence is deliberate because the session path pins a SHA through `resolvedCommitSha` and asks for a commit with haves. — EVIDENCE: spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md:91
WATCHOUT: the CRD path bypasses the admin validator (`applyCRDFields` mirrors CRD `sharedAssets`), so the CEL rule is the only enforcer there; dropping it from CODE-2 item 4 reopens the hole. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 3 and item 4
FACT: `workspaceplan.IsCommitSHA` is the existing lowercase 40-hex predicate, and `gitref.pickSHA` resolves only ls-remote names (branches and tags). — EVIDENCE: pkg/workspaceplan/refresolve.go:86, pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/gitref.go:137
DECISION: SPEC-2 makes a fill that does not complete within "the adapter's fill deadline" a warm-fill failure and states no value; CODE-4 step 2.1 (`--git-store-fill-timeout`, default 15m) stays the value's home — BECAUSE TEST-1 item 7 and the CODE-4 deadline need a spec basis, and nothing else bounds `warming` (the §11.3 watchdogs cover other states; WarmPoolBootstrapping is pool-level) — ALTERNATIVES: stating 15 minutes in spec (two homes, code tuning in spec); a §17.8.1 row (no deployer sets the flag; neither controller nor Helm renders it); no change (the spec would not require a bound).
WATCHOUT: the edge-case bullet **A warm fill fails** is now a bare pointer to §6.4 **Repository stores.**; do not re-add a list of causes there, because SPEC-2 owns them and a copy goes stale. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edge cases list
OPEN: CODE-4 step 2.1 calls `--git-store-fill-timeout` "operator-tunable" while stating that the controller does not render it; a non-spec round should decide whether the wording or the rendering is wrong.

### [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: restore completion (§14 rule 6) fetches when the slot config carries `lenny.storeCommit` and the pod holds no store for `lenny.gitStoreUrl` or holds one at a different commit; rule 3 records `lenny.storeCommit` only when the repository keeps alternates and is not shallow; rule 6 rewrites or removes it after a fetch by rule 3's condition — BECAUSE stores are immutable and content-addressed, so same url at same store commit proves baseCommit's closure without a walk — ALTERNATIVES: presence check on baseCommit (never discriminates, see WATCHOUT); a closure walk (`rev-list --objects`, rejected non-goal); always fetch (double-counts slot objects against workspaceSizeLimitBytes); copying baseCommit's store-only closure at capture (whole repo in every checkpoint); recording storeCommit for shallow slots too (needless fetch, breaks TEST-1 shallow-fallback no-call case).
WATCHOUT: any presence test on `lenny.baseCommit` (`cat-file -e <baseCommit>^{commit}` or on its tree) as the restore-fetch predicate is wrong: a delta-built slot holds baseCommit's commit object in its own pack (pack = objects reachable from want not from haves), while its trees, blobs, and history stay store-only, and rule 5 deliberately excludes objects reachable from baseCommit. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 5 "but not from `lenny.baseCommit`"; SPEC-5 `FetchGitPack` row delta definition.

WATCHOUT: non-spec CODE-6 step 2.4 says "Index as CODE-5 step 6 states" and does not name the `lenny.storeCommit` rewrite; it relies on the CODE-6 heading's citation of §14 rule 6. A non-spec round may want the step to name it; this spec round did not author it (grant forbids). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 step 2.
FACT: CODE-4's in-memory url map holds `(destPath, resolved_commit)` per normalized url, so the adapter has the pod's store commit to compare against `lenny.storeCommit` with no new state. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 step 3.

DECISION: §14 rule 2 cites the §13.4 path-traversal, path-depth, path-length, and symlink rules instead of writing out their values, and a cache-served source honours the Runtime's `allowSymlinks` (default reject) — BECAUSE no spec text admits gitClone symlinks unconditionally, the owner prefers fail-closed, and admitting them only when a source is cache-served makes a symlink-bearing repository's outcome depend on whether the Runtime holds a store — ALTERNATIVES: delete only ", as for every gitClone source" (keeps an unjustified bypass of a security opt-in on the cache path only); stage §13.4/§7.4 edits making every gitClone admit symlinks to match the code (relaxes a security default, outside the problem's scope).
FACT: the cold gitClone path admits symlinks whatever allowSymlinks, in code only — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go:123-124 comment, :147 `upload.RuntimeAllow{AllowSymlinks: true, ...}`; spec/13_security-model.md:762 and spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:520 reject by default. Not filed in BUILD-GAPS (grep for gitClone+symlink finds only BUILD-GAPS.md:8352, unrelated).
WATCHOUT: an implementor of ValidateTree will copy extractGitCloneSource's `AllowSymlinks: true`; CODE-3 step 4 carries that trap. The memo key has to include allowSymlinks for the same reason the limit is not memoized.
DECISION: §6.2 **Client visibility:** points at the §15.1 `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` row instead of naming "archive validator violation", and the SPEC-6 row's cause clause names §14 rule 2 refusals instead of only the size check — BECAUSE the row is the one home for which violations map to the code, and a gitlink `non_regular_entry` refusal is neither a §13.4 validator nor a size check — ALTERNATIVES: listing "or a §14 cache-served refusal" in §6.2 (a second list of causes that drifts from the row).
FACT: finalize already maps every `*upload.ValidationError` to UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED, whatever produced it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/podclaimerror.go:76, :309; doc comment pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go:195-197 says "§13.4 archive validator violation" and goes stale with the §6.2 edit (CODE-5 owns podclaimerror.go, not finalize.go).

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: a `gitRepository` `gitRef` names a branch or tag, and registration rejects a 40-hex value (SPEC-2 parenthetical; CODE-2 Go check plus CEL rule; one TEST-1 item 12 case) — BECAUSE a store fill sends no haves, so a 40-hex want takes the SPEC-5 shallow branch, and a shallow store breaks rule 3 deltas; rejecting at registration fails closed at the API rather than as a pool whose every pod goes warming→failed — ALTERNATIVES: keying the SPEC-5 full-history branch on "a store-fill request" (needs a request-purpose field on FetchGitPack; Decision 16 already declines one); admitting a SHA store with a `shallow` file (rule 3 delta haves exclude ancestors the store never got); "branch or tag" with no registration check (the bad value still reaches warm fill and every pod fails).
FACT: the existing ref resolver matches only branch and tag names (refs/heads/X, peeled refs/tags/X^{}, refs/tags/X, exact ref), so a 40-hex ref resolves NOT_FOUND through ResolveRef anyway; plan-level resolution short-circuits SHAs with `workspaceplan.IsCommitSHA` before ls-remote — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/gitref.go:137-160 (pickSHA), pkg/workspaceplan/refresolve.go:86,212
WATCHOUT: §14 `gitClone.ref` reads "(branch, tag, or commit SHA)" and SPEC-2 copied that wording; the `gitRepository` divergence is deliberate, so do not "harmonize" it back — EVIDENCE: spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md:91
DECISION: the stalled-fill bound goes into the SPEC-2 failure sentence as "the adapter's fill deadline" with no value in spec; CODE-4 step 2.1 stays the value's home (15m) — BECAUSE §17.8.1 already uses "the specification states no value" for component tunables, and the owner directive keeps code tuning out of spec text — ALTERNATIVES: stating 15 minutes in spec (puts the code default in two homes); a §17.8.1 row (the flag is not rendered by the controller or Helm, so no deployer sets it).
OPEN: CODE-4 step 2.1 calls `--git-store-fill-timeout` "operator-tunable" while saying the controller does not render it, so no surface lets an operator set it; a later round should either drop "operator-tunable" or add a render path (and then a §17.8.1 row).

### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: FetchGitPack pack production leaves the Upload Handler gate and runs under a bare `subsystem.Limiter` (no Breaker, no Subsystem name) sized by a chart value beside `gateway.gitMirror.sizeLimit`; bind-time ValidateTree stays on the binder's UploadGate. SPEC-4 Edit (c) only loses its pack-production clause. BECAUSE a bare limiter adds no §4.1 subsystem, no §16.1 `subsystem` label, no GatewaySubsystemCircuitOpen case, and no §17.8 row, while still bounding pack-objects processes in the gateway pod. ALTERNATIVES: (a) a fifth §4.1 subsystem with its own breaker (reviewer's suggestion) cascades into §4.1's enumeration, the §16.1 closed label set, the alert, and §17.8, and a breaker on the pack path would let one unreachable remote fail every url's fills on the replica, recreating the coupling across urls; (b) no bound at all lets concurrent pack-objects OOM the gateway container, which takes every subsystem down; (c) relying on the per-url single-flight lock as the bound depends on an ambiguous sentence (CODE-3 step 3, test 20) that another round may narrow to fetches only.
FACT: today the binder's gitClone network clone (`gitref.CloneArchive`) runs OUTSIDE the Upload Handler gate; only the archive decode is gated. Network fetches (Mirror.Ensure, ResolveRef) therefore follow precedent by staying ungated. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go:141-150
WATCHOUT: `gatedExtract` records `lenny_upload_extraction_aborted_total` on every failure, so routing ValidateTree through it contradicts SPEC-6 ("not counted in lenny_upload_extraction_aborted_total"). ValidateTree must call `UploadGate.Do` directly. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go:163-184
WATCHOUT: `subsystem.Subsystem.Do` records every fn error as a breaker failure. The code already has a non-§4.1 Subsystem (`token_service`) whose state is published under `lenny_gateway_subsystem_circuit_state`, outside §16.1's closed label set. Do not copy that precedent for the pack limiter. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:125-128; cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:416
OPEN: the `token_service` label published on `lenny_gateway_subsystem_circuit_state` sits outside the §16.1 enumeration {stream_proxy, upload_handler, mcp_fabric, llm_proxy}. This is a shipped-tree drift for a later round or BUILD-GAPS, unrelated to 0111. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:416; spec/16_observability.md:102

### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: restore completion keys its fetch on a new slot-local config key `lenny.storeCommit`, which §14 rule 3 records only when the repository keeps alternates and is not shallow, and which rule 6 rewrites (or removes) by the same condition after it fetches — BECAUSE a delta-built slot holds `baseCommit`'s commit object in its own pack while that commit's trees, blobs, and history live only in the build-time store, so any presence test on `baseCommit` (CODE-6's `cat-file -e <baseCommit>^{commit}`) skips the fetch exactly when the store changed; a store is immutable and content-addressed, so "same url, same store commit" is the one cheap predicate that proves the closure — ALTERNATIVES: always fetch on restore (double-counts objects already in the slot against workspaceSizeLimitBytes and re-fetches on every same-store restore); full closure walk of `baseCommit` (the rejected `rev-list --objects` non-goal); copy `baseCommit`'s store-only closure at capture (puts the whole repository in every checkpoint, rejected non-goal).
WATCHOUT: rule 6 must rewrite `lenny.storeCommit` after a fetch. Without that, a slot restored onto store S2 (delta against S2) and checkpointed again still names S1, and a later restore onto an S1 pod skips the fetch while objects shared only with S2 are missing. The TEST-1 rule-6 case "second restore onto the first store" is the discriminating assertion. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0111_.../spec-changes.md:117 (rule 5 copies nothing reachable from baseCommit)
WATCHOUT: the rule-6 connectivity check (`rev-list --objects ... --not <baseCommit>`) excludes baseCommit's closure by construction, so it cannot detect the different-store defect; fetching correctly is the only guard. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0111_.../non-spec-changes.md:140
FACT: `lenny.storeCommit`, like `lenny.gitStoreUrl` and `lenny.baseCommit`, lives in agent-writable slot config; tampering only breaks or enlarges the tampering session's own repository (fetches stay Runtime-authorized and size-bounded). Do not "harden" it with a new mechanism.
DECISION: Runtime-entry removal is stated as an accepted failure mode (a restore whose rule 6 must fetch for a url the Runtime no longer names fails the resume on PERMISSION_DENIED), and the Goal is qualified — BECAUSE widening §4.7.1 authorization to the bound session's stored plan needs a pod→session(s)→plan read in the handler, is ambiguous on multi-slot pods, exposes another tenant's plan urls through the accepted spoofable-podId gap, and lets the mirror hold repositories no Runtime names; owner preference is simple and fail-closed, and a failed resume yields no wrong tree — ALTERNATIVES: plan-based authorization (above); dropping the Runtime check for commit-SHA wants (gateway becomes an arbitrary public-git fetcher for any pod-cert holder).
WATCHOUT: under the storeCommit predicate, pods warmed before the entry was removed whose store is at the recorded commit still restore without a fetch; only a fetch is denied. Keep the line-21 edge-case wording conditional on "has to fetch".

### [spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: the one home for how the adapter handles an agent-written slot repository (rules 5 and 6) is the SPEC-4 Edit (a) §13.4 exception; rules 5 and 6 each carry one pointer to it, and CODE-6 gains one intro paragraph naming the single helper that implements it — BECAUSE both findings concern the same trust boundary and the §13.4 exception must name rules 5 and 6 anyway (they inflate agent-written objects, which the bare §13.4 sentence forbids) — ALTERNATIVES: a new §14 rule 7 (rule numbers are cited across the proposal and G4 rewrote rule 6; a forward-placed rule reads out of order); stating the check in rule 6 with rule 5 citing it (the reviewer's suggestion; splits the discipline from the §13.4 exception that has to exist regardless).
DECISION: config isolation is by construction (git runs with an adapter-owned scratch GIT_DIR holding copies of HEAD, packed-refs, refs/, logs/, index, shallow, worktrees/; GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY = the slot objects dir) — BECAUSE overriding command-running keys with -c is a denylist: -c cannot disable include.path and does not cover keys a later git adds, so it is not fail-closed — ALTERNATIVES: -c overrides (reviewer's suggestion; rejected as above); run git as the agent UID through the runtime supervisor (git is not guaranteed in a third-party runtime image and the supervisor is not on every pod); Landlock confinement (gVisor does not implement it, so it would be tier-dependent); rewriting the slot config before git runs (destroys the agent's user/remote config in the checkpoint).
DECISION: store protection is by write resolution (every file the adapter adds/removes in a slot repository is written via openat2 RESOLVE_BENEATH|RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS from the slot, index-pack writes into scratch and the helper renameat()s into objects/pack) — BECAUSE the first capture copy (and the second, for a non-Full runtime) runs while the agent is live, so a check-then-path-write has a TOCTOU window; fd-relative writes close it — ALTERNATIVES: store dirs 0555 after fill (defence in depth only; leaves other adapter-writable dirs reachable and does nothing for config exec); the reviewer's real-directory check alone (TOCTOU against a live agent).
WATCHOUT: a copied refs set without `shallow` makes rule 6's connectivity check walk past the shallow boundary and fail every shallow-fallback restore; TEST-1 item 11's "slot that holds baseCommit through the shallow fallback" row is the one that catches it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 step 2.5 and TEST-1 item 11.
WATCHOUT: reads through GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY still follow the live slot's agent-written objects/info/alternates during a capture copy; the reachable extra object stores are the pod store (public) and other concurrent slots, which share the agent UID and process namespace under acknowledgeProcessLevelIsolation, so no read escalates — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:34 (per-slot credential-read scope), spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:644.
FACT: the §5.2 scrub already implements symlink-proof resolution (openat2 RESOLVE_BENEATH|RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS, unlinkat) in package-private helpers; spec label is §5.2 **What the scrub reaches.** — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/resolve_linux.go:16-45; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:569-571.
FACT: /workspace/staging is adapter-private (0700) on the same /workspace emptyDir as the slots, so renameat from a scratch dir there into a slot works — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go:77-81.
FACT: the SPEC-2 immutability sentence needs no edit; it becomes enforceable once slot-repository writes resolve beneath the slot — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 Edit (b).
OPEN: the CODE-6 rule that "objects not present in the repository's own object directory" are copied is ambiguous when the slot's own info/alternates names the store (git presence checks consult alternates); CODE-6 does not say how ownness is tested. Not G5's finding; a mechanism lens should check it.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: A slot built from a delta pack holds lenny.baseCommit's commit object in its own object store (SPEC-5 delta = objects reachable from want and not from haves, so it includes want's commit), while baseCommit's trees and blobs live only in the store. Any "is baseCommit present" test by commit-object presence (non-spec CODE-6 step 3, `git cat-file -e <baseCommit>^{commit}`) therefore passes after a restore onto a pod whose store is at a different commit, and skips the fetch rule 6 exists for. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:118 ("When `lenny.baseCommit` is not reachable after that"), non-spec-changes.md:138.
FACT: FetchGitPack authorization is keyed on the pod's Runtime's current gitRepository urls (spec-changes.md:156); restore completion (rule 6) calls it unconditionally for any restored repo carrying lenny.gitStoreUrl, so removing the entry from the Runtime makes every checkpoint of a cache-served session unrestorable.
WATCHOUT: The spec has no warming watchdog for non-preConnect pools; the only bound on a stalled warm fill is the adapter flag --git-store-fill-timeout in non-spec CODE-4. Not filed (the mechanism exists in staging), but a spec edit removing "for any reason" would leave a hang unbounded. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:104.

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
FACT: On a preConnect pool the controller projects `warming → sdk_connecting` as soon as the pod is Running, before READY, and the warm-fill watchdog clock starts at pod.Status.StartTime, so every pre-READY adapter step (CODE-4's store fill included) counts against `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` (default 60s). — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:266-271 (DecideSDKWarm default case), pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go:113 and 180-192 (podRunningFor), spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:67 and 87-91

DECISION: SPEC-2 now says a failed fill "takes the pod to `failed` on its warm-fill path (§6.2)" and adds one sentence that on a preConnect pool the fill runs in `sdk_connecting`, so the §6.1 watchdog also bounds it and the deployer sizes `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` to cover it. Edge-cases bullet, summary decision 13, CODE-4 step 4, and TEST-1 item 18 follow. — BECAUSE the existing knob and existing failed edges already cover the case — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the fill out of sdk_connecting or re-anchoring the clock (needs a pod-to-controller signal; agent pods have no apiserver path); rejecting preConnect+gitRepository (removes an opt-in mode a sizable knob covers); capping the adapter fill timeout at the watchdog (adapter cannot see the pool value); a minimum-timeout validation (cross-object, arbitrary threshold).
WATCHOUT: Do not name `sdk_connecting → failed` (or both edges) in SPEC-2 or its citers. §6.2 owns the per-path edges; a second listing is a second home. Do not put a numeric watchdog size or a restatement of §6.1's "measured from pod start" clock into spec text. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:67,82-91
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 18 carries no `// spec:` annotation, matching its Tier 4/5 siblings (items 15-19), which rely on the blanket annotation rule at the head of TEST-1. The design's fallback "cite 6.4 and 6.2 as its siblings do" did not apply because the siblings cite nothing. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 Tier 5 list
OPEN: docs/reference/configuration.md's `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` row ("Maximum time for SDK warm connection establishment") does not say pre-READY adapter work (sharedAssets population, now store fills) counts against it. The design marked it separate-finding; a non-spec round decides whether 0111 owes a docs deliverable for it.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: On a preConnect pool the WarmPoolController enters sdk_connecting as soon as the pod is Running (PodNotReady counts), so every pre-READY adapter step, including shared-asset population and the store fill, runs inside the sdk_connecting watchdog measured from pod.Status.StartTime. FACT: Agent pods run with RestartPolicy Never, so a fatal adapter startup error makes the pod Failed and the controller projects failed from either warming or sdk_connecting; both are existing §6.2 warm-fill failure edges. ### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2's failure sentence stops naming `warming → failed` and says the pod takes `failed` on its warm-fill path (§6.2), plus one sentence: on a `preConnect` pool the fill runs in `sdk_connecting`, the §6.1 `sdk_connecting` watchdog also bounds it, and the deployer sets `sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds` to cover the fill — BECAUSE §6.2 already has a failed edge on both warm-fill paths (sdk_connecting → failed covers the adapter exiting), and §6.1 already measures the warm-fill watchdog from pod start, so no new mechanism is needed — ALTERNATIVES: re-anchoring the warm-fill watchdog at fill completion (the controller cannot see fill completion; agent pods have no apiserver path, so this needs a new pod-to-controller signal); rejecting `capabilities.preConnect: true` together with a `gitRepository` entry at registration (removes an opt-in latency mode when the existing knob covers the case); capping the adapter fill deadline at the watchdog (the adapter does not know the pool's watchdog); a size-based floor on sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds (cross-object validation with an arbitrary threshold).
FACT: on a preConnect pool the controller projects warming → sdk_connecting as soon as the pod is Running, and the watchdog clock is pod.Status.StartTime, so all pre-READY adapter work (the existing sharedAssets population and the store fill, which CODE-4 places after ConnectGateway and before RunPreReady) counts against sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:266-271, pkg/controller/sandbox/sdkwarm.go:113,180-192, cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413,516
FACT: preConnect is opt-in and false by default for every reference coding-agent runtime, and sdkConnectTimeoutSeconds has Minimum=0 and no maximum, so a deployer can size it — EVIDENCE: spec/26_reference-runtime-catalog.md:65-68; pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/sandboxwarmpool_types.go:52

### [spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: A shallow-pack restore of an alternates-backed repository whose refs, reflogs, or index reach an ancestor of `lenny.baseCommit` (amend or reset of the pinned commit) is an accepted fail-closed outcome; SPEC-3 rules 5 and 6, the SPEC-5 shallow-pack clause, and CODE-6 stay unchanged. Edits: spec-changes.md edge-case bullet "A checkpoint restores onto a pod whose store is at a different commit or absent." now says restore fetches `lenny.baseCommit` and names the amend/reset failure; summary.md Goals checkpoint bullet replaced by the integrity invariant (restores intact or fails the resume, never with a missing object) — BECAUSE rule 6's "references an object it cannot reach, fails the resume" already enforces this, and only the proposal's success claims ("fetches what is missing", "restores intact on any pod ... whatever store") were false — ALTERNATIVES: copying ancestor commit+tree closures at capture (no native git walk; grows checkpoints toward the rejected dissociate/repack Non-goal); deleting rule 5's baseCommit exclusion (copies full history into every checkpoint); marking every commit with a missing parent shallow at restore (masks real missing-parent corruption, defeats the connectivity check); expiring reflogs on restore (wrong tree).
WATCHOUT: Do not "fix" the amend/reset restore failure by grafting a shallow frontier or by widening the rule 5 copy; both were weighed and lost (see DECISION above). The bind-time-shallow slot does not hit this: amending a shallow-boundary commit yields a parentless commit and `reset HEAD~1` fails there. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 3 (shallow file names only resolvedCommitSha), rule 6 last sentence.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the amend/reset-after-shallow-restore gap is closed as an accepted fail-closed outcome. Rules 5 and 6 and the SPEC-5 pack clause stay unchanged. Rule 6's last sentence ("whose repository references an object it cannot reach, fails the resume") already gives the outcome. Two edits: the spec-changes edge case **A checkpoint restores onto a pod whose store is at a different commit or absent.** drops "fetches what is missing" and records the failure, and the summary Goal "restores intact on any pod ... whatever store that pod holds" becomes the integrity invariant (the repository restores or the resume fails, and it never restores with a missing object) — BECAUSE copying an ancestor's tree closure at capture goes toward the whole repository in every checkpoint (summary Non-goal "Dissociating or repacking"). A restore step that marks every commit with a missing parent as shallow would turn a genuinely missing session-commit parent into silently truncated history, which defeats the connectivity check. Git also has no native walk for "stop at ancestors of baseCommit", so tips at an ancestor (reset), boundary parents (amend), and index blobs from older trees would each need their own enumeration — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's primary fix (frontier copy plus frontier shallow marking), rejected as above; deleting rule 5's exclusion, rejected because it copies the whole history; a rule-6 spec sentence naming the amend case, rejected because it restates what rules 3, 5, and 6 plus SPEC-5 already give, and the owner directive keeps derived failure modes out of spec text.
FACT: amending the shallow-boundary commit in a shallow repository creates a parentless commit, and `git reset HEAD~1` there fails with "unknown revision". So a slot that was shallow from bind time cannot reach an ancestor of baseCommit, and the finding's sub-claim "a slot shallow from bind time has the same problem on every resume" is false. Only alternates-backed slots whose restore receives the shallow pack hit the failure — EVIDENCE: reproduced locally with git (`clone --depth 1`, `commit --amend`, `cat-file -p HEAD` shows no parent line).
FACT: an alternates-backed slot that ran `commit --amend` on baseCommit and is then restored with only the shallow pack (baseCommit plus its tree, `shallow`=baseCommit) fails `rev-list --objects --all --reflog --indexed-objects --not <baseCommit>` with "revision walk setup failed". The finding's main case is real — EVIDENCE: local reproduction; spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 5, rule 6, SPEC-5 row.
WATCHOUT: do not "fix" this by having restore add every commit whose parent is missing to `shallow`. That masks capture-copy defects as truncated history and makes the TEST-1 item 11 row "a missing object after completion (resume fails)" pass vacuously for missing commits. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 11.
WATCHOUT: in practice a shallow-pack restore needs a destination pod with no store for the url (a pod warmed before the Runtime gained the entry; a removed entry fails earlier on PERMISSION_DENIED) or a delta over the §4.7.1 bound (force-push, or a store far behind the pin). A forward-refreshed store makes baseCommit an ancestor of the store commit, so the delta is empty. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 row; SPEC-2 warm fill before READY.
CORRECTS [spec.2.review-security.1]: the FACT line saying the rule-5 "not reachable from baseCommit" exclusion is "narrow (only a force-pushed store plus a shallow fallback)" understates the case. Any amend, reset, or branch at an ancestor of baseCommit in an alternates-backed slot fails the resume when the restore receives the shallow pack. The outcome is fail-closed and now accepted in the edge-case list.
OPEN: whether TEST-1 item 11 should carry an explicit "amend of baseCommit, restored onto a pod with no store, fails the resume" row. The existing "missing object after completion (resume fails)" row pins the same rule-6 predicate, so this design stages none. The non-spec lane may add it under the owner's expansive-tests preference.

### [spec.4.review-performance.1]
FACT: The staged spec adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write: no CRD field, no status write, and no store key. The only new per-unit work is gateway-local (mirror fetch, tree walk, pack-objects) plus the adapter's local git, so Postgres failover, Redis reset, and coordinator handoff have no new state to lose. A replica switch on FetchGitPack only costs a cold memo or mirror, because packs are content-addressed. EVIDENCE: proposals/0111*/0111*.spec-changes.md SPEC-5 row; non-spec CODE-3 step 3 "The mirror writes no durable state"
FACT: The sdk_connecting watchdog clock on the warm-fill edge measures from pod start, so it bounds a preConnect warm fill whichever phase the fill projects in. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:67 "On the warm-fill edge (`warming → sdk_connecting`) the clock measures from pod start"
FACT: §4.1 names four gateway subsystems. Pack production sits in none of them, and its concurrency limit appears only in non-spec CODE-3 step 7 and Decision 18. Existing GatewayControl RPCs also sit outside the four, so the spec is silent on this rather than contradicted. I did not file it. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:100
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-3 step 3 says "Each operation on a url holds that url's single-flight lock". Read literally, every Pack stream (which can run to multiple GB), Ensure, and ValidateTree on one url is serialized per replica. That makes the per-url lock a hot-key bottleneck at Tier 3 and leaves `maxConcurrentPacks` (step 7) without effect for a single-repository pool. What should be true: the single-flight lock covers only mirror mutation (fetch, rebuild). Pack and ValidateTree run as concurrent readers, and the rebuild path removes the directory only when no reader holds it, or else readers retry once. This fix lands in code staging, so it is out of scope for the spec loop.

### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
FACT: On a preConnect pool the controller moves a Running pod from warming to sdk_connecting before the adapter is ready, so a fill placed after ConnectGateway runs in sdk_connecting, and the warm-fill watchdog clock runs from pod start. The SPEC-2 preConnect sentence is accurate. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:255-271 (DecideSDKWarm, PodNotReady/PodReady -> SDKConnecting); spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:67
FACT: Agent pods run with RestartPolicy Never, so the adapter exiting on a fatal fill error turns the pod Failed, and both warming->failed and sdk_connecting->failed follow. Nothing retries the fill in place. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:47; lifecycle.go:259-260,287-290
WATCHOUT: Rule 5 does not copy objects reachable from lenny.baseCommit. Rule 6 relies on its baseCommit fetch to bring them back, but SPEC-5 can answer that fetch with a shallow pack when there is no have or the delta is larger than the tree. A session whose refs or reflogs reference history before baseCommit (rebase -i, a revert of an ancestor's blob, a branch at an ancestor) therefore fails its resume on a shallow restore. The staged rule 6 text already fails the resume, so the outcome is fail-closed. Edge-case lines 24 and 26 in spec-changes.md (the revert example, and "fetches what is missing") overstate what the mechanism covers. I did not file this, because the refuted "restore depends on remote serving baseCommit" item shows the skeptic accepts rule 6's fail-the-resume sentence as sufficient. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:24,26,129-130,170; summary.md:77

### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no spec-scope reliability findings in round 6 — BECAUSE the round-5 diff is a vocabulary sweep plus the §2 pointer and the shallow-restore edge case; I re-traced rule 3 (build, no-have retry), rule 5 (two copies), rule 6 (storeCommit predicate, same/different/absent store, shallow fallback) and SPEC-2 fill failure under crash/restart and found every failure either atomic (build tree promotion), retried once by rule, or fail-closed through rule 6's unreachable-object clause — ALTERNATIVES: filing the line-24 "revert" edge case (see WATCHOUT), filing cross-Runtime derive/replay (see OPEN).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-3 step 3 "Capacity: when a fetch fails for lack of space, the replica deletes that url's mirror directory ... and refetches once" and test 16 "a mirror volume at `sizeLimit` during `Ensure` rebuilds once and then fails with `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`" assume ENOSPC at the emptyDir sizeLimit. A disk-backed emptyDir with sizeLimit does not return ENOSPC; the kubelet evicts the pod once usage exceeds the limit (spec/04_system-components.md:273 states this for the workspace emptyDir). As staged (CODE-3 step 9, `git-mirror` emptyDir, sizeLimit 20Gi), mirror growth evicts a whole gateway replica, dropping every session it coordinates. True instead: the mirror must self-enforce a byte budget below sizeLimit (measure before fetch, rebuild/evict urls), or the volume must not carry a sizeLimit-eviction cap. Owner: non-spec loop.
WATCHOUT: spec-changes.md:24 edge case says the capture copy moves a reverted-to store-only blob into the slot; rule 5 excludes everything reachable from lenny.baseCommit, including its ancestry, so a revert of a pre-pin change is NOT copied and fails a shallow restore via rule 6. Outcome is fail-closed and the class is accepted (review-log DECISIONs at lines ~283/303); only the example is wrong. Earlier reviewer (line ~371) also declined to file. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:24,129
OPEN: rule 6 applies to any restore of a captured tree, so `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/derive` and `replay` with a `targetRuntime` that does not name the url fail on FetchGitPack PERMISSION_DENIED when the slot carries lenny.storeCommit (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:771 promises the replayed session "starts with the source workspace"). Fail-closed, analogous to the accepted Runtime-loses-entry edge case; not filed. A later round or the owner may want an edge-case clause. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:21,130; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:87
USEFUL [review-log lines 283-308]: the accepted shallow-restore DECISION settled the ancestry-exclusion family and kept me from re-filing it.

### [spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: §14 rule 3 removes only a pre-existing `.git` entry at the source's `path` (no symlink follow) before `git init`; §13.4 exception stays scoped to rules 5 and 6 — BECAUSE a fresh adapter-created repository needs no isolation widening and keeps the file-granular Path collision rule true — ALTERNATIVES: remove the whole `path` subtree (deletes non-colliding files from earlier sources and the 0109 restore seed, contradicts spec/14 Path collision rule); widen §13.4 to rule 3 (still merges untrusted refs, index, `shallow`, objects into the slot); fail when `path/.git` exists (every 0109 derive of a cache-served session would fail).
WATCHOUT: once rule 3 removes `.git`, every seeded or earlier-source file under `path` is untracked, so a non-forced `git checkout --detach` refuses and fails every 0109 derive of a cache-served session. CODE-5 step 6 now uses `--force`; do not drop it as unrelated — EVIDENCE: proposals/0109_*/0109_*.spec-changes.md:74 (restore seed extracted into build tree before sources); spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md:338.
DECISION: the §14 `gitClone` `depth` default is qualified in place with a pointer ("default full clone except as **Cache-served `gitClone` sources.** rule 3 states", folded into SPEC-3 Edit (a)), and the schema's "Omit for a full clone." is deleted through a CODE-5 target — BECAUSE the cell that states the default is where a client reads it, and the schema is a second statement of the same promise — ALTERNATIVES: appending a shallow-outcome sentence to the Purpose cell (leaves the default unqualified and restates rule 3); full-history fallback (summary Decision 8 rejects it).

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §14 rule 1 eligibility and §4.7.1 FetchGitPack authorization both name "the Runtime of the pod's pool" (rule 1 replace, SPEC-5 row replace; CODE-5 step 1 sources the url set from the matched SandboxTemplate's runtimeRef) — BECAUSE FetchGitPack is pod-scoped and is called at warm fill with no session, so the pool's Runtime is the only Runtime both predicates can share; the pod's stores are filled from that Runtime's list — ALTERNATIVES: authorizing FetchGitPack against the session's Runtime (no session exists at warm fill, and podId cannot name it); adding a session id to the request (new wire field for no gain).

### [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: §14 rule 3 removes any `.git` entry the build tree already holds at the source's `path` (without following a symlink) before `git init`; CODE-5 step 6 checks out with `--force`; TEST-1 item 8 gains one pre-existing-`.git` case — BECAUSE a fresh adapter-built repository keeps rule 3 outside the §13.4 untrusted-repository exception (which stays scoped to rules 5 and 6) and keeps §14 **Path collision rule** file-granular — ALTERNATIVES: removing the whole subtree at `path` (reviewer option 1; drops non-colliding files from earlier sources and the 0109 restore seed, contradicting spec/14 Path collision rule's same-path overwrite); widening the §13.4 exception to "rules 3, 5, and 6" (leaves rule 3 merging an untrusted repository's refs, index, `shallow`, and objects into the slot, undefined and not fail-closed); failing materialization when `path/.git` exists (every derive of a cache-served session under 0109 would fail).
WATCHOUT: once `path/.git` is removed, every file an earlier source or the 0109 restore seed left under `path` is untracked, and `git checkout --detach <sha>` on a fresh repository refuses to overwrite untracked same-path files. Without `--force` a derived session of a cache-served session fails on checkout every time. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 6; proposals/0109_*/0109_*spec-changes.md FinalizeWorkspace row ("extract the starting snapshot's workspace tree into the build tree first ... Then materialize the plan's sources over it").
FACT: uploads may write `.git/...` paths: nothing in §13.4/§7.4 or pkg/upload, pkg/adapter/workspace rejects a `.git` component, and the cold gitClone archive carries `.git` (pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/archive.go:22). So an earlier source can plant `path/.git/config` before rule 3 runs. EVIDENCE: grep of spec/13, spec/07, pkg/upload, pkg/adapter/workspace for ".git".
DECISION: the `depth` default contradiction is closed at the claim site: SPEC-3 Edit (a) also replaces "default full clone" in the §14 `gitClone` row with "default full clone except as **Cache-served `gitClone` sources.** rule 3 states", and CODE-5 deletes "Omit for a full clone." from schemas/workspaceplan-v1.json `depth` description — BECAUSE a pointer at the default is one statement, and the schema then defers to §14 (its top-level description already says "See spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md") — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's clause appended to the Purpose cell (leaves "default full clone" unqualified and restates rule 3's outcome); a full-history fallback for depth-absent sources (summary Decision 8 rejects it); leaving the schema as a separate finding (it is the same client-visible promise, so the finding would return).
FACT: "Omit for a full clone." exists only in schemas/workspaceplan-v1.json:124; no openapi or Go copy carries it. EVIDENCE: repo-wide grep.

### [spec.4.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no spec-scope performance findings in this round — BECAUSE the staged spec adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write, and every top-tier cost it creates (a full-history pack per warm pod, a per-session tree walk, and restore-time packs) is gateway-local. The spec mandates none of the expensive parts per call: pack caching, bitmaps, the pack limit, and memoization are all left to code. The spec also states no gateway bandwidth budget that these costs would exceed. — ALTERNATIVES: rejected filing "on every call" ref resolution as a hot key. It is a close variant of the refuted git-host-outage finding, and it costs one ls-remote rather than a pack. Rejected filing the absence of a sizeLimit for the /workspace/shared store against the §17.8.2 ephemeral-storage quota, because no spec sentence ties a pod's ephemeral-storage request to its slots, so nothing it says is falsified.
FACT: Restore under rule 6 never adds more bytes than the shipped full-clone .git would. Shipped slot = checkout + full history + session growth. New slot after restore = checkout + session growth + a pack of at most the tree's blob and tree bytes (the §4.7.1 bound). The rule 6 size-limit failure is therefore no less reliable than shipped. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:126,130,176
USEFUL [spec.4.review-performance.1 and spec.6.review-performance.1 in the review log]: their FACTs on the absence of store writes and on the per-replica pack limit staying non-spec saved me re-deriving the failover traces.

### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]

The spec therefore needs no per-RPC deadline for those calls. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:255 (finalizing row of the timer table)
FACT: An adapter that exits retires a warming, sdk_connecting, or idle pod, so a fill that fails as a fatal startup error reaches `failed` through an existing edge on both warm paths. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1192-1205 (Runtime supervisor **Exit**)
DECISION: Filed no reliability findings for round 4. BECAUSE: rule 6's fetch condition and its shallow fallback hold under restore onto a pod with no store, a pod whose store matches, and a pod whose store differs. The cases where restore fails (ancestor refs under a shallow pack, a race on a runtime that is not paused, a denied url) are each accepted in the Edge cases list, and the restore connectivity check catches them. FetchGitPack writes nothing durable, so it needs no fencing or dedup. ALTERNATIVES: I considered and rejected three. Gateway drain dropping in-flight fills: the pod is replaced, and the gateway clone path already has the same exposure. A transient index failure taking the no-have fallback: the outcome is degraded but still correct. Restore depending on a reachable remote during an outage: this is a close variant of the refuted remote-deletion item.

I did not file it. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:137 (GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY is the slot's objects dir, so its info/alternates is read)

### [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: rule 3 builds the slot repository's git directory with `git init` in a fresh directory outside the build tree, runs every rule 3 git operation with that GIT_DIR and the source `path` as work tree, and moves it to `<path>/.git` after checkout, replacing any entry there — BECAUSE git then never opens repository state a plan source wrote, through any symlink at `path` or a parent component, and no new failure conflicts with the §14 last-writer-wins Path collision rule — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's suggested predicate (remove a symlink at `path`, fail on a symlinked parent) narrows round 4's cleanup one step and adds a failure; widening the SPEC-4 §13.4 exception to rule 3 copies refs/index/HEAD of a repository that is being created fresh; `-c` overrides are an open-ended denylist.
WATCHOUT: do not re-add a "remove the `.git` already at `path`" sentence beside the GIT_DIR ownership sentence; the removal is reachable around through an in-tree symlink an earlier source planted (symlink source under allowSymlinks, or the cold gitClone path's unconditional AllowSymlinks). Do not pass a work tree to `git init` either: it records an absolute `core.worktree` that points into the build directory after the move. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit (c) rule 3; non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 6; pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go writeSymlink (AllowSymlinks gate)
FACT: prototyped locally: `GIT_DIR=<tmp> git init; git config core.bare false; fetch; GIT_DIR=<tmp> GIT_WORK_TREE=build/src git checkout --force --detach <sha>` with `build/src -> evil` (evil/.git carrying a post-checkout hook and core.fsmonitor marker, plus an earlier same-path file), then `rm -rf build/src/.git; mv <tmp> build/src/.git`: no marker fired, the file held the pinned content, `git status` was clean, and no `core.worktree` was recorded. — EVIDENCE: shell prototype, this round
CORRECTS [standing context "Rule 3 `.git` removal" (spec.4.fix-G2.1 second round)]: rule 3 no longer removes a pre-existing `.git` before `git init`; it creates the git directory outside the build tree and moves it into `<path>/.git` after checkout. The §13.4 exception still stays scoped to rules 5 and 6, CODE-5 keeps `--force`, and TEST-1 item 8 now also covers a symlinked `path` and a symlinked parent component.
CORRECTS [standing trap "Checkout needs `--force`"]: the reason now reads "seeded or earlier-source files under `path` are untracked relative to the fresh adapter-created git directory"; the `--force` requirement is unchanged.

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §14 rule 3 builds the slot repository's git directory with `git init` in a new directory outside the build tree, runs every rule 3 git operation with that git directory and the source's `path` as the working tree, then moves it to `.git` at `path`, replacing any entry there. The "removes any `.git` entry ... without following a symlink" clause is deleted. BECAUSE git then never opens git state that a plan source wrote, whatever symlinks the build tree holds, and this is the CODE-6 bullet (2) per-operation-git-dir pattern applied to construction. It adds no new failure mode. ALTERNATIVES: the finding's own fix (remove a symlink at `path`, fail on a symlinked parent) narrows round 4's cleanup predicate the same way round 4 did, adds a failure that conflicts with §14 last-writer-wins, and still lets git run over build-tree state. Extending the §13.4 exception to rule 3 does not fit: its scratch copy of slot refs and index is the wrong tool for a fresh repository, and the build tree is not the slot. `-c` overrides of hook and fsmonitor keys are an open-ended list, and git still reads repo config (filters, includes).
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-G2.1 (second round)] / Standing context "Rule 3 `.git` removal": removing the `.git` without following a symlink does not stop the attack, because a symlink at `path` or at a parent component reaches a planted repository that `git init` reinitializes and keeps (config and hooks survive). The settled line should instead record the external-git-dir construction.
WATCHOUT: `git --git-dir=X --work-tree=Y init` writes an absolute `core.worktree = Y` into config, and `GIT_DIR=X git init` alone sets `core.bare = true`. Construction must run `GIT_DIR=X git init` followed by `git config core.bare false`, or the moved repository points into the build directory after promotion. EVIDENCE: reproduced locally in /tmp (git 2.x on AL2023).
FACT: a local prototype ran external GIT_DIR init, fetch, `GIT_WORK_TREE=<symlink to dir holding a planted .git with post-checkout hook and core.fsmonitor>` checkout --force, `rm -rf path/.git`, and mv. The result left no marker, the pinned content won, status was clean, and there was no core.worktree. EVIDENCE: prototype only; nothing in tree.
FACT: the adapter build tree is `filepath.Dir(root)/staging` (mode 0755). For a root of `/workspace/current` it collides with upload staging and falls back to `current.build`, so `/workspace/staging/` (CODE-6 bullet (2)) is never inside the build tree. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go promotionBuildDir, promotionStagingName, promotionStagingMode.
FACT: every existing source writer resolves `path` with resolvePath plus os.MkdirAll, which follow in-tree symlinks, so writing through an earlier source's symlink is the existing posture for all sources. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go writeInlineFile, writeUploadFile, writeSymlink, resolvePath.

### [spec.7.review-security.1]
- FACT: The round-6 rule 3 rewrite (git dir created outside the build tree, work tree = source `path`, git dir moved to `<path>/.git` last) removes the agent-config/hook exposure the earlier `.git`-removal design had. A symlink an earlier source plants at `path` or a parent can only redirect the checkout inside the session, because §13.4 requires every allowed symlink target to canonicalize inside `/workspace/slots/{sessionId}/current` and forbids `/workspace/shared`. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:762, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:520, spec-changes.md:127
- FACT: The promotion build tree is the sibling `/workspace/staging` (pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go:106-124), and CODE-5 step 6 puts the rule 3 git dir in a fresh 0700 directory there. So "a new directory outside the build tree" in SPEC-3 rule 3 does not give the agent a path into the git dir. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 6
- DECISION: I filed no security finding in round 7 — BECAUSE every bound the lens checks has a trusted authority: the gateway checks `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` (rule 2), the trusted adapter enforces the restore limit, and FetchGitPack authorization was already refuted as accepted — ALTERNATIVES: I rejected a haves-amplification DoS on FetchGitPack (unbounded `haves`, one remote fetch each) as hardening that is below the bar.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
FACT: `git ls-tree -r -t -l --full-tree <commit>` prints `-` for tree sizes and never lists the root tree; `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <commit>` lists the commit, root tree, subtrees, and blobs. A pack built from that listing indexes under `index-pack --strict` in an empty repo whose `.git/shallow` names the commit (git 2.50.1). — EVIDENCE: local reproduction in /tmp/g1t
WATCHOUT: `git rev-list --objects` prints `<oid> <path>`; piping it to `git cat-file --batch-check='%(objectsize)'` reports every named object `missing`, because cat-file splits the input line only when the format contains `%(rest)`. CODE-3 step 4 now uses `'%(objecttype) %(objectsize) %(rest)'`. — EVIDENCE: local reproduction; git-cat-file(1) `%(rest)`
OPEN: CODE-3 step 5's delta clause ("`git rev-list --objects <want> --not <haves>` piped to `git cat-file --batch-check='%(objectsize)'`") has the same defect: named objects come back `missing`. Fix by adding `%(rest)` or `--no-object-names`. Not in G1's findings or design, so left for the mirror group or a later round.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: per-url mirror lock is shared for Ensure, ResolveRef, ValidateTree, and Pack; fetches for one url are serialized and identical ones coalesce; only capacity deletion takes the lock exclusively, via TryLock (CODE-3 step 3) — BECAUSE git fetch adds packs by atomic rename beside readers, so only directory deletion needs readers excluded, and a non-waiting writer cannot trigger sync.RWMutex writer preference — ALTERNATIVES: see WATCHOUTs below.
WATCHOUT: do not make fetches (Ensure, ResolveRef) take the url lock exclusively. Under Go's RWMutex writer preference a waiting Ensure for a new push queues behind a stalled Pack stream, and every later Pack and ValidateTree on that url queues behind it, which brings back the serialization. A blocking Lock in capacity deletion has the same defect and can also deadlock against Pack's nested have-Ensures. A reader-retry or reader-drain protocol was also rejected: it adds reader code and still fails in-flight streams. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 3; summary Decision 5 "single-flight per url" stays true because it is about fetches.
CORRECTS [Deferred D7]: closed. CODE-3 step 3 now uses the shared-lock and TryLock-deletion discipline, and TEST-1 item 20 asserts that Packs overlap, that a stalled Pack consumer delays neither a ValidateTree nor an Ensure, and that deletion skips the busy url.
DECISION: the git-mirror emptyDir carries no sizeLimit, and the gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` (default 20Gi) by LRU deletion of url mirrors it can TryLock, before each fetch. RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED means the mirror is over budget with nothing deletable left — BECAUSE a disk-backed emptyDir sizeLimit is enforced by eviction (spec/04_system-components.md §4.4.4 **Hard workspace size limit.** "if disk usage under the emptyDir volume exceeds the limit, the pod is evicted"), and /workspace/shared follows the same no-sizeLimit precedent — ALTERNATIVES: a sizeLimit backstop with maxBytes at 80% (two knobs, Helm cannot do quantity arithmetic, and an overshoot still evicts the replica); rebuilding the requested url (a fetch by SHA brings full history, so the rebuilt mirror is rarely smaller); deleting every idle url (causes a refetch storm).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce an emptyDir sizeLimit "as a backstop" or any ENOSPC or "rebuild once" branch on the gateway mirror. The kubelet evicts the replica, which drops every session stream it holds, and the ENOSPC branch cannot be reached. TEST-1 item 22 pins that the sizeLimit is absent. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.4.4; non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 steps 3 and 9.
CORRECTS [Deferred D8]: closed in CODE-3 steps 3, 6, and 9, in TEST-1 items 16 and 22, and in summary Decision 5. Its "operator-docs narrative" half is not closed here: a DOCS-1 row for the gateway.gitMirror values is a separate finding.
FACT: the extraction-abort metric is recorded only through Binder.gatedExtract → recordExtractionAbort → Binder.ExtractionAbort, so only a binder-level assertion modelled on TestBindUploadArchiveAbortRecordsMetric discriminates against a violation. podclaimerror never touches the metric. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go recordExtractionAbort; binder.go ExtractionAbort field; binder_archive_test.go TestBindUploadArchiveAbortRecordsMetric
WATCHOUT: under §14 rule 3 one truncated delta pack succeeds, because the adapter retries with no have. TEST-1 item 16's truncation case must truncate every call, and the retry rows sit in item 6. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 3 "requests the pack again with no have".
WATCHOUT: the malformed-object row (TEST-1 item 16) uses a `.git` tree entry because rule 2 does not refuse it. hasDotgit is WARN-level and becomes fatal only under the strict fsck that fetch.fsckObjects enables, so the row fails when the fsck config is dropped. Asserting "no ref" would pass vacuously, because a fetch by SHA creates no ref; assert that the commit object is absent instead.
DECISION: the chart test is appended as item 22 under a trailing "Tier 1 (Helm unittest):" heading, after item 21 — BECAUSE renumbering items 12 to 21 would break the item-number anchors of other groups — ALTERNATIVES: inserting it after item 11 and renumbering (rejected).

WATCHOUT: CODE-6 bullet (2)'s mode-0700 git directory is per-operation scratch that is deleted afterwards, so 0700 is correct there. Do not "harmonize" it with CODE-5 step 6, and do not cite bullet (2) as the precedent for the promoted `.git` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 (2); pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go promotionStagingMode comment.
FACT: `/workspace/staging` is created 0700 (pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go warmStagingMode), so the parent keeps the agent out while the slot `.git` is built. `git init` on an existing directory keeps that directory's mode. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go:77-81,120.

FACT: TEST-1 item 6 now covers all four adapter-guard conjuncts (non-40-hex sha, auth, depth, submodules: true) plus the nil-fetcher fail-closed arm. Item 10 asserts that either copy failing fails the checkpoint, and that a late commit lands in the slot's own pack (the discriminating case for the second copy).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: `git ls-tree -r -t -l --full-tree <sha>` omits the root tree and prints `-` as every tree's size. `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <sha>` lists the commit, the root tree, and every distinct tree and blob, and piping it to `git cat-file --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectsize)'` gives every size. A pack built from that listing indexes under `index-pack --strict` into a bare repository whose `shallow` file names the commit. Reproduced locally on 2026-10-30. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 steps 4-5
DECISION: ValidateTree keeps ls-tree for per-entry path, mode, and blob size (rev-list drops modes and lists a blob once even when it appears at several paths), and adds the rev-list|cat-file object listing for tree-object sizes and the memo's summed bytes. Pack step 5 pipes that same listing into pack-objects. — BECAUSE one listing feeds both the bound's tree term and the shallow pack — ALTERNATIVES: rev-list alone (loses modes, so the gitlink, non-regular, and symlink checks cannot run, and it undercounts duplicated blob paths); ls-tree plus `<sha>^{tree}` plus cat-file on tree oids (three commands, and the shallow pack still needs its own listing)
FACT: no code applies the §4.4 512Mi default for workspaceSizeLimitBytes. resolve.go leaves an unset template value at 0, the controller renders no `--workspace-size-limit-bytes`, nothing gives the slot volume a sizeLimit, and both `checkpoint.WorkspaceSizePreCheck` (the checkpoint probe and the §7.3 Resume pre-extraction check) and the proto comment on `workspace_size_limit_bytes` treat 0 as disabled. No 512Mi constant exists in pkg/ or cmd/. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:158-163,352-353; pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:458-466; pkg/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:287-290; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1434-1440
DECISION: a zero limit becomes the §4.4 default (512Mi) only at the proposal's two new consumers, the binder's ValidateTree call and restore completion. The rule lives in CODE-5 step 1, and CODE-6 step 2.4 points at it. — BECAUSE applying the default in resolve.go's match also changes the existing §7.3 Resume pre-extraction check, the match's only other consumer (resume_rebind.go:264). With the checkpoint probe still disabled on unset pools, an ordinary session that checkpointed above 512Mi would then be unable to resume. — ALTERNATIVES: default in resolve.go's match (regresses non-cache-served resumes); implement the default across the tree in resolve.go, the controller flag, and the slot sizeLimit (the coherent fix, but a pre-existing defect outside this proposal's scope, so it is filed in the summary's defects list)
WATCHOUT: 0109 deliberately omits `workspace_size_limit_bytes` from FinalizeWorkspaceRequest, so restore completion called at 0109's restore seed (CODE-6 step 3) has no limit forwarded by the gateway. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0109_*/...summary.md:62
OPEN: on 0109's FinalizeWorkspace path, where does restore completion's rule 6 size limit come from? This is a separate finding against CODE-6 step 3 and is not fixed by G1.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: one per-url lock design closes the lock-scope and capacity findings together. Every mirror operation (Ensure, ResolveRef, ValidateTree, Pack) holds the url's lock shared. Fetches for one url run one at a time, and identical ones share one fetch. Only capacity deletion takes the lock exclusively, and only with a try-lock that skips a busy url. The ENOSPC "rebuild once" path is deleted outright — BECAUSE git fetch adds packs by atomic rename beside running readers, so deletion is the only mutation that needs readers excluded. A try-lock deletion never queues a writer, so a stalled Pack blocks nothing — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's RW lock with fetches exclusive was rejected because Go's sync.RWMutex blocks new readers once a writer waits. A stalled Pack would then block an Ensure for a newly pushed commit, which would block every later Pack and ValidateTree on that url, recreating the serialization. "Readers retry once after a rebuild" (D7's second option) was rejected because it adds a retry path to every reader.
DECISION: the git-mirror emptyDir carries no sizeLimit. The gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` (default 20Gi) before each fetch by deleting least-recently-used idle url mirrors, and fails the fetch with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED when none is left to delete — BECAUSE a disk-backed emptyDir sizeLimit evicts the replica instead of returning ENOSPC (spec/04_system-components.md, the workspaceSizeLimitBytes sentence "the pod is evicted"). The `/workspace/shared` store volume already runs with no sizeLimit (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/volumes.go), and the gateway container has no ephemeral-storage limit, so node storage bounds the overshoot — ALTERNATIVES: keeping sizeLimit as a backstop with maxBytes below it was rejected because it needs two knobs, a consistency rule Helm cannot compare across quantities, and it still evicts whenever an in-flight fetch overshoots. A gateway-side fixed fraction of sizeLimit was rejected as a hard-coded non-spec constant (code-best-practices.md, Configuration). Rebuilding the requested url when it alone is over budget was dropped, because a SHA fetch brings full history, so a rebuild rarely shrinks the mirror.
FACT: chart helm-unittest suites under charts/lenny/tests run at tier 1 through tests/tier1_unit/helm/helm_test.go (`helm unittest`), so a gateway-deployment_test.yaml row sits inside S11's tier set. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier1_unit/helm/helm_test.go:58
FACT: the extraction-abort metric is recorded only in Binder.gatedExtract → recordExtractionAbort → Binder.ExtractionAbort hook; podclaimerror never touches it. The binder precedent is TestBindUploadArchiveAbortRecordsMetric. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go:163-199; binder_archive_test.go:185
FACT: gitref tests drive git against local path repositories rather than an HTTP fixture, and the reused fetch form sets GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0, so an auth-requiring fixture fails rather than hangs. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/clone_test.go:25-64; clone.go:75
WATCHOUT: for the malformed-object test, `hasDotgit` (a tree entry named `.git`) is a WARN-level fsck message. It becomes fatal only because fetch.fsckObjects makes fetch-pack run index-pack with `--strict`. If the row ever passes without fsck enabled, switch to a tree with duplicate entries, which is ERROR-level under any strictness. Rule 2 does not refuse `.git` entries ("Uploads may write .git"), so fsck is the gateway-side guard here. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 3
WATCHOUT: under the try-lock deletion design a recursive RLock (Pack calling the public Ensure for unknown haves) cannot deadlock, because no writer ever waits. A fixer who changes deletion to a blocking Lock reintroduces both the deadlock and the stall propagation. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 5 "Unknown haves get one `Ensure` each"
OPEN: CODE-3 step 3's Ensure reuses `git fetch -- <url> <sha>`, which writes only FETCH_HEAD and creates no ref. Git's automatic gc after a fetch would treat every mirror object as unreachable: repack -A explodes them loose and later prunes them. A later round should decide between `gc.auto=0` on the mirror and a ref per ensured commit. Not filed by this group.
OPEN: DOCS-1 stages no operator-facing row for the `gateway.gitMirror.*` chart values (maxBytes, maxConcurrentPacks) or for the RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED mirror failure. That gap predates this design (D8's docs half), so it is a separate finding.
CORRECTS [Deferred D7]: closed by this design. The url lock is held shared by every operation, and only try-lock capacity deletion takes it exclusively, so Pack and ValidateTree overlap, and maxConcurrentPacks is the effective bound on a hot url.
CORRECTS [Deferred D8]: closed by this design. The volume carries no sizeLimit, the gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` with LRU deletion of idle url mirrors, and the handler's RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED row reads against that budget.

FACT: cmd/runtimes/{preconnect-echo,echo-embedded} decode the flag with sharedassets.Decode and materialize through the same package, so the Materialize skip covers them. — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:138; cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:116
DECISION: CRD-path url uniqueness goes in CODE-2 step 3 as one sentence. `mirror` applies the step 2 check to the CRD's list and returns a permanentError before mirroring, on the requireSoPeercred precedent. — BECAUSE the reconciler already has a pre-mirror permanent-rejection slot, and CEL cannot normalize. — ALTERNATIVES: a validating admission webhook for Runtime CRDs (none exists in the chart, and it is new surface); approximating normalization in CEL with lowerAscii and trimming (it duplicates the §6.4 rule outside its one Go implementation); dropping the uniqueness rule (it leaves CODE-4's url-keyed map ambiguous).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: the warm fill runs inside `Server.ConnectGateway` (pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go), which calls an unexported fill in gitstore.go after the GatewayControl dial; its empty-gatewayAddr branch returns an error when the shared-asset set has a gitRepository entry. The deadline is a new `Server.GitStoreFillTimeout` field (zero means the 15m default), set by cmd/lenny-adapter from `--git-store-fill-timeout`. The embedded runtimes leave it at the default and need no source edit. — BECAUSE all three entry points (lenny-adapter, echo-embedded, preconnect-echo) already call ConnectGateway and log.Fatalf on its error, so the fill reaches every pod with no per-binary call site. — ALTERNATIVES: an exported fill method that each binary calls (rejected: a binary that omits the call reproduces this exact defect); embedded binaries treating a gitRepository entry as a fatal startup error (rejected: it contradicts SPEC-2's embedded-mode sentence, which is spec-loop text, and it would let TEST-1 item 18's preConnect row pass on a rejection instead of a fill failure).
DECISION: CODE-1's git image set grows to lenny-gateway, lenny-adapter, runtimes/echo-embedded, and runtimes/preconnect-echo. TEST-1 item 18 gains an echo-embedded success row as the discriminator, because a failure row also passes when the image has no git.
DECISION: the volume-exhaustion test is a TEST-1 item 7 (tier 1) row. ENOSPC is injected through the git-runner and filesystem seams, and the row asserts nothing exists at destPath. A tier-4 tmpfs or loop device needs root, and at tier 5 ephemeral exhaustion evicts the pod instead of returning ENOSPC.
FACT: sharedassets.Decode is the only shared-asset decoder. The embedded binaries call it and have no decoder of their own, so the "cmd/runtimes decoders" in CODE-2 never existed as a separate site. — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:135, cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:118, pkg/adapter/sharedassets/sharedassets.go:66
FACT: the embedded runtime images build from the root distroless Dockerfile. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/install.sh:190,194; Makefile:210 (echo-embedded-tarball); .github/workflows/release.yml:115; scripts/cloud/aws/build-images.sh:59
FACT: ConnectGateway callers are cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413, cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:153, cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go, and pkg/adapter/gatewaylink_test.go. No pod has a liveness or startup probe: only the sidecar adapter has a tcp readiness probe (podspec/sidecar.go:89). A long fill before Listen therefore delays readiness but does not kill the container.
WATCHOUT: Dockerfile.git must put the binary at the same PlatformBinaryPath, because the embedded container's preStop hook execs it. CODE-1 step 1's "builds the binary the same way ... same ENTRYPOINT" covers this, so do not loosen that wording. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/embedded.go (Lifecycle: preStopDrainHook comment)
OPEN: summary open question 4 says rendering adds a chart value, a controller flag, and an adapter argument. It omits that a rendered argument also lands on the embedded runtime container (the checkpointProbeArgs pattern, podspec/embedded.go:54), so echo-embedded and preconnect-echo would have to define the flag or flag.Parse exits. This omission predates G4 and is a separate finding.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 step 6 builds the slot git directory in "a fresh directory under `/workspace/staging/`" and sets it to 0755 before the rename; the "as CODE-6 bullet (2) does" pointer goes — BECAUSE CODE-6 (2)'s 0700 dir is per-operation scratch that is deleted, while step 6's dir becomes the session's `.git`, and promotion carries its own mode onto the slot (pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go promotionStagingMode comment) — ALTERNATIVES: recursive chmod 0755/0444 as CODE-4 step 6 does for stores (rejected: the slot repo is agent-written afterwards, and the cold path does no such thing; parity with the cold path's 0755 extracted dirs, extract.go TypeDir case, is the bar); `core.sharedRepository` (rejected: extra config surface for no gain).
FACT: adapter UID 65532 and agent UID 65533 differ on sidecar pods and share only the fsGroup cred-readers group — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go AdapterUID/AgentUID; base_pod.go FSGroup. Git creates children of the git dir under the adapter umask, as the cold path's MkdirAll does, so only the top directory needs the explicit mode.
OPEN: in sidecar mode every adapter-materialized workspace entry (cold gitClone included) is adapter-owned 0755, so whether the runtime UID can WRITE its `.git` (commit) at all is unverified and predates 0111. A later round should check before filing; do not fold it into CODE-5.
DECISION: discriminating test for the mode fix is a clause on TEST-1 item 17 (Tier 5, sidecar-model pool, real UID split): the runtime runs `git status` in a cache-served source. A Tier 1 mode assertion alone would pass under a single test UID without proving traversal.
DECISION: findings 6, 17, 15, 19 are test-list edits only (items 6, 10, 20); no CODE or spec text changes. Item 10's "a copy failure" becomes "a failure of either copy", matching rule 5's "either copy".
### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 step 6 drops "mode-0700" and the "as CODE-6 bullet (2) does" pointer, and chmods the git directory to 0755 before the rename into `<path>/.git` — BECAUSE the pod-level `/workspace/staging` is already 0700, so it keeps the agent out while the directory is built, and the promoted `.git` has to be traversable by the runtime UID the way the cold path's extracted `.git` is — ALTERNATIVES: pinning every inner directory and file mode (the cold path does not do this either, because extract.go MkdirAll 0o755 is umask-filtered, so this goes beyond parity); a new spec sentence in rule 3 (rule 3 states no modes, and this is an implementation detail).
FACT: pod-level `/workspace/staging` is 0700 (warmStagingMode), while the per-slot promotion build tree is 0755 (promotionStagingMode), and promotion carries each entry's own mode onto the slot. Adapter UID 65532 and agent UID 65533 are distinct. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/warmlayout.go:77-81; pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go:124-128; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:52-53
WATCHOUT: CODE-6 bullet (2) keeps 0700 correctly, because its directory is deleted per operation and never promoted. Only CODE-5 step 6 promotes its directory. Do not "harmonize" the two. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 (2), CODE-5 step 6
UNVERIFIED: the runtime UID cannot write into adapter-owned 0755 workspace directories on the cold path either (the agent cannot commit to a cold `.git`). This is pre-existing, applies to the whole workspace, and is not caused by 0111. Whoever owns workspace writability in the sidecar model should check it. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/extract.go:59
DECISION: the runtime-UID test goes in TEST-1 item 17 (tier 5, sidecar-model pool), because tier-1 tests run as a single UID and `git status` passes there whatever the mode.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
DECISION: INFRA-1 step 3 is the one statement of the spike outcome; step 4 becomes "When step 3 does not pass, implementation stops ..." — BECAUSE the two steps disagreed on (c) and on a slow (d); keeping step 3's (d) escape is right because checkout is paid by the cold path too ((e) times it), so a slow checkout does not falsify the cache, while (c) is the cache's whole value claim and must stop — ALTERNATIVES: drop the (d) escape so a slow checkout stops (rejected: it gates on a cost both paths share); fold the outcome into step 4 (rejected: step 3 already enumerates the conditions).
DECISION: the spec-map rule goes as one sentence directly under `## Testing` in non-spec-changes.md, ahead of INFRA-1, so it covers INFRA-1's spike, TEST-1, and any CODE step that lands a test — BECAUSE the TEST-1 preamble would not govern INFRA-1, and per-deliverable Target lines would need the same clause four times — ALTERNATIVES: TEST-1 preamble (reviewer's suggestion; misses the spike); adding tests/spec-map.json to every Target line (restates one rule per deliverable).
FACT: validateTestFilesMapped walks every _test.go under componentAndAboveTierDirs (tier2..tier12, including tier7a_load_local) with no build-tag exemption; tests/tier4_integration is mapped wholesale, tier7a and the rest per file. A later round may move that preamble sentence up under `## Testing` beside the spec-map sentence; not done here (separate finding).

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]

FACT: a disk-backed emptyDir `sizeLimit` is enforced by kubelet eviction of the pod, not by ENOSPC; the spec says so for the slot volume ("if disk usage under the emptyDir volume exceeds the limit, the pod is evicted") and the summary relies on it (Decision 8 "evict the pod"). So CODE-3 step 3's "fetch fails for lack of space → delete url mirror, refetch once → RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED" never fires on the git-mirror volume CODE-3 step 9 sizes; the gateway replica is evicted instead, and TEST-1 item 16's mirror-full row cannot be produced. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:273; non-spec-changes.md:77, :94, :228; summary.md:25
WATCHOUT: the mirror has no shrink path other than that unreachable rebuild; every Ensure/ResolveRef only adds objects, so per-replica mirror growth is monotone over the replica's lifetime. Any fix needs an in-process byte budget below the volume sizeLimit (evict whole url mirrors under the write lock) rather than reacting to ENOSPC. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:74-77
WATCHOUT: CODE-3 step 3 makes every per-url operation hold one lock and "adds no reader-drain protocol", so the lock must cover the whole Pack stream; one slow FetchGitPack consumer then blocks Ensure/ValidateTree/Pack for that url on that replica up to the caller's deadline (15m warm fill vs 600s materialization). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:74, :77, :92, :104
FACT: agent pods are RestartPolicy Never (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:47) and the adapter is a regular container, so the warm-fill `.tmp-<hash>` → rename and the in-memory url map have no adapter-restart recovery case to handle. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/base_pod.go:47
