### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: ValidateTree tree walks (memo misses) share the mirror-held maxConcurrentPacks limiter with Pack, and the CODE-3 step 9 memory formula drops the "+ 1" walk term — BECAUSE walks on distinct memo keys were bounded only by the 200-slot Upload Handler gate, which is not sized to memory — ALTERNATIVES: a separate walk limiter added to the formula (rejected: one more chart knob for the same memory budget).
WATCHOUT: the limiter now lives on gitref.Mirror (binder and handler both reach it); a walk started inside a Pack reuses that Pack slot, otherwise eight Packs each needing a re-walk after memo eviction self-deadlock. Acquisition order is UploadGate then limiter, Pack takes the limiter only, so no cycle — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 7.
WATCHOUT: step 9 sizes a walk at a pack peak; INFRA-1 phase (g) measures no walk RSS, it relies on a walk listing no more objects than the shallow pack of the same commit.
DECISION: the startup mirror wipe is ordered at gitref.Mirror construction, before any mirror operation — BECAUSE the binder (CODE-5 step 2) calls Ensure and ValidateTree outside FetchGitPack.
FACT: citer sweep over "maxConcurrentPacks", "limiter", "walk", "startup", and "wipe" across every proposal file found no stale description outside CODE-3 steps 3, 7, 9 and TEST-1 item 20; item 20 gained a walk-waits assertion. The Standing context line "Pack limiter ... Only ValidateTree stays on the Upload Handler gate" is now incomplete: walks also take the pack limiter.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: applied the G1 design as four in-place replacements in non-spec-changes.md (CODE-3 step 3 creation clause gains `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`; Ensure fetches `<sha>:refs/lenny/<sha>` in gitref.Clone's environment; the capacity pass runs before Ensure takes the requested url's lock and takes the requested url's mirror last; a startup wipe of the mirror root replaces "The mirror writes no durable state."; TEST-1 item 16's capacity sentence replaced and gains the refspec and restart clauses) — BECAUSE the design removes the duplicate-history cause, the only out-of-lock mirror writer, and the crash-left partial state with no new branch or path — ALTERNATIVES: see the WATCHOUTs and CORRECTS in non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1.
FACT: re-verified on git 2.50.1 with gc.auto=0, maintenance.auto=false, and transfer.unpackLimit=1: `git fetch -- file://src <A>:refs/lenny/<A>` then `<B>:refs/lenny/<B>` (B child of A) wrote both refs, and the second pack held 3 objects (commit, tree, blob) against 6 in the first. EVIDENCE: /tmp reproduction, verify-pack -v object counts.
FACT: gitref.Clone's authenticated fetch env comes from authEnv, which always appends GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 and adds the extraHeader only when a token is set, so "gitref.Clone's environment with no credential" means GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 and no header. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/cred.go:35-52; clone.go:69-94.
WATCHOUT: item 16's refspec assertion counts the second pack's objects, but a fetch below fetch.unpackLimit/transfer.unpackLimit (default 100) explodes the objects loose and leaves no pack. The test sets transfer.unpackLimit=1 on the mirror under test or counts received objects from a packet trace. EVIDENCE: reproduction above; git-config fetch.unpackLimit.
UNVERIFIED: the gc.auto/maintenance.auto config is asserted only by the TEST-1 item 5 clause "every CODE-3 step 3 key" that the G2 group stages per the G1 design. At this edit item 5 carries no such clause. If G2 does not land it, the gc keys have no test; the next reviewer should check item 5.
USEFUL [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]: the design's site adjudication and CORRECTS entries covered every review-log site, so this fix touched no review-log body.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: bound gateway mirror git memory by config at creation (CODE-3 step 3 adds `pack.threads=1`, `pack.windowMemory=64m`, `pack.deltaCacheSize=64m`, `core.deltaBaseCacheSize=32m` to G1's clause), single-flight concurrent ValidateTree per memo key (step 4), measure summed peak RSS in INFRA-1 phase (g) with a pass bullet that the full fetch fits the fill deadline, and size the default `gateway.resources.limits.memory` in step 9 (request + fetch peak + (maxConcurrentPacks+1) x larger pack peak; values.yaml comment: +1 fetch peak per extra url fetched at once) — BECAUSE child git processes are charged to the gateway container cgroup (512Mi limit, charts/lenny/values.yaml gateway.resources; no tier-3 preset override) and git's default caches are unbounded per process — ALTERNATIVES: lowering the maxConcurrentPacks default (changes a Settled default, throttles everyone); putting fetches under the pack limiter (deadlocks with Pack's in-slot Ensure, one slow remote starves all urls); a separate fetch limiter key (extra key and test row; left OPEN); raising the memory request (reserves capacity in every deployment).
WATCHOUT: the step 9 sentence is the one home of the memory sizing rule. Do not repeat the formula in the summary, the checklist, or DOCS-1. TEST-1 item 5's "every CODE-3 step 3 key" assertion is the single config test and covers G1's gc keys, so do not add a second config clause elsewhere. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 9, TEST-1 item 5
WATCHOUT: measure phase (g) as the summed RSS of the process tree (cgroup memory.peak or equivalent), because getrusage maxrss reports one child and the cgroup charges all of them (fetch spawns index-pack; ValidateTree pipes rev-list into cat-file). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md INFRA-1 step 2 (g)
FACT: no spec or docs page states the gateway's default memory limit, and no chart unittest pins it; the runbooks gateway-capacity.md and gateway-replica-failure.md only say to raise `gateway.resources.limits.memory`, which stays true. — EVIDENCE: lookup exact "gateway.resources" over spec+docs; grep charts/lenny/tests
OPEN: whether mirror fetches for distinct urls need their own concurrency bound; step 9's values.yaml comment documents the per-url increment instead. A later performance round may revisit.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 17 names the `cred-shell-echo` sidecar Runtime and asserts the runtime container reads the slot's `.git/objects/info/alternates` and a store pack file; the runtime `git status` clause is gone — BECAUSE the adapter (UID 65532) owns the slot repo and git refuses it as "dubious ownership" for the runtime UID 65533 at any mode, and no sidecar runtime image carries git — ALTERNATIVES: `git -c safe.directory='*' status` plus a git test image (asserts config the product never sets; Traps reject safe.directory), a tier-1 mode assertion (misses cross-UID promotion and alternates-path resolution).
FACT: `cred-shell-echo` is the only sidecar runtime in the kind workload with sh and cat (Alpine, cmd/runtimes/cred-shell-echo/Dockerfile; Runtime `cred-shell-echo-runtime`, deploymentModel sidecar, tests/testinfra/kind/agent-workload.yaml). The runtime container runs as agentUID via containerSecurityContext regardless of the image's USER 65532 — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go (SecurityContext: containerSecurityContext(in.agentUID())), shared mount ReadOnly at sidecar.go:47.
DECISION: TEST-1 item 18 carries a success row for each embedded image on Dockerfile.git (`echo-embedded` and `preconnect-echo`) — BECAUSE only a success row fails on a git-less image — ALTERNATIVES: drop preconnect-echo from CODE-1 step 2 (it runs the CODE-4 fill inside ConnectGateway and needs git).
WATCHOUT: no runtime-side git command can pass against an adapter-materialized repository without a safe.directory override; any future test that runs git as the runtime UID in a slot will fail on dubious ownership — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go AdapterUID 65532 / AgentUID 65533; no Chown in pkg/adapter.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G4.1]
WATCHOUT: no hex string of a length other than 40 is a malformed `want`: gitRef registration rejects only `^[0-9a-f]{40}$`, so a 39-hex (or any non-40 hex) branch name is a legal ref and a FetchGitPack test must not expect INVALID_ARGUMENT for it; an absent one is the handler table's NOT_FOUND row. `HEAD~1` covers non-ref syntax. TEST-1 item 13 dropped its 39-hex case. — EVIDENCE: pkg/workspaceplan/refresolve.go IsCommitSHA; spec-changes.md §4.7.1 FetchGitPack row "`want` (a ref or a 40-hex commit SHA)"; non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 step 6 handler table
DECISION: TEST-1 item 7 pins the zero-value `GitStoreFillTimeout` → 15m default by asserting the context deadline through the fetcher seam — BECAUSE CODE-4 step 2.1 makes this deadline the only bound on a stalled non-preConnect fill and the embedded runtimes rely on the zero default — ALTERNATIVES: restating the default and its rationale in the test item (second copy of CODE-4 step 2.1's rule).

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: one mirror-state design closes the capacity-wedge, auto-gc, and crash-mid-deletion findings in CODE-3 step 3, with four in-place replacements: (1) Ensure fetches with the refspec `<sha>:refs/lenny/<sha>` so later fetches send haves; (2) mirrors are created with `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`; (3) the capacity pass runs before Ensure takes the requested url's lock and deletes the requested url's mirror last instead of excluding it; (4) the gateway empties the mirror root at startup, replacing "The mirror writes no durable state." — BECAUSE the wedge's cause is the ref-less fetch duplicating full history on every fetch (reproduced: second ref-less fetch packs 9 objects, with a ref 3), the only out-of-lock mirror writer is fetch-launched gc, and a startup wipe also clears stale git `.lock` files a crash leaves, which rename-to-.trash does not — ALTERNATIVES: a post-fetch "rebuild once if the fresh mirror alone exceeds maxBytes" branch (hair: a second measurement and branch; deleting the exclusion closes it); keeping auto-gc and relying on refs for reachability (detached gc runs outside the lock and a repack of a multi-GB repo inside the gateway's 512Mi memory limit risks OOM-killing the replica, charts/lenny/values.yaml gateway.resources.limits); rename-to-`<root>/.trash/` plus startup trash cleanup (keeps the cache warm across a restart but leaves crash-orphaned ref locks; more text); generation-swap rebuild with reader drain (new state and a sweeper; rejected for simplicity).
FACT: `git fetch -- <url> <sha>:refs/lenny/<sha>` works with a full-SHA source on git 2.50.1 and the next fetch negotiates from the written ref; a ref-less mirror sends no haves. Small fetches below fetch.unpackLimit are exploded loose and duplicate loose objects are skipped, so a duplicate-history test must count the received pack's objects (set transfer.unpackLimit=1 or trace), not loose objects. EVIDENCE: local reproduction in a temp dir; pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/clone.go fetch form (no refspec destination).
WATCHOUT: the capacity pass must run before Ensure takes the requested url's shared lock. Run under that lock, the try-lock on the requested url always fails and the one-url wedge returns. A have-Ensure nested inside Pack runs under Pack's hold, so the url is correctly skipped there. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 3 "Each operation on a url ... holds that url's lock shared for its duration"; step 5 "Unknown haves get one `Ensure` each".
WATCHOUT: residual, accepted: a url over budget whose mirror some operation holds at every pass (a hot url at Tier 3 under continuous Pack streams) still fails fetches with RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED until a moment no operation holds it. With refs the mirror grows only by new objects, so reaching maxBytes takes far longer than before. A repository larger than maxBytes on its own is deleted and refetched on every fetch, or refused while held; the operator raises gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes. Do not re-file either without proposing something smaller than a generation-swap rebuild.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 DECISION at review-log "rebuilding the requested url (a fetch by SHA brings full history, so the rebuilt mirror is rarely smaller)"] and [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 "Rebuilding the requested url when it alone is over budget was dropped"]: the premise was false under ref-less fetches (each fetch stored another history copy). The requested url is now a deletion candidate, taken last, and the refspec removes the duplication.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 WATCHOUT "do not reintroduce ... any ENOSPC or 'rebuild once' branch"]: still holds for ENOSPC-reactive and post-fetch rebuild branches. It does not forbid the LRU pass considering the requested url; that deletion is the same try-lock deletion as any other url's.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 WATCHOUT malformed-object row "Asserting 'no ref' would pass vacuously, because a fetch by SHA creates no ref"]: Ensure now writes refs/lenny/<sha>, so the reason is stale; the advice to assert the commit object is absent still stands (fsck rejection writes neither object nor ref).
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 OPEN "decide between gc.auto=0 on the mirror and a ref per ensured commit"]: decided: both. The ref is for negotiation and gc is off so nothing rewrites a mirror outside its lock.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close the gateway-memory finding with one envelope: bound each mirror git process in CODE-3 step 3's creation config (`pack.threads=1`, `pack.windowMemory=64m`, `pack.deltaCacheSize=64m`, `core.deltaBaseCacheSize=32m`), single-flight `ValidateTree` per memo key in step 4, measure summed peak RSS in a new INFRA-1 phase (g), and size the default `gateway.resources.limits.memory` in step 9 as request + fetch peak + (`maxConcurrentPacks` + 1) x larger pack peak — BECAUSE a limit (not a request) reserves nothing, so raising it costs no scheduling capacity, and keeping `maxConcurrentPacks` at 8 leaves the Settled pack-limiter entry and TEST-1 items 20/22 true — ALTERNATIVES: lowering `maxConcurrentPacks` as the knob (two knobs, the rule must pick one); a git-mirror sidecar container with its own limit (new in-pod protocol); RLIMIT_DATA via a `prlimit` wrapper (adds a util-linux dependency to the distroless git image); counting mirror fetches in the pack limiter (a Pack's unknown-have Ensure inside its slot deadlocks, and one slow remote starves packs for healthy urls, the rejected breaker failure mode); serializing all fetches per replica (K urls x multi-minute full fetches on a fresh replica overrun the 600s materialization bound and the 15m fill deadline).
FACT: no spec, docs, or chart test states or pins the gateway's 512Mi memory limit or 256Mi request, so the step 9 default change is non-spec only. spec/17 §17.7 Gateway replica failure remediation ("increase memory requests in Helm values") stays true. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:2456-2462; lookup over spec, docs, charts for gateway memory limit/request returned only spec/17_deployment-topology.md:811.
FACT: the shipped cold path already runs `git fetch` in the gateway container with default git memory settings and no concurrency bound per session (gitref.Clone, called by CloneArchive outside any gate). The mirror lowers that exposure to one fetch per url per replica. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/clone.go:71-94
WATCHOUT: `pack.threads=1` also caps index-pack's delta-resolution threads during the mirror fetch, which slows the initial full fetch of a large repository. That is why INFRA-1 phase (g) records the full fetch's time and INFRA-1 step 3 gains a bullet requiring it to finish within CODE-4 step 2.1's default fill deadline. Do not drop that bullet as padding.
WATCHOUT: measure the summed peak RSS of every process a step spawns (fetch spawns remote-https and index-pack; the shallow pack is a rev-list | pack-objects pipe). getrusage maxrss and `/usr/bin/time` report the largest single child, and cgroup `memory.peak` counts page cache. The container cgroup charges the sum.
WATCHOUT: Kubernetes 1.28+ on cgroup v2 sets memory.oom.group on containers, so an OOM caused by a git child kills the gateway process as well. Do not argue that the kernel would pick the git child.
OPEN: concurrent mirror fetches for different urls stay unbounded (only per-url single-flight exists). Step 9's values.yaml comment tells the operator each further url fetched at once adds one fetch peak, and the default covers one. A later round may want a fetch cap. Read the alternatives above before proposing one.
OPEN (separate finding, not staged): the shipped cold gitClone path (gitref.CloneArchive, ungated) runs unbounded concurrent full fetches and an in-memory archive in the gateway container. It belongs in the summary's **Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage** list. Nobody has filed it.
OPEN (separate finding, not staged): DOCS-1 documents no `gateway.gitMirror.*` Helm value. The new values (`maxBytes`, `maxConcurrentPacks`) are operator-tunable but have no reference page row.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 17 runs on a sidecar-model pool whose Runtime uses the `cred-shell-echo` image, and its runtime-UID clause asserts file readability (the runtime container reads the slot's `.git/objects/info/alternates` and a pack file under the store directory that file names) instead of running `git status` — BECAUSE the 0755 on the promoted `.git` delivers cross-UID read and traverse access and nothing more; git refuses any repository another UID owns ("detected dubious ownership", git >= 2.35.2) whatever the mode, and no sidecar runtime image carries git — ALTERNATIVES: `git -c safe.directory='*' status` plus a git-bearing test runtime image (adds an image deliverable and asserts a git config the product never sets); deleting the clause and adding a tier-1 `.git` mode == 0755 assertion (add rung, and it misses promotion dropping the mode and the absolute alternates path resolving inside the runtime container's `/workspace/shared` mount).
FACT: `cred-shell-echo` is the only sidecar-model runtime image with a shell and `cat` (Alpine); `echo-runtime-sidecar` is distroless static, so item 17's existing EROFS write clause was equally unrunnable there. Its Runtime and template sit in the shared kind workload, and tier 9 already execs into its `runtime` container. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/agent-workload.yaml:114-137; tests/testinfra/kind/install.sh:184-200; cmd/runtimes/cred-shell-echo/Dockerfile; tests/tier9_security/credential_file_contract_test.go:72,88 (execContainer "runtime")
FACT: the runtime container runs as agentUID (65533) and mounts `/workspace/shared` read-only at the same path the adapter mounts it read-write, so an absolute alternates path written by the adapter resolves in the runtime container. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:36,47,109
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1 DECISION at review-log :322]: the 0755 reason is read and traverse parity with the cold path's adapter-owned 0755 `.git`; a 0700 `.git` leaves the runtime unable to READ the repository. "Unable to run git" is wrong as a reason: git refuses an adapter-owned repository as the runtime UID at any mode.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1 FACT at review-log :325]: item 17's runtime-UID check is a `cat` readability check on the `cred-shell-echo` pool; there is no runtime `git status` check.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1 DECISIONs at review-log :383 and :390]: the discriminating test for the mode fix is item 17's runtime-UID read of the slot's alternates file and a store pack, on the `cred-shell-echo` sidecar pool.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-G4.1 WATCHOUT :315, non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1 DECISION :371]: item 18 has two success rows, echo-embedded and preconnect-echo; each discriminates its own image's git binary, because the preConnect failure row passes on a git-less image.
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce any runtime-side git command in a tier-5 row. It fails on dubious ownership for every adapter-materialized repository, the cold gitClone path included, and the trap list already rejects `safe.directory=*`. — EVIDENCE: review-log Traps "No `-c` overrides, no `safe.directory=*`"
OPEN: in sidecar mode every adapter-materialized repository (cold and cache-served) is owned by UID 65532, so the agent cannot run git in its workspace unless its image sets `safe.directory` in system or global config. This predates 0111 and joins the writability UNVERIFIED at review-log :389. Whether the spec promises in-workspace git for sidecar runtimes decides whether it is a defect for the summary's list.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 13 drops ", for a 39-hex `want`," and adds no replacement clause — BECAUSE the staged `want` contract admits any ref, and gitRef registration rejects only exact 40-hex, so a 39-hex string is a legal branch name; the absent-ref NOT_FOUND row is already a handler-table row and its own test case is not owed by this finding — ALTERNATIVES: keep a malformed-SHA case with a different value (no value distinguishes a malformed SHA from a legal ref name except non-ref syntax like `HEAD~1`, which item 13 already carries).
DECISION: TEST-1 item 7 gains "a zero `GitStoreFillTimeout` yields a 15m fill deadline (asserted on the context deadline through the fetcher seam);" after "deadline expiry is fatal;" — BECAUSE the embedded runtimes leave the field at zero (CODE-4 step 2.1), so a zero-as-unbounded implementation would leave a stalled non-preConnect fill unbounded and pass every other listed test.
WATCHOUT: any future malformed-`want` test case must use a value that is not legal ref syntax (`HEAD~1`, `a..b`); hex-length variants are legal branch names. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 13; spec-changes.md "registration rejects a `gitRef` that matches `^[0-9a-f]{40}$`".

FACT: no runtime image in the tree carries git. runtime-echo, echo-embedded, and preconnect-echo build from the root distroless Dockerfile (tests/testinfra/kind/install.sh:189-194, Dockerfile:23), and cred-shell-echo is bare alpine:3.20 with /bin/sh, cat, and ls only (cmd/runtimes/cred-shell-echo/Dockerfile). FACT: the embedded runtimes only decode --shared-assets into adapterSrv.SharedAssets and call adapterSrv.ConnectGateway before they listen, so the CODE-4 fill inside ConnectGateway reaches all three binaries. ### [non-spec.2.review-citations.1]

### [non-spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: git >= 2.35.2 refuses any command in a repository whose git dir or worktree is owned by another UID ("fatal: detected dubious ownership", rc 128), whatever the mode bits; reproduced locally with git 2.50.1 (root-owned repo, a+rX, run as ec2-user). safe.directory is honoured only from protected config (system, global, or `-c` on the command line), never from the repo. Adapter UID 65532 and agent UID 65533 differ by default. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:52-53; spec/04_system-components.md:1300
WATCHOUT: mode 0755 on the slot git dir (CODE-5 step 6) does not let the runtime UID run git there; the TEST-1 item 17 `git status` clause fails on a sidecar pool unless the runtime's git carries safe.directory. The cold gitClone path has the same ownership (adapter extracts .git), so this is not new to 0111. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 6, TEST-1 item 17
FACT: `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <sha>` lists the commit, the root tree, every subtree, and each distinct blob once (deduplicated), and `%(rest)` in cat-file batch-check carries the path; verified locally. fetch.fsckObjects rejects a tree entry named `.git` (hasDotgit) and leaves the commit absent; verified locally.
FACT: the only ConnectGateway callers are cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:413, cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:158, and cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:134, each after EnsureWarmWorkspaceLayout and before ready; embedded pods get --gateway-grpc-addr via platformMCPArgs (podspec/embedded.go:53). release.yml carries runtime-echo-embedded (line 115); build-images.sh carries lenny-runtime-preconnect-echo (line 59).

### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: a ref-less `git fetch -- <url> <sha>` into a bare mirror sends no haves, so a second fetch of a child commit re-downloads the whole history (reproduced on git 2.50.1: pack of 9 objects, then pack of 12 objects for one extra commit). The fetched objects are unreachable, and `git gc --prune=now` removes them (reproduced). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/clone.go:89-94; non-spec-changes.md:75
FACT: `git fetch` runs `git maintenance run --auto` by default, and `gc.autoDetach` defaults to true, so auto-gc runs in the background after the fetch returns, outside any lock the caller holds. EVIDENCE: `git help fetch` (--auto-maintenance), `git help gc` (gc.autoDetach)
WATCHOUT: the round-1 refutation of "ref-less mirror re-downloads history" rested on "a mirror that runs out of space is deleted and refetched once". This round removed that path and excludes the requested url from capacity deletion, so the refutation's premise no longer holds. Decision text "a SHA fetch brings full history, so a rebuild rarely shrinks the mirror" is wrong: the mirror holds one full-history copy per fetched commit, so a rebuild shrinks it to one. EVIDENCE: review-log.md:293, :345; history non-spec.r2 line 72
CORRECTS [review-log.md:351 OPEN]: the gc question is now a defect, because the new lock model asserts "A fetch adds objects beside running readers" while the fetch's detached auto-gc repacks and prunes. Filed this round.
FACT: all three binaries call `Server.ConnectGateway` exactly once, after EnsureWarmWorkspaceLayout and SharedAssets are set, and log.Fatalf on its error, so the CODE-4 relocation into ConnectGateway holds. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:390-416; cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:142-161; cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:120-137

### [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1]
FACT: charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml is the §17.4 Embedded Mode profile `lenny up` pre-renders over values.yaml; its `gateway.resources` override pins requests cpu 100m / memory 128Mi and limits cpu "1" / memory 512Mi, so any change to the values.yaml gateway memory default never reaches Embedded Mode unless the preset changes too. pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml is the byte-equal render under that preset, pinned by tier-11 TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender_spec_17_4. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml:1-16,183-209; pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:1-3; tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go:88,95
DECISION: CODE-3 step 9 deletes only the `memory` limit from dev.yaml's `gateway.resources` override, and CODE-3 Targets, Files touched, and the summary CODE-3 line add dev.yaml and the regenerated embedded manifests — BECAUSE step 9 stays the single home of the gateway memory sizing, Helm deep-merges -f values so the dev 128Mi request and cpu limit survive, and a limit reserves nothing on a laptop — ALTERNATIVES: a dev-specific maxConcurrentPacks plus a dev memory figure (second sizing home whose inputs are unknown until the spike runs); leaving 512Mi and exempting Embedded Mode (keeps the OOM case, a gitRepository Runtime registers fine under `lenny up`); deleting the whole dev `gateway.resources` block (raises the dev request, contradicting the preset's small-requests posture).
WATCHOUT: do not add a TEST-1 Helm unittest row for dev.yaml; the tier-11 embedded-manifests sync test already pins the regenerated render. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go:88
DECISION: INFRA-1 (g) measures each pipeline that holds a CODE-3 step 7 limiter slot, each `Pack` mode separately (delta with (b)'s have), and step 9 multiplies maxConcurrentPacks by the largest limiter-slot peak — BECAUSE the closed list missed the ref-want warm pack (SPEC-5: every object reachable from the resolved commit, i.e. full history, issued by CODE-4 step 2.1 on every warming pod), and citing step 7 covers the ValidateTree walk and any future slot holder — ALTERNATIVES: appending "the warm pack" to the list and "larger"→"largest" (same closed list, still omits the ValidateTree walk); git config to bound the warm pack (per-object entry cost is not capped by pack.* keys).
OPEN: INFRA-1 step 3 has no pass criterion on (g)'s memory peaks; whether the spike should fail when the sized default limit exceeds some ceiling is a separate decision not taken here.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 Tier 7a item 20 gains one clause: at pack limit 1, a delta `Pack` on a commit no `ValidateTree` has walked completes before its deadline (discriminating assertion for CODE-3 step 7's nested slot) — BECAUSE an implementation whose nested ValidateTree walk takes a second limiter slot self-deadlocks at limit 1, and no existing row had a memo-miss Pack — ALTERNATIVES: an extra limit-2 two-Pack row (same defect caught, more text); restating step 7's rule in the test (second home).
WATCHOUT: the row must use a delta Pack (a have that resolves). Only step 5's delta mode reads the ValidateTree memo; a ref-want (warm) or shallow Pack starts no walk and passes on the deadlocking implementation. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 5 ("compared with the summed blob and tree-object bytes from the memo").
OPEN: CODE-3 steps 4 and 7 leave two things unstated: the memo key a Pack's lookup uses (the key includes pathPrefix and allow, which a Pack call does not carry), and the interleaving where a Pack joins a shared memo computation whose leader is a standalone ValidateTree waiting for the slot the Pack holds (deadlock at limit 1). A later round should design both.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Embedded Mode gateway memory — delete the `memory: 512Mi` line under `gateway.resources.limits` in charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml so the dev profile inherits CODE-3 step 9's default; add dev.yaml and pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml (regenerated) to CODE-3 Targets, Files touched, and the summary's CODE-3 component list — BECAUSE one sizing basis keeps one home (values.yaml default per step 9); a limit is not a reservation, so a larger limit costs a laptop nothing, and the dev comment only promises "small resource requests" (128Mi request stays) — ALTERNATIVES: dev-only `maxConcurrentPacks: 1` plus a separately sized dev limit (second sizing basis, hair; the formula's inputs are unknown until the spike anyway); leaving dev at 512Mi (OOM case step 9 exists to prevent).
DECISION: (g) defines its measured set by reference to CODE-3 step 7's limiter slot holders, taking each `Pack` mode separately, and step 9 multiplies by "the largest limiter-slot peak (g) measures" — BECAUSE the defect was a closed enumeration of pack kinds that missed the warm (ref-want, full-history) pack; adding it to the list is the same kind of answer, while step 7 is the home of what holds a slot (each Pack, each ValidateTree walk), so a reference covers the warm pack and the walk — ALTERNATIVES: append "the warm pack" to (g) and change "larger" to "largest" (reviewer's fix; same enumeration one item longer, still omits the ValidateTree walk that also holds a slot).

FACT: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml is a byte-equal `helm template --no-hooks` render under presets/dev.yaml, regenerated by `make generate` and pinned by TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender_spec_17_4; any gateway chart edit changes it — EVIDENCE: Makefile:65, tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go:88-95
WATCHOUT: "embedded adapter mode" (single-container echo-embedded/preconnect-echo images, summary :44, :110) and §17.4 "Embedded Mode" (`lenny up` k3s, presets/dev.yaml) are different things; findings conflate them. The dev-preset issue stands on its own because a Runtime with gitRepository can be registered under `lenny up`. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml:1-16
WATCHOUT: a full-history warm pack-objects keeps one object entry per object in history, so (g) may yield a per-slot peak near 1 GiB for the spike repository and step 9's default limit may land at several GiB with maxConcurrentPacks 8; INFRA-1 step 3 has no memory pass criterion, so a large result does not stop implementation. A later round may want the spike to report the resulting default limit. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md INFRA-1 step 3

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 Tier 7a item 20 gains one clause: with the pack limit at 1, a delta `Pack` on a commit no `ValidateTree` has walked completes before its deadline, the discriminating assertion for CODE-3 step 7's nested slot. — BECAUSE at limit 1 an implementation whose nested walk takes a second slot blocks on itself, so this one row catches the defect. — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's extra row (two Packs at limit 2) was rejected because it catches the same defect as the limit-1 row and adds nothing; restating step 7's rule in the test was rejected because the test points at the rule's home.
WATCHOUT: only a delta `Pack` (one with a have that resolves) reads the memo in step 5. A warm (ref `want`) or shallow `Pack` makes no comparison, so a row using one of those exercises no nested walk and passes on the wrong implementation. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 5 ("compared with the summed blob and tree-object bytes from the memo"), step 8 kinds.
OPEN: CODE-3 step 4 keys the memo by `(url, sha, pathPrefix, allow.AllowSymlinks, allow.WorkspaceRoot)`, but `Pack(ctx, url, want, haves, w)` has no pathPrefix or allow, so the key a Pack's memo lookup uses is unstated. Also, if a Pack's walk joins an in-flight shared computation (step 4, "Concurrent calls with one memo key share one computation") whose leader is a standalone `ValidateTree` still waiting for a slot that this Pack holds, the two block each other at limit 1 until their deadlines. Step 7's nested-slot clause does not cover that interleaving. A later round should decide whether the leader takes its slot before registering the flight, or whether a Pack never joins a flight. This is a separate finding, not this group's, and nothing is staged for it here.

### [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30]

- DECISION (owner, 2026-10-30): private repositories are in v1. Tenant-owned warm pools exist only where a Runtime caches a private repository. The fill uses the owning tenant's existing VCS credential pool through a credential lease. Gateway mirrors are keyed by (tenant, url) for private repositories and by url for public ones.
- DECISION (owner, 2026-10-30): a built-in generic `git-https` credential provider (username plus token or password from a secretRef) is added in this proposal, so any HTTPS git host works on the cold and warm paths. SSH stays deferred.
- DECISION (orchestrator under delegation, 2026-10-30): widen the §6.2 `/start` clause; render the fill timeout from a controller-wide chart value; add the capture-failure edge row; on derive or replay onto a Runtime that does not name the repository, fall back to the cold clone rather than failing.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

- DECISION: SPEC-8 (b2) says the gateway obtains the `git-https` pair from the Token Service (§4.3), which resolves the Secret per §4.9 **Token Service secret resolution.**; "as `namespace/name`" and "so a rotated or revoked credential takes effect at the next operation" are dropped, and the Edit (c) row note keeps only "The token carries no upstream expiry." CODE-7 swaps the production `vcscred.SecretReader` from `connectorsecret.NewKubeResolver` to a Token Service client over a new unary `ReadCredentialSecret` RPC, for every VCS provider — BECAUSE §4.9 makes the Token Service the sole owner and reader of credential material (resourceNames-scoped Role, live probe on the TS SA) and §4.6.3 and the chart give the gateway no Secret grant — ALTERNATIVES: a gateway resourceNames Secret grant plus live-probe changes (stages §4.6.3/§4.9 edits and contradicts "The Token Service owns all durable credential material" and the §13 Security Boundaries bullet); reusing `AssignCredentials` (requires `session_id`, pkg/tokenservice/grpc.go:79, and writes a lease row per call, so a warm fill has no session and every mirror operation would add a Postgres write); keeping the gateway read and filing a defect (new code knowingly against the spec); routing only `git-https` through the TS while `github` keeps the gateway read (two mechanisms on one seam).
- FACT: the `SecretReader` interface (`Resolve(ctx, ref) (string, error)`) is kept, so CODE-7's dispatch bullet, TEST-1 items 24 and 29, and the fakes stay true; only the production implementation and the TS RPC are new. The precedent is `tokenServiceSecretProber` in cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/vcscred/vcscred.go:67-73, cmd/lenny-gateway/auxstores.go:77-86, cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:145
- FACT: the Token Service implements no credential-Secret read today; lease material comes from pool config through credassign (`credentialSecret`), and its only Secret access is the `secretprobe` SSAR. `cmd/lenny-token-service` has a clientset and a `--secret-namespace` flag (default `lenny-system`). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:359,510; cmd/lenny-token-service/surfaces.go:82; cmd/lenny-token-service/flags.go:42
- WATCHOUT: a Token Service credential-Secret read does not work on a default chart install today. The chart renders no `lenny-token-service-secrets` Role (the admin handler only emits a `kubectl patch role` for it), and neither §13.2.4 nor charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml gives the Token Service kube-apiserver egress. Both are pre-existing and also break the §4.9 live probe; they are filed as one summary defect rather than fixed here. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:443; spec/13_security-model.md:278; system-network-policies.yaml apiserver egress only for admission-webhook, controller, and pool-scaling-controller
- WATCHOUT: §4.9 **Token Service secret resolution.** specifies an informer, while the RBAC Role grants only `get` with resourceNames, which cannot back a list/watch informer. - FACT: the edge-case row "The owning tenant's VCS credential is revoked, removed, or rebound" and SPEC-2 rule 4 stay true under the Token Service read. Revocation, removal, and rebinding are pool-store states that vcscred reads on every resolution (`pickActiveCredential`, `ResolveVCSPool`), and only a change to a Secret's contents waits on the TS resolution interval. — EVIDENCE: vcscred.go:118-127

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

FACT: a derived or replayed session carries no WorkspacePlan and so no `gitClone` source. `derive.go` and `replay.go` build the `sessionstore.Session` with `WorkspaceSnapshot` and `ParentWorkspaceRef` only. The binding decision's wording, "fall back to the cold gateway clone for that source", has no source to clone on a derive. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:358-378, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replay.go:195-209
FACT: §4.5 **Workspace lineage:** already defines `parent_workspace_ref` as recording "the session ID and artifact path of the workspace snapshot that seeded this session", and `agent_pod_state` carries `session_id`. Pod → bound session → parent session → `PoolRef` → Runtime can therefore be resolved from existing state. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:332, migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:222
DECISION: the derive/replay fallback is one sentence in the SPEC-5 `FetchGitPack` row. For a commit-SHA `want` from a pod whose bound session carries `parent_workspace_ref`, the gateway also accepts a url that is an entry on the Runtime of the pool of the session that ref names. That pool replaces the pod's pool for the private-entry checks and the mirror key, and a private entry additionally requires the bound session's tenant to equal that pool's `ownerTenantId`. §14 rule 6 and CODE-6 stay unchanged because the adapter still calls `FetchGitPack`. — BECAUSE a restored repository carries `lenny.storeCommit` only when the capturing pod held a store for its url, so the capturing session's pool Runtime named the url. This gives authorization parity with the pod that wrote the snapshot, takes one lineage step instead of a walk, and admits no url an administrator did not register. — ALTERNATIVES: (a) a gateway cold archive of the source extracted over the restored tree. The derived plan has no source, and a full-clone archive with `.git` would overwrite the agent's refs, index, and working tree, which is a wrong tree. (b) Authorizing any agent-written url, unauthenticated or by host binding. That makes the gateway a git proxy for the pod and reverses the option (b) non-goal. (c) Authorizing a url that is an entry on any Runtime. It needs a session-tenant check anyway for private keys and leaks admin-registered internal public repositories across Runtimes. (d) Copying the parent's plan sources into the derived session. The sources would then overwrite the restored repository under 0109's order. (e) A full lineage walk. One step suffices, because rule 6 removes `lenny.storeCommit` on a pod without a store, so a re-derived snapshot is self-contained.
WATCHOUT: a derive from a derived session that never checkpointed copies the grandparent's capture, while its `parent_workspace_ref` names the intermediate session, whose pool Runtime may not name the url. The fetch is then refused and the bind fails closed. This is accepted under the fail-closed and GC-handles-corner-cases preferences; do not extend it to a walk. — EVIDENCE: derive.go copySnapshotRef (a derived session's WorkspaceSnapshot is the copy until it checkpoints)
WATCHOUT: do not add "the adapter does not call `FetchGitPack`" or a gateway-side clone step to rule 6, as the G2 finding suggested. That suggestion assumed the derived plan carries the source. — EVIDENCE: derive.go:358-378
CORRECTS [Settled "Authorizer = pool Runtime" (spec.4.fix-G1.1)]: once this design lands, the SPEC-5 authorizer accepts the pod's pool Runtime, plus, for a commit-SHA want from a derived or replayed session, the Runtime of the pool of the session `parent_workspace_ref` names. §14 rule 1 eligibility still uses only the pod's pool Runtime.
CORRECTS [Open "derive/replay with targetRuntime" (spec.6.review-reliability.1)]: closed by this design. A restore fetch for a url the target Runtime does not name is authorized through `parent_workspace_ref`, and it is still refused when neither Runtime names the url, which is the accepted Runtime-entry-removal failure.
CORRECTS [Open "Agent-caused capture failures docs" (spec.2.review-docs-alignment.1)]: closed once G2 adds the edge-case bullet that points at the §13.4 exception.
USEFUL [Traps "Runtime-removal edge case stays conditional"]: confirmed that the line-23 removal bullet stays true under the new design and needs no edit.

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: FetchGitPack carries `SessionId session_id` on a commit-SHA want (the slot the pack is for), and the §4.7.1 derive clause fires only when that id names a session of the tenant on the calling pod's `claim-<podName>` SandboxClaim (`spec.tenantId`) that carries `parent_workspace_ref`; a private entry needs that tenant to equal the lineage pool's `ownerTenantId`. "The request is pod-scoped." is deleted because §4.1 Request Message Scope derives session scope from the `SessionId session_id` field — BECAUSE on a multi-session pod "the session bound to the pod" has no single referent and only the adapter knows which slot a restore serves; tenant is the boundary (co-tenant slots share the agent UID, §5.2 tenant pinning) — ALTERNATIVES: reject a session_id not bound to the pod (pod_assignment is written by commitResume only after the adapter Resume returns, so every rule 6 derive fetch would be denied); "a session bound to the pod" with no field (GetActiveSlotsByPod reads pod_assignment, misses the resuming session); tenant-keyed any-Runtime acceptance (widens beyond lineage, reopens decision 11); separate warm-fill RPC (duplicate surface); `lenny.dev/tenant-id` Pod label (stamped only on concurrent pods).
WATCHOUT: do not add a "session_id must be bound to the calling pod" check or read `pod_assignment`/`agentpodstate.SessionID` for the derive clause; a resuming session has no pod_assignment until commit, and agentpodstate holds one session per pod. CODE-3 step 6 carries this trap. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go completeResume → commitResume (writes r.PodAssignment after the outcome); spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 "The session-to-pod binding lives on the Postgres session row's `pod_assignment` column"
WATCHOUT: an absent, unset, or other-tenant session_id makes the clause not fire; the outcome is the existing CODE-3 PERMISSION_DENIED row (url on neither Runtime). Do not add an INVALID_ARGUMENT row for session_id on a ref want or a new PERMISSION_DENIED row. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 6 status table
FACT: the §5.2 "session-scoped request whose session identifier is empty is rejected at the adapter boundary" rule binds the adapter as receiver; FetchGitPack's receiver is the gateway, so an unset session_id on a ref want (warm fill) conflicts with nothing. The §4.1 tier-0 gate requires the pairing `SessionId session_id`. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:642; spec/04_system-components.md:155
FACT: SandboxClaim spec.tenantId is written at claim CREATE (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go, `TenantID: req.TenantID`), and §4.6.1 names it; the anchor is `#461-warm-pool-controller-pod-lifecycle`. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:395,407

### [spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: the capture-failure edge row's label now reads "**Agent-written slot content that the §13.4 exception rejects.**" and keeps the outcome sentence — BECAUSE the old label ("rewrites a slot repository's git directory or `lenny.baseCommit`") paraphrased the SPEC-4 Edit (a) predicate more broadly than its home (agent-written config, hooks, and includes are ignored rather than failed on, and a different well-formed `lenny.baseCommit` is fetched by rule 6) — ALTERNATIVES: listing the cases (symlink or non-directory git/objects dirs, non-40-hex baseCommit) restates the predicate and drifts the next time Edit (a) changes; deleting the row is barred by the binding orchestrator decision.
WATCHOUT: the edge-case list rows are pointers. A row's bold label must not paraphrase the predicate of the rule it cites, because a paraphrase is a second home that drifts from the rule. Name the rule and nothing else. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md edge-case list, the "Agent-written slot content" row; SPEC-4 Edit (a), spec-changes.md:160.
CORRECTS [spec.2.fix-*.? DECISION "capture-failure edge row added as a pointer to the §13.4 exception, listing none of the predicates" (review-log.md:744)]: the row as first added did paraphrase the predicate in its label. It is a pure pointer now.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: FetchGitPack's derive clause keys on a `sessionId` the request carries on a commit-SHA want (the slot's session). The clause fires only when that id names a session of the tenant on the calling pod's `claim-<podName>` SandboxClaim `spec.tenantId` (§4.6.1). A private entry is accepted only when that tenant equals the lineage pool's `ownerTenantId`. "The request is pod-scoped." is deleted, because §4.1 derives the scope from the field set. BECAUSE the adapter is the only party that knows which slot a restore serves, and the claim's tenantId is the one per-pod tenant record the gateway writes synchronously at acquisition. Tenant pinning makes it single-valued on a multi-session pod. ALTERNATIVES: (a) the reviewer's "reject a sessionId not bound to the pod". §4.6.1 defines the binding as `pod_assignment`, which a resuming session's row does not name until commitResume, so that check would deny every rule 6 fetch. (b) "a session bound to the pod" via GetActiveSlotsByPod: it reads the same pod_assignment and misses the resuming session. (c) A session-free, tenant-keyed clause accepting any registered entry: it widens acceptance, changes the Runtime-entry-removal accepted failure and the Goal qualification, and needs a tie-break among the tenant's pools with differing leaseScope. (d) A separate RPC for the warm fill: duplicate surface.
WATCHOUT: do not authorize the derive clause from `pod_assignment`, `GetActiveSlotsByPod`, or `agentpodstate.SessionID`. During `resuming` the row's pod_assignment is cleared on the resume_pending edge and written only by commitResume, after the adapter Resume (restore completion included) returns. agentpodstate is an async controller mirror that holds one SessionID per pod. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go commitResume (sets PodAssignment); pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go GetActiveSlotsByPod doc; pkg/agentpodstate/agentpodstate.go SessionID field.
FACT: SandboxClaim spec carries `sandboxRef` and `tenantId` (spec §4.6.1 CRD table and **Pod claim mechanism**). The gateway sets TenantID at claim CREATE (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go, slotclaimer.go). On a concurrent pool all sessions multiplex onto the one claim. EVIDENCE: pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/sandboxclaim_types.go SandboxClaimSpec.
FACT: only concurrent-slot pods get the `lenny.dev/tenant-id` Pod label in code (podclaim/slotclaimer.go). An exclusive pod has no label, so the label cannot serve as the pod's tenant source for FetchGitPack. EVIDENCE: grep LabelTenant under pkg/.
FACT: existing session-scoped GatewayControl RPCs (CallPlatformTool, ListConnectors) trust the adapter's session_id with no pod-binding check. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/platformtools.go CallPlatformTool doc comment.
WATCHOUT: after the fix FetchGitPackRequest declares `SessionId session_id`, so §4.1 classifies it as session-scoped, and the warm fill sends it unset. §4.1 does not forbid an unset address. A later lens that files "session-scoped request with no session" should be pointed at this entry. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.1 **Request Message Scope**.

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: Token Service resolves Secrets from an in-memory informer on `lenny-system` (30s resync), so the SPEC-8 (b2) per-operation Token Service call is an mTLS RPC to an in-memory lookup; at Tier 3 (4 Token Service replicas) a per-FetchGitPack call on private keys is negligible load. It sits under the gateway's Token Service breaker, so a Token Service outage fails private fills and private-key restores (fail-closed; shipped path already depended on a credential read per op via connectorsecret.NewKubeResolver). EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:675, :678; pkg/agentpodstate/agentpodstate.go:37-39
FACT: the derive-clause lookup reads Postgres only (agentpodstate pgstore, session row, checkpoint_manifest), runs only on a pod-Runtime miss for a SHA want, so it adds no etcd/Redis write and degrades only during a Postgres failover, when resume already stalls. EVIDENCE: proposal non-spec-changes.md:82

The source session therefore still exists when the derive clause runs. - FACT: the Token Service resolves Secrets through an informer with a 30 s resync, so (b2)'s per-operation Token Service read takes effect for a rotation on resync rather than at once. The fixer correctly deleted the "takes effect at the next operation" claims. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:2186

### [redesign.2]
- FACT: redesign 2 (scratchpad/redesign/0111_...-redesign-1.md, reconciled edit list) applied: E1 to E44 and E46 to E55 (the list has no E45). Every anchor occurred exactly once in its target file when checked, and no edit was skipped. Reconciliation added two edits the list did not carry: the CODE-7 opening sentence now names **Token Service secret resolution.** (SPEC-8 Edit (h)) and the §13.2.4 row (Edit (i)) among its homes, and the spec-changes Design paragraph says SPEC-10 "adds the VCS credential Secret read to the §28.7 Token Service artifact row" because SPEC-10 Edit (f) does not spell `ReadCredentialSecret`.
- DECISION: areas redesigned are (A) the VCS credential Secret read path, with §4.9 **Token Service secret resolution.** as its home (SPEC-8 Edits (h) and (i), CODE-7 steps 3, 7 rules T1 to T4, and 8, TEST-1 items 24 and 33 to 35, summary decision 22), and (B) restore-fetch authorization through the pod-scoped restore lineage record (§14 **Cache-served `gitClone` sources.** rule 7, SPEC-5, SPEC-10, CODE-3 step 6, CODE-9, TEST-1 items 11, 13, 21, and 36 to 38, summary decisions 11 and 15) — BECAUSE the chart grants the Token Service no Role and no kube-apiserver egress, so no credential read works on a chart install, and a `FetchGitPack` keyed by a `session_id` makes the request session-scoped while the warm fill has none and lets a pod borrow any same-tenant session's lineage.
- FACT: deleted: the §4.7.1 derive clause and `sessionId` field, `SessionId session_id = 5`, CODE-3 step 6's claim, session-row, and manifest-row lookup, CODE-5 step 5's fetcher session id, item 11's and item 13's `session_id` cases, decision 15's "no register row" claim, the §4.9 informer sentences, the `secret_ref` request field with its `InvalidArgument` namespace refusal, the summary defect "The Token Service cannot read credential Secrets on a chart install.", and the §28.5.7 register enumeration.
- WATCHOUT: do not restore a `session_id` on `FetchGitPack` or a derive clause that authorizes against the session `parent_workspace_ref` names.
  It makes the request session-scoped under §4.1 **Request Message Scope** while the warm fill has no session.
  A pod could name any session of its tenant on the claim and borrow that session's lineage.
  The pod-scoped `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:git_lineage` record (§14 rule 7) replaces it, and a concurrent pod holds every slot's pool.
- WATCHOUT: do not authorize a restore fetch from `pod_assignment`, `agent_pod_state`, or the `SandboxClaim`.
  A resuming session's `pod_assignment` is written only after the Resume returns.
  `agent_pod_state` holds one session per pod, and the claim spec is fixed for the whole occupancy.
  CODE-9 writes the record in `Binder.Resume` after `runOnClaimed` and before `handshake`, with an expiry computed at the grant.
- WATCHOUT: do not return to a Token Service informer on Secrets, or to a `ReadCredentialSecret` keyed by a raw `secret_ref`.
  The §4.9 Role grants only `get` on named Secrets, so no list or watch can back an informer.
  A raw-reference read returns any Secret the Role names through a call that exists only for VCS pools.
  Rule T2 keys the read on tenant, pool, and credential and refuses non-VCS pools and revoked credentials before any Secret read.
- WATCHOUT: do not file the Token Service chart RBAC and egress gap as an unstaged defect again, and do not treat a one-segment `secretRef` as malformed.
  The 2026-10-30 owner decision requires any HTTPS Git host to work on the cold and warm paths, and neither path reads a credential on a chart install without the Role and egress.
  The chart seeds bare Secret names (`bootstrap.defaultGithubVcsPool.secretRef`, `anthropic-api-key`), so rule T3 reads a one-segment ref in `--secret-namespace`, which CODE-7 step 8 sets to the release namespace.
  This supersedes the earlier WATCHOUT that kept the informer tension out of 0111.
- WATCHOUT: do not re-add an enumeration of register identifiers to the §28.5.7 sentence; SPEC-10 Edit (e) deletes it so that a new register row such as `REG-GITLINEAGE` leaves no second list to go stale.
- FACT: related-passages lookup ran once with five changed statements (the `FetchGitPack` request and authorization, the §4.9 informer sentence, the §13.2.4 Token Service egress, the §28.5.7 enumeration, and the §12.4 pod-scoped-key exception). The §4.9 rotation step 2 and **Ordering:** leads are already SPEC-8 Edit (h) sites. The §28.6 **Units carried outside the channel register.** paragraph names only exclusion primitives, and `REG-GITLINEAGE` is not one, so it stays true. The docs leads (`adapter-contract.md` `sessionId` on `tool_call`, `echo-runtime.md`, `session-lifecycle.md` derive example) concern other frames and stay true. No staged edit was added under spec/ or docs/.
- UNVERIFIED: after Edit (h), the §4.9 sentence that keeps "within its resync interval" and `lenny_token_service_secret_reloads_total` (§16.1) no longer name the mechanism that refreshes the Token Service's in-memory LLM credential cache (§4.3, §13.3 **Translator trust boundary:**). A later round decides whether that cache refresh needs a sentence.
- OPEN: OD1, a payload-logging exclusion for `ReadCredentialSecret` in §16.4 and §4.7.11 item 6. Default (b) stages no spec edit, because CODE-7 step 7 already requires that neither the handler nor the client logs the value.
- OPEN: OD2, the §4.9 Role example `resourceNames: []`. Default (b) leaves the example unchanged; CODE-7 step 8 renders a placeholder entry so the rendered list is never empty.
- FACT: the summary's **Open decisions for human to make** was not edited for OD1 or OD2, matching how [redesign.1] recorded its open decisions.
- FACT: kept on purpose after the sweep: the CODE-3 step 1 proto comment "No session_id: the request is pod-scoped" and the CODE-3 step 6 sentence "It reads no session row, `pod_assignment`, `agent_pod_state`, or `SandboxClaim`", because the redesign wrote both as implementor constraints, and the summary **Non-goals** entries for the rejected alternatives.

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
- FACT: §14 rule 5 copies into the slot only objects reachable from refs/reflogs/index and NOT from `lenny.baseCommit`, so a captured workspace of an alternates-built slot omits baseCommit's closure. Only rule 6 (restore of a captured workspace) completes it. Paths that move a snapshot without rule 6 (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` download, the §19 Decision 12 manual derive via `uploadArchive`, delegation file exports) carry an incomplete repository with an `objects/info/alternates` naming a pod path. No round had considered these paths. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 5; spec/19_resolved-decisions.md:18; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:698
- FACT: Token Service RBAC is already get-only with Helm-populated resourceNames (spec/04_system-components.md:1682-1693), so SPEC-8 Edit (h) "the only access the lenny-token-service-secrets Role grants" is accurate; the Token Service row at spec/13_security-model.md:278 had no kube-apiserver egress before Edit (i).
- FACT: pools are platform-global with `pool_tenant_access` join tables (spec/04_system-components.md:188); §15.1 tenant deletion blocks on "pools ... under the tenant" (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1259), which SPEC-2 (c) rule 2 binds to ownerTenantId.
- FACT: pinned pods (recycle or concurrent) are tenant-pinned for life (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454-469), so a pod-scoped lineage record cannot leak a T-owned lineage pool to another tenant's session. Do not re-file it.
- OPEN: a CRD Runtime apply that removes a private entry leaves an owned pool that rule 2 then makes un-updatable (every pool write either "sets it on any other pool" or "changes it"). This is a corner case and was not filed.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
- DECISION: filed SPEC-8 Edit (h) "reads the Secret at each such call" as a top-tier bottleneck — BECAUSE each private cache-served claim makes about three vcscred resolves (ref pinning at creation, binder Ensure/ValidateTree, FetchGitPack), each a ReadCredentialSecret per key (git-https: username and password), so about 6 apiserver GETs per claim, about 180/s at Tier 3's 30 claims/s, against 4 Token Service replicas (spec/17:1246) whose clientset uses client-go defaults (QPS 5, burst 10; cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go:84-88), about 20/s — ALTERNATIVES: code-only QPS raise (leaves the apiserver as a hard per-operation dependency); not filing (earlier rejection assumed an informer and QPS 100).
- CORRECTS [review-log.md:927 FACT]: the Token Service has no Secret informer (review-log.md:735) and SPEC-8 Edit (h) replaces the informer sentence with a per-call `get`, so a private-key FetchGitPack is not "an mTLS RPC to an in-memory lookup"; it is one apiserver GET per key per call.
- CORRECTS [review-log.md:834 ALTERNATIVES]: "2-3/s per replica against QPS 100" is not the binding limit; the reads land on the Token Service (4 replicas at Tier 3), whose client runs at the client-go default QPS 5.
- UNVERIFIED: CODE-3 rule M4 puts multi-minute warm-fill packs and latency-sensitive session-materialization and restore packs on one per-replica limiter (8 slots) with no priority, so a warm-pool refill burst can queue claim-path packs up to maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds. The non-spec loop should check this against the session-start SLO; the fix is code only.
- WATCHOUT: the git-host-outage and per-call ls-remote findings were refuted in earlier rounds (review-log-archive.md:555, :566). Do not re-file them. The Redis lineage fail-closed case is an accepted edge case (spec-changes.md:33).

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
- CORRECTS [spec.4.review-performance.1 "No durable state"]: the staged spec now adds one Redis write, `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:git_lineage` (SPEC-3 rule 7, SPEC-10). FetchGitPack still needs no fencing, but the lineage record is shared mutable state written by several replicas. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-10 Edit (a).
- WATCHOUT: the lineage record is keyed by pool name, not by session, so two sessions with the same lineage pool share one member. A write that replaces the member's score with an earlier expiry (a 300s resume bound after a longer finalize-bounded restore, or replica clock skew) cuts the first session's window short. The member score has to be the later of the old and new expiry (ZADD GT). EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-10 Edit (a) "each scored by its expiry time"; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:243 (300s), spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:255 (600s).
- FACT: a failed resume returns to `resume_pending` while retries remain, so the §12.4 row's "next resume attempt" exists for resumes. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:243.
- FACT: the Token Service Role grants only `get` on Secrets (spec/04_system-components.md:1682-1693), so the existing informer sentence at :2186 was already inconsistent; SPEC-8 Edit (h) resolves that rather than introducing a regression.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
- DECISION: filed no security findings on the staged spec edits — BECAUSE every bound I traced has a trusted, fail-closed source: FetchGitPack authorizes from the token-bound podId and the pod's pool Runtime; the lineage substitution reads a gateway-written Redis record whose outage fails the fetch (SPEC-10 Edit (c)); rule 7 re-checks the lineage pool's ownerTenantId against the session tenant at each bind; private rule 4 re-resolves the owning tenant's credential before every mirror read; the claim rule and the tenant pin (spec/05:454, :469) keep a tenant-owned pool's pods within one tenant — ALTERNATIVES: (a) git-https long-lived password reaching the pod through the in-pod helper or direct-mode materializedConfig, rejected because anthropic_direct already delivers a long-lived key (spec/04:1754) and the §4.9 principle is already qualified; (b) the gateway holding the Secret value from ReadCredentialSecret against "Long-lived credentials live only in the Token Service" (spec/04:2168), rejected because the gateway already holds real upstream keys in its in-memory Token Service credential cache (spec/13:19) and proxy mode keeps keys in the gateway; (c) vcs.git-https.read resolving to a write-capable credential, rejected as an explicit owner-decided relaxation rather than a bypassed control.
- FACT: the §4.9 Token Service Role grants only `get` with Helm-populated resourceNames, so SPEC-8 Edit (h)'s "the only access the lenny-token-service-secrets Role grants" is accurate. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1682-1693
- WATCHOUT: lenny/vcs_token issuance gating ("only issued when sources[] contains a gitClone entry targeting a private repo", spec/26:121) is not restated by SPEC-8 Edit (b) rule 4; rule 4 governs host binding only. Do not read rule 4 as widening issuance. — EVIDENCE: spec/26_reference-runtime-catalog.md:121

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4 Edit (a) points its walk and resolution root at "the workspace tree that rule 5 or 6 operates on" / "beneath that tree", and widens both failure outcomes to "the capture, resume, or bind" — BECAUSE §14 rule 6 is the one home for where a restore lands (slot on resume, build tree at first bind, citing §7.1 **Derive restore semantics:**), and rule 6's own failure sentence names only its pack-size and unreachable-object causes, so the exception's outcome must stay explicit — ALTERNATIVES: "walking the slot or, at a first bind, the build tree" (a second copy of rule 6 that goes stale); "fails that rule's operation" plus a generic failure sentence in rule 6 (more text); "fails the restore" in the edge rows (an outcome term no rule defines).
CORRECTS [earlier decision to leave the commit-between-copies edge row at "fails the resume"]: a derive from such a snapshot fails at the first bind, so that row now says "fails the bind or resume", matching rows for Runtime-entry, §13.4-rejected, and different-store.

### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: The lineage record expiry (rule 7) is always later than the restoring call's deadline. For a resume, the gateway writes the record after the `resume_pending → resuming` write that starts the 300s watchdog, so write time + 300s falls after that deadline. For a first bind, the FinalizeWorkspace call is bounded by the `maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` watchdog, and 0109 says "every bind attempt mints its own", so each retry rewrites the record. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:678 (claimOrphanTimeout windows), spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:243, main:spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:90
- FACT: Rule 6 survives a crash at any step. It indexes the pack before it rewrites `lenny.storeCommit` (the rewrite is a renameat), so a retry on the same tree either fetches again harmlessly or sees the matching key and skips the fetch. Rule 5's packs and rule 6's config replacement are both renameat moves out of scratch. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:150,153; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 bullet (4)
- WATCHOUT: Repeated warm-fill failures (for example, a remote outage) recycle pods through the existing warm-up failure path, and the WarmPoolController has no replenish backoff for any error_type. That gap predates this proposal and is not a 0111 finding. EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:126,503
- FACT: The 0109 §7.1 **Derive restore semantics:** paragraph is on main (spec/07:90) but not in the draft-0111 tree. SPEC-3 Edit (e) quotes its text accurately.

### [spec.4.review-security.1]
- FACT: Every session-mode pod serves one tenant for life. Recycling and concurrent pools are tenant-pinned on every pool, `allowCrossTenantReuse` included, and a non-recycling pod serves one session and then retires. So an unexpired `git_lineage` entry left by tenant A's restore cannot be read through `FetchGitPack` by another tenant's session on that pod, even when the agent writes `lenny.gitStoreUrl` and `lenny.baseCommit` itself. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454, :469, :646
- FACT: The Token Service accepts only gateway-replica client certificates (mutual SAN validation), so a new `ReadCredentialSecret` that returns a Secret's value can be called only by the gateway. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:346
- FACT: Holding a raw long-lived credential in the gateway is already allowed: proxy mode keeps the real API key in gateway memory. Direct-mode delivery of a plaintext long-lived key to the pod is also already allowed (`anthropic_direct` `apiKey`, `azure_openai` `apiKey`) and is gated by `DirectModeStandardIsolationMultiTenantRejected`. So the `git-https` username and password in Edits (b2) and (d) regress no control. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:717-728, spec/04_system-components.md:1757, :1974
- DECISION: returned no findings for the security lens in spec round 4 — BECAUSE the diff since the last snapshot only widens the §13.4 exception and the edge rows to cover the bind, and the cross-tenant, credential, and lineage paths all hold against the tenant pin and the gateway-only Token Service — ALTERNATIVES: I left out tenant deletion leaving a private mirror in the gateway emptyDir until LRU eviction or restart, because no §12.8 text requires caches to be purged, so it is speculative.

### [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: OD-2 flips from option A (retain until capacity eviction or restart) to a per-replica purge. SPEC-2 (c) rule 4's last sentence becomes "each replica deletes its mirror of a private key once the key's owning tenant is not registered (§10.2), within the §12.9 erasure-on-deletion timeline for workspace files"; CODE-3 step 7 gains rule M8 (a 30 s timer sweep through `tenantRegistered`, exclusive lock without waiting, memo drop as M2, retry on a held lock or read error). The spec edge bullet "The owning tenant is deleted." is deleted. — BECAUSE §12.9 maps workspace files to T3/T4 and requires erasure within 72 h / immediately (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:1068, :1098, :1110); option A contradicted it, and a §12.9 exception would weaken a GDPR-aligned control (owner-only, fail-open). — ALTERNATIVES: §12.9 exception naming the mirror (owner-only compliance regression); §12.8 erasure-scope row (mirror implements no DeleteByTenant, needs replica fan-out); purge only before each M2 measure (review-log OD-2 option B: a quiet replica never measures, so still unbounded); idle-TTL eviction (unbounded relative to deletion, never "immediate" for T4); a Phase 4 fan-out to replicas (no surface).
CORRECTS [the earlier DECISION rejecting "purge on every replica (no fan-out surface, new mechanism)"]: a purge needs no fan-out. Each replica reads tenant registration itself through the existing `tenantRegistered` reading (cmd/lenny-gateway/auth_seams.go, `tenantstore.Tenant.Registered`: missing, deleting, deleted -> false), the same one the §10.2 bearer chain uses.
CORRECTS [review-log FACT "§12.8 makes no completeness claim ... does not contradict the spec"]: the contradiction is with §12.9's per-tier erasure-on-deletion row, which no earlier round read.
FACT: §10.2 defines "not registered" as `TenantState` `deleting` or `deleted` (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:243); the §12.8 tenant-deletion controller runs one phase per 30 s pass (pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/runnable.go:17), so a 30 s M8 sweep keyed on `deleting` normally purges before Phase 4 runs.
WATCHOUT: T4's "Immediate" is met only to the sweep cadence; do not shorten the spec sentence to name a value or re-add a §12.8 erasure-table citation, and do not add a refuse-on-unregistered check to rule 4 (re-creation needs a pool owned by the tenant, which the §15.1 409 dependents gate removes first, and the next pass purges anyway). — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:1098
WATCHOUT: non-spec CODE-3 step 3 says "only rule M2's capacity deletion takes the lock exclusively, and it never waits for it"; M8 must also not wait, and that sentence must name M8. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:78
UNVERIFIED: whether every gateway configuration wires a tenant store for `tenantRegistered`; with none wired the only owner is `default`, which a single-tenant deployment cannot delete (§12.8). The non-spec loop should confirm M8's wiring in cmd/lenny-gateway.

### [spec.6.review-security.1]
FACT: The lineage pool cannot carry one tenant's private mirror key to a pod of another tenant. The tenant pin lasts for the pod's whole life on every pool, and a recycled pod never takes `{tenant_id} → unassigned`. So the pod-keyed `git_lineage` record is always read by the pinned tenant's sessions. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:459, :469
FACT: The SPEC-8 Edit (d) direct-mode `git-https` password, a long-lived plaintext value, follows an existing precedent: `anthropic_direct` and `azure_openai` already deliver long-lived keys in direct mode, under the direct+runc multi-tenant admission block and the revocation caveat. It is not a regression of the "short-lived leases" principle. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1634, :1754, :1974, :2170
FACT: Tenant IDs are never reused, because the tombstone prevents it. §15.1 blocks tenant deletion while pools exist under the tenant, so a tenant-owned pool is deleted before its tenant is. The retained mirror objects of a deleted tenant therefore cannot be served to a new tenant with the same id. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:926 (tombstone), spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1259
UNVERIFIED: whether §12.9 T4 "workspace files" covers private-repository objects that the shared gateway mirror retains. A T4 tenant's tenant-owned private pool would leave plaintext repository objects on non-dedicated gateway nodes beyond the session and the 4h deletion SLA. I did not file this because §12.9 classifies workspace files and snapshots, and the existing cold gitClone path also handles repository content on the gateway with no T4 statement. A human or the compliance lens should decide. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:1086, spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:411

### [spec.8.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: Recycling and concurrent pods are tenant-pinned for life, across recycle, so an unexpired restore lineage record (rule 7, "Expiry is the only removal") cannot widen FetchGitPack for another tenant's later session on the same pod. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454, :459
- FACT: §6.2 **`resuming` timeout (300s):** is a watchdog that starts when the session enters `resuming`. Rule 7 writes the record later, with its expiry at the call's bound, so the record never expires before the resume does. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:243
- FACT: "Not registered" is defined. A tenant whose TenantState is `deleting` or `deleted` is not registered, and Phase 3 sets `deleting` first, so the rule 4 mirror purge trigger is level-triggered and reachable. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:243, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:924
- FACT: 0109 derive restore says "every bind attempt mints its own", so the §12.4 lineage row's "next attempt" exists for a derive's first bind as well as for a resume. EVIDENCE: main:spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:90
- DECISION: no spec-loop finding under reliability in round 8 — BECAUSE the lineage record (write before restore call, Redis fail-closed and rebuilt per attempt), warm-fill failure, the rule 4 purge, and the Edit (h) poll-style resync all hold under crash, restart, and failover — ALTERNATIVES: three were rejected. Filing warm-fill churn on a remote outage was rejected because it matches the existing setup_command_failed and WarmPoolReplenishmentFailing behaviour. Filing mirror stale `.lock` files after a gateway crash was rejected as code-only (CODE-3 M-rules, emptyDir survives a container restart), so the non-spec loop should check it. Filing a FetchGitPack drain cut was rejected because §10.1.7 stage 3 drains active streams.
- UNVERIFIED: CODE-3 step 3 does not say that a git `.lock` file left in a mirror by a killed fetch is cleared, and an emptyDir mirror survives a gateway container restart. Non-spec reliability reviewer should check whether the mirror recovers (spec loop out of scope).

### [spec.8.review-security.1]
FACT: Rule 4's new purge trigger ("owning tenant is not registered", §10.2) fires at tenant deletion Phase 3, because §10.2 defines a `deleting` or `deleted` tenant as not registered. A §15.1 tenant delete is already refused while pools or credential pools exist, so by then rule 4's credential resolution already refuses every operation on the key. The purge only removes residual bytes and does not gate access. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:243, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:924, spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1259
WATCHOUT: Do not file the rule 4 purge for (a) legal holds, since §12.8 Phase 3.5 holds cover sessions, artifacts, audit ranges, and workspace snapshots and the mirror is not among them; (b) the absence of a §12.8 erasure-scope table row, since that table lists store interfaces with DeleteByTenant and the mirror is a per-replica cache with its own §12.9-bounded purge; or (c) the M8 behavior of keeping a key when the registration read fails, since resolution already refuses service and that behavior is non-spec. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:821-844,932
