### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: FetchGitPack's pod binding (CODE-3 step 6) is keyed on the configured `--sa-token-audience`. With an audience set, a token yielding no pod name or a mismatched `pod_id` is PERMISSION_DENIED; with none set, the handler takes `pod_id` as given, like every other GatewayControl handler — BECAUSE the empty-audience posture already admits every GatewayControl call with no token (satoken.go RequireSATokenInterceptor), and a fail-closed handler there would refuse every warm fill on Kind, tier 4, and compose — ALTERNATIVES: refuse all calls with no audience and set the audience on Kind (cannot work, see WATCHOUT); refuse only private entries (exception clause, breaks item 21 private-pool rows on Kind); SPIFFE binding (no per-pod adapter cert, F-10.3.10; spec-loop change); chart gate on gitRepository registration (entries arrive via admin API/CRDs, chart cannot see them); adding the §10.3 required guard on global.saTokenAudience here (breaks every install while the adapter sends no token).
WATCHOUT: Do not set `global.saTokenAudience` in tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml or give tier 4 an audience to "exercise" the binding. The adapter never attaches the SA token (`gatewaycontrol.WithSAToken` has no non-test caller; `ConnectGateway` dials with TLSClientOption only), and Kind runs mtls.enabled=false where a RequireTransportSecurity=true credential cannot be sent, so every GatewayControl call would fail token_missing. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go ConnectGateway; pkg/adapter/gatewaycontrol/satoken.go:64; tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:49-50
WATCHOUT: Do not write the binding as "deny when a pod name was found and differs". That silently skips the check when VerifyPod returns no extra; the TEST-1 item 13 missing-extra and missing-claim rows are the discriminators for the audience-keyed branch. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 6
FACT: Chart default `global.saTokenAudience: ""` contradicts §10.3 (required, no default); the controller mounts no projected token and the gateway builds no TokenReview verifier when it is empty. BUILD-GAPS F-10.2.10 claims the adapter half (a) is present, which the tree refutes. Filed in the summary defects list for REC-1. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:2056; pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sa_token.go:31-36; cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:201-212; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:364; BUILD-GAPS.md:14844
FACT: The gateway flag is `--sa-token-audience` (env LENNY_SA_TOKEN_AUDIENCE). — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:968
MISTAKE: Earlier text had TEST-1 item 21 assert the pod binding "on Kind's TokenReview"; Kind has no audience and no TokenReview verifier, so that row could never pass. Moved to an envtest VerifyPod row in item 13.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: warm fills are capped by a nested warm share W = max(1, maxConcurrentPacks − 2) inside M4 (third per-replica subsystem.Limiter, taken by a ref-want Pack immediately before its M4 slot, released with it); M4 is W's one home and M6, INFRA-1 (g), and TEST-1 item 20 cite it — BECAUSE subsystem.Limiter is a bare channel semaphore with no FIFO or priority, so only capping warm holders guarantees commit-SHA Packs and walks a slot, and nesting leaves M7's sum, the chart values, and summary open question 4 unchanged — ALTERNATIVES: separate maxConcurrentWarmPacks chart value (new knob, M7 term, chart-test row); priority-ordered limiter (shared type change, and holders still block for minutes); fixed reserve with no floor (pack limit <= 2 starves warm fills and breaks the M4/M5 rows at limits 1 and 2).
WATCHOUT: the warm-share slot must be taken BEFORE the M4 slot; a warm Pack holding an M4 slot while it waits for the warm share recreates the starvation. The TEST-1 item 20 M4 warm-share sentence discriminates. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 7 rule M4
FACT: subsystem.Limiter.Acquire is a buffered-channel select (fast path, then a slow path on slots or ctx.Done) with no ordering or priority among waiters — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/core/subsystem/limiter.go:61-89
DECISION: FetchGitPack calls run under a context the gateway-control step of finalShutdownSteps cancels before GracefulStop, beside inputWaits.Close; the cancellation maps to UNAVAILABLE through the CODE-3 step 6 table row, and M6 lost its DEADLINE_EXCEEDED clause so the table is the one status home — BECAUSE GracefulStop waits for every handler with no deadline and all final closers share one shutdown-timeout context, so a live stream starves the postgres, sqlite, redis, and openfeature closes until SIGKILL — ALTERNATIVES: GracefulStop bounded by ctx with a Stop() fallback (consumes the shared closer budget and can cut request_input exit writes); Mirror.Close (misses handlers in auth, registry, lineage, or credential reads); relying on the preStop drain (lenny_gateway_active_streams counts event-bus subscribers rather than GatewayControl streams).
FACT: the gateway-control close step is `inputWaits.Close()` then `gatewayCtrlSrv.GracefulStop()`, ignoring its ctx; closers run in order trace, llm-proxy, gateway-control, postgres, sqlite, redis, openfeature on one shutdown-timeout context — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:67-78, :163-185
WATCHOUT: the shutdown test lives in cmd/lenny-gateway (TEST-1 item 13), because tier 4 does not run finalShutdownSteps; do not move it to item 16. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 13
DECISION: M8 production wiring is pinned by a two-sided tier-4 sentence in TEST-1 item 32 (mirror survives two passes while acme is registered, gone within two passes after pool then tenant deletion), with no nil-OwnerRegistered rule — BECAUSE the two-sided assertion fails on every wrong wiring (nil skip, nil delete-all, constant true), so a nil rule changes nothing built — ALTERNATIVES: a "public mirror of the same url stays" assertion (item 29's fixture is Basic-auth only, so no public mirror exists; item 5 already pins other keys); citing 12.8 (staged rule 4 cites §12.9).
WATCHOUT: item 32 deletes acme's pool before acme, because CODE-8 step 4 refuses tenant deletion with 409 while an owned pool exists. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-8 step 4

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
- DECISION: the fill deadline is rendered as chart `controller.tuning.gitStoreFillTimeout` → lenny-controller `--git-store-fill-timeout` (default 15m, non-positive refused at startup) → `sandbox.Reconciler`/`podspec.Inputs.GitStoreFillTimeout` → sidecar.go adapterArgs `--git-store-fill-timeout` — BECAUSE [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30] requires a controller-wide chart value and the supervise timing path is the existing precedent in every layer — ALTERNATIVES: top-level `controller.gitStoreFillTimeout` (breaks the `controller.tuning.*` convention); rendering on embedded pods (two fixture binaries to edit, no deployer gain); an env var (second config surface); fixed 15m (violates the decision and code-best-practices); a Runtime/pool field (excluded by summary decision 13).

FACT: `guardTokenServiceStub` (tests/tier4_integration/token_service_unavailability_guard_test.go:71-90) is the only `tokensv1.TokenServiceClient` implementer that lists its methods one by one without embedding the interface. DECISION: the Token Service outage narrative is closed by one DOCS-1 bullet covering four pages: the runbook (opening paragraph and Trigger), the security.md **Failure Handling** table, the troubleshooting.md **Token Service Unavailability** symptoms, and the architecture.md **High availability** paragraph. The pages are also added to the Files-touched docs line and the summary's DOCS-1 index line. No spec edge-list row was added — BECAUSE the spec-changes edge-list preamble requires each case to cite an owning rule, and no staged spec rule owns outage behavior — ALTERNATIVES: an edge row now (no owning rule), reverting the gateway SecretReader to connectorsecret (reverses Edit (h)), a gateway credential cache with a grace period (defeats Decision 4), and editing rules.go now (diverges from §16.5) were all rejected.
WATCHOUT: tier-11 `TestTokenServiceOutageRunbookAttributesToFinalize` (tests/tier11_docs/session_creation_phases_test.go:83) requires the runbook to keep `finalize` and CREDENTIAL_MATERIALIZATION_ERROR and to make no "New sessions return" attribution. A DOCS-1 rewrite of the runbook's opening paragraph must keep that sentence. EVIDENCE: docs/runbooks/token-service-outage.md:32.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: SPEC-8 Edits (b2) and (h) and CODE-7 step 3 make the gateway read every VCS credential through the Token Service at each git operation, with no cache. The following spec sites are now false:
- spec/04_system-components.md §4.3: ":224 Sessions that already hold credential leases continue operating", ":226 Sessions that do not need LLM credentials ... are unaffected", and ":228 Token Service unavailability does not affect already-leased credentials until the lease expires".
- spec/16_observability.md:430 `TokenServiceUnavailable` "(existing sessions unaffected until lease expiry)".
- spec/17_deployment-topology.md:826 Trigger parenthetical and :828 Remediation (1) "Existing sessions continue".
What is true instead: during an outage, authenticated gitClone fails at session creation, the in-pod credential helper (`lenny/vcs_token`) fails in running sessions with no grace period, and private repository stores fail to fill, materialize, and restore. The remedy is a SPEC-8 §4.3 edit as the home, §16.5 and §17.7 citing it, and a spec edge-list row citing that home.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, after the spec edit above]: pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go `TokenServiceUnavailable` Description and the generated docs/alerting/rules.yaml:117 ("Existing sessions are unaffected until lease expiry.") follow §16.5. They need a CODE follow-on once §16.5 changes. Do not edit them before the spec does.
OPEN: CODE-7 step 7 does not say whether the gateway's `ReadCredentialSecret` client goes through the `token_service` subsystem breaker, or which code session creation returns for an authenticated gitClone during an outage (a wrapped Unavailable surfaces through the existing auth/ref-resolution mappings). The DOCS-1 bullet deliberately names no client error code until this is settled.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 resolves the lineage pool through one sessionserver helper `lineagePool(ctx, row)` and gives `ResumeRequest`, `BindRequest`, and `SlotBindRequest` the pair `LineagePool`/`LineageTTL` (`resumingCap()` on resume, `launchBound()` on the bind requests); one Grant rule covers `Binder.Resume`, `Binder.Prepare`, and `materializeSlotStages` — BECAUSE a derived or replayed session's first bind builds BindRequest/SlotBindRequest in bind_request.go and never passes resume_rebind.go, so the resume-only Grant never fired before FinalizeWorkspace — ALTERNATIVES: lazy closure like RestoreChunks (second pattern), Grant inside workspaceRestore's closure (hides a Redis write in a mint closure), resolution inside the Binder (no manifest/session/pool stores there), gating on first bind or non-nil RestoreChunks (rule 7 reads the pool at each bind).

FACT: `SlotBindRequest` is declared in slotbinder.go (main:podsession/slotbinder.go:26), and `BindRequest` in bindrequest.go:17; `launchBound()` is main:pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:305 (maxFinalizing or the watchdog default).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: the adapter never attaches the projected SA token. `gatewaycontrol.WithSAToken` and `NewSATokenCredentials` have no caller outside their own file and test, and `Server.ConnectGateway` dials with only `TLSClientOption`. So with `global.saTokenAudience` set, `RequireSATokenInterceptor` refuses every GatewayControl call with `token_missing`. BUILD-GAPS F-10.2.10's resolution says the adapter attaches the token on every call, and that claim is false. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go:36-58; pkg/adapter/gatewaycontrol/satoken.go:60-68; `grep -rn WithSAToken --include=*.go .`; BUILD-GAPS.md:14853-14857
FACT: Kind e2e runs `mtls.enabled: false` (plaintext GatewayControl), and `SATokenCredentials.RequireTransportSecurity()` returns true, so the token could not travel on Kind even with the adapter wired. Setting an audience in e2e-values cannot work today. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:36-50; pkg/adapter/gatewaycontrol/satoken.go:58
FACT: §10.3 makes `global.saTokenAudience` a required chart value with no default. The chart defaults it to "" and its comment says the gateway then applies no audience check. This is pre-existing chart drift. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:364; charts/lenny/values.yaml:2043-2056
DECISION: the CODE-3 step 6 pod binding applies only when an SA-token audience is configured, and the branch keys on the configured audience. With no audience, the handler takes `pod_id` as given, as every other GatewayControl handler does. With an audience, a token that yields no pod name is refused. Kind, tier 4, and compose need no harness change. The real-apiserver check of the pod-name extra moves from TEST-1 item 21 (Kind) to an envtest row in item 13. — BECAUSE the empty-audience posture is outside the §10.3 contract, and in that posture RequireSATokenInterceptor and RequireVerifiedPeerInterceptor already admit every call, so SPEC-5's "same checks as every other GatewayControl call" holds. Failing closed instead would make warm fill work in no configuration the tree can run: an empty audience refuses everything, and a set audience gets token_missing from the adapter defect above. That would also make tiers 5 and 9 (items 17-19, 21, and the private rows) impossible — ALTERNATIVES: refuse every call when no audience is set (the reviewer's suggestion; it needs the adapter token wiring plus Kind mTLS, which F-10.3.10 blocks); refuse only private entries when no audience is set (Kind's tier-9 private rows warm with no audience, and it adds an exception clause); bind to the SPIFFE pod segment of the peer certificate (there are no per-pod adapter certs, Kind is plaintext, the staged spec text names the SA token, and summary defects bullet 1 already scopes it out); chart-side "require an audience when a gitRepository entry is registered" (entries come from the admin API and CRDs, which the chart never sees)
WATCHOUT: the trap is `if podName != "" && podName != req.PodId { deny }`. That skips the binding whenever VerifyPod finds no extra. The handler must branch on the configured audience. Item 13's rows for a missing pod-name extra and a missing pod claim are the discriminators. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 6
OPEN: with design (b), FetchGitPack's cross-tenant private-store protection (summary decision 14) holds in no install the tree can run until the chart enforces §10.3's required audience and ConnectGateway applies WithSAToken over mTLS. The owner should decide whether 0111's private-repository support waits on that §10.3 work or ships with the defect recorded. The design records the defect in the summary's defects list.
UNVERIFIED: whether envtest's bundled kube-apiserver fills `authentication.kubernetes.io/pod-name` in a TokenReview of a pod-bound TokenRequest token. The new item 13 envtest row is what answers it. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/envtest; tests/tier3_contract/rest_sessions uses envtest at tier 3

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: warm fills get a nested warm-share limiter inside M4 (a ref-want Pack takes a slot of a third subsystem.Limiter sized W = max(1, maxConcurrentPacks - 2) immediately before its M4 slot and releases both together); M6's formula and INFRA-1 (g) cite W — BECAUSE subsystem.Limiter is a bare counting semaphore with no FIFO or priority, so only a cap on warm holders guarantees free slots for commit-SHA packs and binder walks, and nesting keeps M7's memory sum (total <= maxConcurrentPacks) unchanged — ALTERNATIVES: a separate warm limiter with its own chart value (new knob, M7 gains a term, chart test grows); a priority-ordered limiter (a SHA waiter still waits minutes behind warm holders); a fixed reserve with no floor (W = 0 at maxConcurrentPacks <= 2 starves warm fills and breaks the TEST-1 M4/M5 rows at pack limits 1 and 2).
FACT: subsystem.Limiter.Acquire is a select on a buffered channel; no FIFO, no priority. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/core/subsystem/limiter.go:61-89
DECISION: FetchGitPack shutdown — the handler runs under a context the gateway-control step of finalShutdownSteps cancels before GracefulStop (the inputWaits.Close precedent); the step 6 status table's UNAVAILABLE row is the one home of the resulting status, and M6 drops its own DEADLINE_EXCEEDED clause (the table's row already states it) — BECAUSE GracefulStop takes no deadline and the closers share one shutdown-timeout context, so the postgres, sqlite and redis steps behind it never run when a stream outlives SIGTERM — ALTERNATIVES: GracefulStop bounded by the step context with a Stop() fallback (consumes the shared closers' context, so the sqlite final snapshot runs on an expired context, and it cuts request_input exit writes); a Mirror.Close (cancels packs but not handlers still waiting in auth or registry reads).
CORRECTS [review-log.md:1262 FACT]: the preStop drain does not cover FetchGitPack. lenny_gateway_active_streams is set from bus.ActiveSubscribers() (client event-bus subscribers), not GatewayControl streams, and a stream past the grace period is SIGKILLed with the later close steps skipped. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/metrics_exporters.go:103; cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:170-185
DECISION: M8 gets no nil-OwnerRegistered rule; TEST-1 item 32 gains a two-sided tier-4 assertion (acme's mirror survives two passes while acme is registered, and is gone within two passes after its pool and acme are deleted) — BECAUSE the two halves fail on every wrong wiring (nil that skips, nil that panics, nil that deletes everything, a constant true), so a nil rule would add text without changing what an implementor builds — ALTERNATIVES: "a nil OwnerRegistered reads every owner as unregistered" (one sentence; redundant given the two-sided row); a public mirror of the same url staying (impossible on item 29's fixture, which requires Basic auth, and item 5 already pins other keys staying).
WATCHOUT: staged rule 4 cites §12.9 Data Classification for the erasure timeline, so item 32's spec line gains 12.9 (not 12.8). — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md rule 4 (Mirror key and credential), last sentence

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: The GatewayControl SA-token interceptor has three modes. It passes calls through when the audience is empty, verifies through TokenReview whenever an audience is set in-cluster (pod_lifecycle.go builds the verifier from the same flag), and decodes the payload without checking the signature only when an audience is set and no verifier exists. The CODE-3 step 6 unverified pod-claim path is therefore reachable only in dev, and an in-cluster deployment always binds podId to a TokenReview. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/satoken.go:47-86, cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:200-212
FACT: tenantRegistered treats a tenant in `deleting` or `deleted` as unregistered, so the CODE-3 rule M8 purge starts when deletion begins. CODE-8 step 4's 409 means the owning pool is already gone by then. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/auth_seams.go:61-81
FACT: Only platform-admin creates pools (§15.1 POST /v1/admin/pools). A tenant-admin may update a granted pool, but §6.4 rule 2 forbids changing ownerTenantId after creation, so a tenant-admin cannot take ownership of a pool. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:797,820; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:289
FACT: The tenant pin persists for a pod's life on every pool where reuse is possible, so a pod-keyed REG-GITLINEAGE record never outlives into another tenant's session. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454,459,469
FACT: All ResolvePool callers in the tree match the CODE-8 step 3 list. The list has 10 call sites, including coordination_seams.go. EVIDENCE: grep "ResolvePool(" over pkg and cmd
WATCHOUT: tokenServiceRBACPatch hard-codes `-n lenny-system`, while the CODE-7 step 8 Role renders in the release namespace. This is harmless when the release namespace is lenny-system. Without a Kind check, a non-default namespace makes item 35's patch target the wrong Role. This is not a security finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:441-446

### [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]
FACT: on the concurrent-workspace `/start` path a stageWorkspace `*upload.ValidationError` carries no gRPC status, so `SlotBindError.Reason()` is transient, `classifySlotBindFailure` returns the error unchanged, and `writePodClaimError` reaches the archiveLimit case. The slotFailed case sits BEFORE archiveLimit, so any wrapping that turns the refusal into a non-retryable SlotFailedError would answer SLOT_FAILED instead. No existing test pins `/start` + ValidationError; only TestFinalizeRejectsOverLimitArchiveAsNonRetryable_spec_13_4 covers the finalize path. ### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
- DECISION: the CODE-3 step 6 gateway-control step runs `GracefulStop` in a goroutine and calls `Stop` if it has not returned 5 s after the per-call cancellation; TEST-1 item 13 asserts the step returns within 6 s with the consumer still not reading — BECAUSE a returned handler leaves its trailer queued behind unsent data when the peer stops reading (grpc v1.80.0 internal/transport/controlbuf.go:696-702 Case 1.B enqueues it; deleteStream runs only from the trailer's onWrite), and stop(graceful) waits on `len(s.conns) != 0` (server.go:1973) — ALTERNATIVES: relying on handler return (refuted); a keepalive/MaxConnectionAge bound (adds a second GOAWAY source, which the shutdown.go comment rules out).
- WATCHOUT: `Server.Stop` does not wait for handlers unless WaitForHandlers is set, so a handler still running when the 5 s fallback fires could race the postgres step's pool close. Input-wait handlers end on `inputWaits.Close` and FetchGitPack handlers return on cancellation, so both normally return well before the fallback. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:178-184.
- FACT: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go gateway-control step calls `inputWaits.Close()` then an unbounded `GracefulStop()`, ignoring its ctx. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:178-184.
- DECISION: spec-changes.md SPEC-8 Edit (j) now also edits the §4.3 "Sessions that already hold credential leases **continue operating**" bullet (spec/04_system-components.md:224) to point at the inserted VCS bullet — BECAUSE otherwise the §4.3 list contradicts itself and DOCS-1's architecture.md rewrite disagrees with its source. This reopens the converged spec edits by one replacement. Checklist S8 and summary SPEC-8 file-list pointers changed to "outage bullets".

### [non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]
FACT: in grpc-go v1.80.0 a server stream's write-quota wait selects only on the stream's own ctx.Done(), and s.cancel() runs only in the finish path after the handler returns, so cancelling a handler-derived child context never releases a blocked Send — EVIDENCE: go.mod:64; grpc@v1.80.0/internal/transport/http2_server.go:609-610, :1324, :1348; flowcontrol.go:55-66
DECISION: CODE-3 step 6 handler sends from one goroutine and waits for each Send or the shutdown-cancelled context, returning UNAVAILABLE on cancellation without joining the goroutine — BECAUSE only the handler's return ends the stream, so GracefulStop otherwise waits up to the 15m fill deadline, past the 240 s grace period — ALTERNATIVES: GracefulStop with a Stop() fallback (kills request_input exit writes, changes shared shutdown); buffered channel or io.Pipe (a second 1 MiB buffer per slot breaks rule M7's P_slot accounting); dropping the cancellation (SIGKILL skips postgres and sqlite closes)
WATCHOUT: do not make the FetchGitPack handler join its sender goroutine before returning; the goroutine unblocks only after the handler returns, so a join reintroduces the hang — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 6 **Handler.**
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 13's shutdown case discriminates only with a pack larger than the 64 KiB write quota plus the client window (hence "at least 4 MiB" and a consumer that stops after the header); a small pack never blocks Send and passes against the broken design — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 13
DECISION: TEST-1 item 20 (M6) asserts every mirror limiter's in-flight count, including the warm share's for a ref-want Pack timing out on M4 — BECAUSE rule M6's "holds no slot" already requires it and one case per rule keeps it in M6 — ALTERNATIVES: a separate warm-share row under (M4) (release on timeout is M6 behavior)
OPEN: whether rule M6's text should name the warm share as a limiter a caller waits on (it currently names M3 and M4 slots only); the warm-share slot is taken immediately before the M4 slot, so a Pack can wait on it when W are held.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
FACT: In grpc-go v1.80.0 a server-stream Send blocked on write quota waits only on the stream's own context: the transport passes `s.ctx.Done()` to `wq.init`, `writeQuota.get` selects on that channel alone, and `s.cancel()` runs in the stream-finish path after the handler returns. Cancelling a context the handler derives never unblocks the Send. GracefulStop sends GOAWAY and waits; it cancels no stream context. — EVIDENCE: go.mod:64; grpc@v1.80.0/internal/transport/http2_server.go:609-610, :1161, :1324, :1348; flowcontrol.go:60-65; cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:178-184
FACT: The gateway's terminationGracePeriodSeconds is 240, below the adapter's 15m fill deadline, so an unbounded GracefulStop on a stalled FetchGitPack ends in SIGKILL and skips the postgres and sqlite shutdown steps. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:2595
DECISION: CODE-3 step 6 gains one sentence: the handler calls `Send` from one goroutine it starts, waits for each `Send` or for the shutdown-cancelled context, and on cancellation returns `UNAVAILABLE` without waiting for that goroutine — BECAUSE only the handler's return ends the stream, and waiting for each Send keeps one chunk buffer per slot so M7 is unchanged — ALTERNATIVES: GracefulStop with a timer falling back to Stop() (rejected: Stop cuts every GatewayControl handler, including the input-wait exit writes the shutdown step exists to let finish); dropping the shutdown cancellation (rejected: GracefulStop then hangs to SIGKILL); a buffered/pipe handoff without waiting for each Send (rejected: adds a second chunk buffer per slot against M7).
WATCHOUT: The item 13 shutdown test passes on the broken design if the stalled stream's pack fits the 64 KiB write quota and the client window, because Send then never blocks. The test's pack must span several 1 MiB chunks and the client must stop reading after the header. — EVIDENCE: grpc@v1.80.0/internal/transport/flowcontrol.go:56-60 (first get always succeeds while quota > 0)
WATCHOUT: A sender goroutine still inside SendMsg after the handler returns is safe in v1.80.0: its Write returns errStreamDone once the stream finishes and its deferred WriteStatus is a no-op on a done stream. Do not "fix" this by joining the goroutine before return; that reintroduces the hang. — EVIDENCE: grpc@v1.80.0/stream.go:1710-1734
OPEN: Rule M6 says a caller waits for "an M3 or M4 slot", but a ref-want Pack also waits for a warm-share slot (rule M4). "the caller holds no slot" still covers release, so the new (M6) test case holds; whether M6 should name the warm share as a waited-for limiter is a separate wording finding for a later round.


### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
- DECISION: the CODE-3 step 6 gateway-control step runs `GracefulStop` in a goroutine and calls `Stop` if it has not returned 5 s after the per-call cancellation; TEST-1 item 13 asserts the step returns within 6 s with the consumer still not reading — BECAUSE a returned handler leaves its trailer queued behind unsent data when the peer stops reading (grpc v1.80.0 internal/transport/controlbuf.go:696-702 Case 1.B enqueues it; deleteStream runs only from the trailer's onWrite), and stop(graceful) waits on `len(s.conns) != 0` (server.go:1973) — ALTERNATIVES: relying on handler return (refuted); a keepalive/MaxConnectionAge bound (adds a second GOAWAY source, which the shutdown.go comment rules out).
- WATCHOUT: `Server.Stop` does not wait for handlers unless WaitForHandlers is set, so a handler still running when the 5 s fallback fires could race the postgres step's pool close. Input-wait handlers end on `inputWaits.Close` and FetchGitPack handlers return on cancellation, so both normally return well before the fallback. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:178-184.
- FACT: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go gateway-control step calls `inputWaits.Close()` then an unbounded `GracefulStop()`, ignoring its ctx. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:178-184.
- DECISION: spec-changes.md SPEC-8 Edit (j) now also edits the §4.3 "Sessions that already hold credential leases **continue operating**" bullet (spec/04_system-components.md:224) to point at the inserted VCS bullet — BECAUSE otherwise the §4.3 list contradicts itself and DOCS-1's architecture.md rewrite disagrees with its source. This reopens the converged spec edits by one replacement. Checklist S8 and summary SPEC-8 file-list pointers changed to "outage bullets".

### [non-spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: grpc-go v1.80.0 server-stream SendMsg blocks on the stream's write quota, which waits only on the stream's own context (`s.wq.init(defaultWriteQuota, s.ctxDone)`). A context the handler derives and cancels does not unblock it. Returning from the handler does, because finishStream/closeStream call `s.cancel()`. EVIDENCE: ~/go/pkg/mod/google.golang.org/grpc@v1.80.0/internal/transport/http2_server.go:609-610,1161,1324,1348; flowcontrol.go:54-66
WATCHOUT: CODE-3 step 6's shutdown cancellation (added in non-spec round 1) ends a handler waiting for a slot. It does not end one blocked in Send on a stalled consumer unless the send loop runs off the handler goroutine. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:84, :286
FACT: The controller's CH-SUPERVISE timing flags are rendered by supervisedAdapterArgs (supervisor.go:249) only on supervised pods. sidecar.go:50-73 and embedded.go:38-54 build their own adapter arg lists, so the CODE-4 flag belongs in sidecar.go alone, as the proposal says. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/sidecar.go:50, embedded.go:38
FACT: Every fake TokenService server in credassign tests embeds UnimplementedTokenServiceServer. The only client-side stub that needs ReadCredentialSecret is guardTokenServiceStub. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client_breaker_test.go:32; tests/tier4_integration/token_service_unavailability_guard_test.go:71

### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: The CODE-3 step 6 shutdown cancellation runs in the "gateway-control" entry of finalShutdownSteps, which executes after SIGTERM and therefore after the §10.1.7 preStop drain window. In-flight FetchGitPack streams keep serving through preStop, and only streams still running at the final step are cancelled. A rollout-churn finding that ignores preStop is wrong. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:155-185; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:177
FACT: The warm share W = max(1, maxConcurrentPacks - 2) is acquired before the M4 slot, and no caller takes the two in the reverse order, so the limiters cannot deadlock. At the default 8 (W = 6), 2 M4 slots stay free for commit-SHA Packs and walks. M7 memory is unchanged because W sits inside M4. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:100, :103
FACT: launchBound() (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:305) and resumingCap() (resume_driver.go:220) exist. The Prepare grant (bindprepare.go:158) and the slot-bind grant (slotbinder.go:376) sit immediately before FinalizeWorkspace. The grant's expiry, now plus the bound, therefore outlasts the launch context. Redis load is one EVAL per derived or replayed bind, which is negligible at any tier.

### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: /finalize runs Binder.Prepare (and so FinalizeWorkspace and any restore FetchGitPack) under r.Context() with no launchBound deadline; only the slot paths use launchContext. A CODE-9 grant of now+launchBound() taken after the row enters finalizing still outlasts the finalizing watchdog bound, so the lineage record cannot expire under a live first bind. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize_handler.go (prepareAtFinalize(r.Context(), ...)), pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:300-310
FACT: the gateway-control shutdown step is input-wait Close then GracefulStop, with no deadline; CODE-3 step 6's handler-context cancel slots in beside the Close. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:178-185
DECISION: no reliability findings on the round-2 diff (shutdown cancel, warm-share limiter W, lineage Grant at Prepare/materializeSlotStages, controller-rendered fill timeout) — BECAUSE warm-share then M4 acquisition has no cycle and holds no fetch slot, Grant is an idempotent keep-later-expiry write repeated safely on slot-bind retries, and a fill cut by gateway shutdown fails its pod, which the controller replaces — ALTERNATIVES: filing shutdown-cut warm fills as lost work; rejected because the pod failure is the stated fill-failure path and the replacement is bounded.

### [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30b]

- DECISION (orchestrator under the owner's standing delegation, 2026-10-30): open decisions 1 to 4 are taken as option B (refuse a cross-tenant private entry on a shared Runtime), option (a) (name ReadCredentialSecret in §16.4 and §4.7.11 item 6), option (a) (spec Role example shows the placeholder), and no INFRA-1 memory ceiling. ### [redesign.reconciliation-2026-10-30c]

- CORRECTS [review-log.md:1381 DECISION, review-log.md:1434 DECISION; repeated at :1590 and :1607]: the alternative "GracefulStop bounded by the step context with a Stop() fallback" was rejected for two harms: the later closes run on an expired shared context, and request_input exit writes are cut. Neither harm follows from a bounded forced stop as such, and both are now constraints every means must meet (CODE-3 step 6 **Shutdown.** rules S3 and S4). The rejection assumed that cancelling the handler ends the call, which review-log.md:1574 refutes: a returned handler's trailer queues behind unsent data when the peer stops reading, and graceful stop waits for the connection. The stalled-consumer path therefore needs some forced end of the transport. The means is now the implementor's choice, pinned by TEST-1 item 13. The 5-second constant adopted at :1574 is withdrawn, because rule S2 takes its bound from `--shutdown-timeout`. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:74-78 (one shared context for the final closes), :178-185 (inputWaits.Close then unbounded GracefulStop), :200-209 (the sqlite close uses that context); cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:594 (`--shutdown-timeout`, default 5s)
- FACT: rule P1's inputs exist on every replica for an unclaimed pod: the pod name is the Sandbox name, SandboxSpec carries required poolRef and runtimeRef, and the gateway ClusterRole grants get on sandboxes. With an audience set, the TokenReview verifier and the cluster client are built in the same --agent-namespace block, so `podIdentityEnforced` is false with an audience set only when no cluster client exists, and the interceptor's unverified decode then serves no FetchGitPack call. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:492; pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/sandbox_types.go:28-35; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:196-198; cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:153-210
- FACT: a CRD-only pool, and every pool on a gateway with no pool store wired, has no poolstore row, and `Sandbox.spec.runtimeRef` is copied from the pool's SandboxTemplate, so CODE-3 step 6 rule P1 resolves the Runtime from the Sandbox. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/controller.go:535-536; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:251-254; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:14-17
- DECISION: one predicate, `podIdentityEnforced` (CODE-2 step 2), gates the handler's pod binding and the registration of a private entry, and §6.4 **Private repository stores.** rule 5 is the normative home of the pod-identity prerequisite (summary open decision 5) — BECAUSE the handler and the admin Router must agree on when a private entry can be served, and the registration refusal is client-visible — ALTERNATIVES: branching the handler on the configured audience alone (equivalent behavior, but registration would need a second condition); leaving the registration half unstaged (decision 5 requires it).

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- DECISION: SPEC-8 Edit (h) gains one sentence after "does not list or watch Secrets.": "It reads every such Secret at startup and again at each resync interval (default: 30 seconds, configurable via `tokenService.secretResyncInterval` Helm value)." The staged "next read" replacements (detection, rotation step 2, **Ordering:**) stay as written; the periodic read or a `ReadCredentialSecret` call is their trigger — BECAUSE without it nothing re-reads a non-VCS pool's Secret, so a credential marked `unavailable` (never selected by assignment) never recovers and rotation is never adopted — ALTERNATIVES: read at each lease issuance or rotation (never reaches an `unavailable` credential; puts an apiserver GET on every LLM lease against client-go default QPS 5); serve `ReadCredentialSecret` from the polled value (reverses summary decision 22 and CODE-7 rule T3); poll non-VCS pools only (an exception clause for no gain); restore "within its resync interval" in three sentences (names no mechanism).

- WATCHOUT: do not re-add "within 30 seconds" to rotation step 2 or rewrite the "next read" sentences; the new sentence's interval bounds them. Do not stage CODE/TEST for the poll in 0111: the tree resolves no lease-material Secret (only `secretprobe` calls `CoreV1().Secrets().Get`; `IncSecretReload` counts signing-secret reloads), so it is filed as a summary defect. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go:82, pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit.go:174-179
- FACT: `tokenService.secretResyncInterval` appears nowhere in charts/, pkg/, cmd/, or docs/; its only spec site is §4.9 **Token Service secret resolution.** (not in §17.8.1). — EVIDENCE: lookup exact over spec, charts, pkg, cmd, docs; spec/04_system-components.md:2186

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-8 Edit (h)'s first replacement text gains one sentence: the Token Service reads every Secret a credential pool references at startup and again at each resync interval (default 30 s, `tokenService.secretResyncInterval`). The "next read" replacements for detection, rotation step 2, and the Ordering bullet stay as staged, because the periodic read (or a `ReadCredentialSecret` call) is now their trigger. The summary Defects list gains one bullet for the missing periodic read in the tree — BECAUSE a periodic `get` is the informer's replacement under a get-only Role: it re-resolves a credential marked `unavailable`, which no assignment ever selects, it restores the existing 30 s rotation bound and Helm value, and it keeps the kube-apiserver off the LLM lease-issuance path — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's "read at each lease issuance or rotation, including for a credential marked `unavailable`" (an assignment never picks an `unavailable` credential, so it still needs a separate retry trigger, and it puts one apiserver GET on every lease against a Token Service clientset at client-go default QPS 5); serving `ReadCredentialSecret` from the polled value (closes the per-call load concern but reverses summary decision 22 and the CODE-7 T3 "no cached client" rule, so it is out of scope for this finding); limiting the poll to non-VCS pools (an exception clause, i.e. hair).
MISTAKE: redesign 3 (scratchpad/redesign/...-redesign-1.md, "Deleted outright": "SPEC-8 Edit (h)'s 'within its resync interval' replacements, because no resync interval exists once the informer is gone") deleted the periodic re-read and did not replace it, which left "the next read" with no trigger for any non-VCS pool. A resync interval does not depend on an informer. A poll of named gets satisfies the get-only Role.
FACT: the tree has no Token Service Secret read apart from `pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go` (`CoreV1().Secrets(ns).Get`). No code resolves a credential pool's `secretRef` into lease material, and `tokenService.secretResyncInterval` appears only in spec/04 **Token Service secret resolution.** (absent from charts/). The periodic read is therefore a spec contract with no code behind it, filed as a summary defect rather than staged in CODE-7. — EVIDENCE: grep `CoreV1().Secrets` over pkg/ and cmd/; grep `secretResync` over charts/, pkg/, cmd/, docs/
WATCHOUT: the periodic read adds N/interval GETs per Token Service replica (N = referenced Secrets) beside CODE-7 T3's per-call reads, all on a clientset at client-go defaults (QPS 5, burst 10; cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go). Whoever implements the filed defect sizes the client QPS. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go clientset construction
CORRECTS [review-log.md "UNVERIFIED: after Edit (h), the §4.9 sentence that keeps 'within its resync interval' ..." (redesign-era spec.1)]: once this fix lands, the periodic read is the mechanism that refreshes the Token Service's in-memory LLM credential cache (§4.3, §13.3 **Translator trust boundary:**). No further sentence is owed.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: one finding. SPEC-8 Edit (j) leaves the §4.3 "Additionally, the gateway caches active credential leases in memory ... grace period proportional to the lease TTL" paragraph unedited, which contradicts the new "VCS credentials have no grace period" bullet — BECAUSE §26.2 and the vcs_token audit treat VCS credentials as leases and Edit (c) gives git-https a leaseTTLSeconds — ALTERNATIVES: filing per-call Secret GET load was rejected (summary decision 22 is binding and the spec states no apiserver read budget).
FACT: the staged spec adds no etcd write; the only new durable write is one Redis ZADD per restore of a derived session (REG-GITLINEAGE), read only on the FetchGitPack fallback branch. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 row, SPEC-10 Edit (a)
FACT: shipped VCS path reads the Secret from the gateway on every operation with no cache (vcscred.StoreResolver -> SecretReader), so "no grace period" matches shipped behavior; only the dependency moves to the Token Service. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/vcscred/vcscred.go:96-131
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md CODE-7]: cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go:84 builds rest.InClusterConfig() with client-go default QPS 5 / burst 10. Under Edit (h)'s per-call Secret get, Tier 3 (about 30 claims/s, two private-key operations per cache-served source, plus every credential-helper git op) exceeds that and queues reads. CODE-7 should set the Token Service rest config QPS/burst (operator-tunable) or state the bound.
DEFERRED [docs]: docs/alerting/rules.yaml:117 and its pkg alerting source, docs/runbooks/token-service-outage.md:32/:104, docs/operator-guide/security.md:133, and docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:192 still say existing sessions are unaffected until lease expiry; after Edit (j), VCS operations fail at once.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
- FACT: tenant pinning holds for the pod's lifetime on every recycling or concurrent pool, and non-recycling single-session pods retire, so the per-pod `git_lineage` record cannot leak a lineage pool to another tenant's later session. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454, :469
- FACT: the Token Service accepts gRPC only from gateway pods (mTLS), so `ReadCredentialSecret` returning Secret values is reachable only by the gateway. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:278
- FACT: current code already has the gateway read VCS Secrets directly (vcscred.SecretReader over connectorsecret.KubeResolver); SPEC-8 Edit (h) moves the read into the Token Service, which tightens the posture. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/vcscred/vcscred.go:66-73
- FACT: git-https delivering a long-lived token to the runtime via lenny/vcs_token has the `anthropic_direct` "Static API key (long-lived)" precedent in the §4.9 provider table; do not file it as a regression. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1578
- WATCHOUT: SPEC-5's podId binding sentence is unconditional, while rule 5, the "gateway does not validate projected service-account tokens" edge case, summary decision 5, and non-spec test 13 all assume a non-validating gateway still serves public entries. Without a validated token, podId is an unauthenticated pod self-report that selects the pool and the lineage record used for authorization. Filed. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:206, :106, :39; summary.md:120; non-spec-changes.md:311
- UNVERIFIED: cross-tenant secretRef (a tenant-admin pointing a VCS pool's secretRef at another tenant's Secret named under lenny-system) predates 0111 and also affects the lease path; nobody has checked whether the admin handler enforces the `lenny-{pool-name}-{credential-id}` naming convention (spec/04:1656). Not filed.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: rotation step 4 (§4.9 **Credential rotation workflow:**) gets a predicate-phrased in-place replacement inside SPEC-8 Edit (h): "Existing active leases continue using the previously materialized credentials until they expire naturally (governed by lease TTL)." becomes "An existing active lease that holds a copy of the previous material keeps using it until the lease expires naturally (governed by lease TTL)." — BECAUSE the sentence then states the deciding property (a held copy), and Edit (j)'s §4.3 VCS bullet ("keeps no copy between operations") already decides it for VCS. No credential type is enumerated, so a later per-operation provider stays correct with no edit. — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "other than VCS leases" carve-out (an enumerated exception, the narrowing that the history warning says fails again); deleting the sentence (removes a statement that is still true for LLM pools, and the revoke sentence loses its antecedent); a pointer to §4.3 (that paragraph covers outages, so it does not own rotation).
WATCHOUT: step 4's third sentence ("use the emergency revocation endpoint ... to force all active leases to re-acquire credentials with the updated material") is imprecise and predates this fix. Revoke marks the credId revoked, so re-acquisition picks another credential. For a VCS pool, Edit (h)'s ReadCredentialSecret refuses a revoked credential, so a single-credential VCS pool fails rather than rotating. This fix does not cause it. If a later round files it, it is a separate finding. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:2193; spec-changes.md:335 ("only for an unrevoked credential of an active VCS pool")
FACT: nothing in proposal 0111 cites rotation step 4 apart from Edit (h)'s step 2 replacement. Proposals 0085, 0108, and 0110 do not touch **Credential rotation workflow:**. docs/runbooks/credential-revocation.md:196 covers the user-credential DELETE row, not pool rotation. — EVIDENCE: grep of proposals/ and docs/ for "Credential rotation workflow|previously materialized|Existing active leases"

DECISION: filed one finding. - FACT: Restore-lineage recovery traced and found consistent. If the gateway crashes between the record write and the restoring call, the entry expires and nothing leaks. A Redis write failure or a lost write on failover leaves the bind proceeding without the record, so a FetchGitPack that needs the record fails, and the next bind attempt rewrites it from Postgres (SPEC-10 Edit (c)). A partial rule 6 completion is idempotent on retry, because `lenny.storeCommit` is written only after the pack is indexed and alternates are overwritten on every attempt. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit (c) rules 6 and 7, SPEC-10 Edit (c)

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: The Token Service clientset takes rest-config QPS/Burst from new Token Service flags `--cluster-qps`/`--cluster-burst` (defaults 100/200, `LENNY_CLUSTER_QPS`/`LENNY_CLUSTER_BURST`), stated in CODE-7 step 7 rule T3 and pinned by a tier-1 flags_test.go row in TEST-1 item 24 — BECAUSE `inClusterClientset()` sets no QPS/Burst, so client-go's 5/10 per replica (about 20 GETs/s at Tier 3's 4 replicas) cannot carry the estimated 240 GETs/s (3 resolutions x 2 keys x about 30 claims/s), and throttled reads time out past `DefaultClientTimeout` (5 s), count against the shared `token_service` breaker, and fail `AssignCredentials` — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's `--kube-api-qps` plus chart values (second spelling of one knob; the gateway chart renders no `--cluster-qps` either); `repeated string keys` on the RPC (new wire surface to halve a load the QPS fix absorbs); a tier-7a 200-concurrent-call row (the fake clientset bypasses the limiter, so it observes nothing); hard-coded constants (no override, against code-best-practices "operator-tunable").
FACT: Token Service clientset: cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go `inClusterClientset()` = `rest.InClusterConfig()` then `kubernetes.NewForConfig(cfg)`, no limiter; its only caller is surfaces.go (secretprobe wiring). Gateway precedent: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go `cluster-qps` envFloat default 100, `cluster-burst` envInt default 200, applied in pod_lifecycle.go. ### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 step 1 decides lineage-pool ownership by pointing at CODE-8 step 3's owner rule (a pool step 3 treats as absent for the row's tenant leaves the lineage pool empty) instead of stating its own "missing or soft-deleted pool" predicate. BECAUSE CODE-8 step 3 is already the one home for "no mirror row / nil reader = no owner, kept; soft-deleted kept when unowned, absent when owned; owned by another tenant = absent". CRD existence is already decided at FetchGitPack time by rule P1 ("a lineage pool whose SandboxWarmPool or SandboxTemplate is missing names no url"), which satisfies spec rule 7's "no longer exists". ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's suggestion to restate the predicate in CODE-9 ("a soft-deleted pool whose row's OwnerTenantID is set" plus a sentence on no-row pools) was rejected because it is a second statement of CODE-8 step 3. Adding a CRD existence read to the helper was rejected because it duplicates P1 and adds a cluster read to the bind path.
FACT: the pgstore `Get` returns soft-deleted rows, and callers consult `Pool.IsActive()`. A missing row is `ErrNotFound`. `poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy` maps `ErrNotFound` to found=false, and `poolPolicyReader()`/`NewPoolPolicyReader` return nil when no pool store is wired. A CRD-only pool therefore has no mirror row. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:263-276; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:18-23,52-60
WATCHOUT: CODE-9 keeps its own read-error rule (any read error leaves the lineage pool empty, logged at INFO). CODE-8 step 3's "A mirror read error fails the resolution" does not carry over through the pointer, so the pointer must name only the "treats as absent" predicate. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-8 step 3 and CODE-9 step 1

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close the M6/M3 contradiction by deleting M6's clause ", and the replacement pod's fill finds the key already mirrored" and leaving M3's last-waiter cancellation as is — BECAUSE a fill that misses the deadline already has an operator remedy (the warm-pool-exhaustion runbook deliverable names `controller.tuning.gitStoreFillTimeout` for `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`), INFRA-1 pass criterion 5 checks M6's inequality at the defaults, and failed-fill churn is the accepted setup_command_failed precedent — ALTERNATIVES: a merged fetch that outlives its waiters (detached context bounded by the lowSpeed settings and the S1 shutdown cancel) was rejected: it rewrites M3 and TEST-1 item 20, adds background work that S1 must also cancel, and holds a fetch slot and disk for a key nobody waits on.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: the runbook's RBAC remedy re-renders only `templates/token-service-rbac.yaml` (`helm template --show-only`) when the Role or RoleBinding is absent, and otherwise appends each denied Secret in the patch form `tokenServiceRBACPatch` emits — BECAUSE kubectl apply (client- or server-side) replaces a Role's `rules` list wholesale (PolicyRule list has no merge key), so re-applying the rendered Role over a live one drops every name the admin API appended and turns one missing grant into an outage for every API-created pool — ALTERNATIVES: fix only the label selector to `lenny.dev/component=token-service` (the finding's suggestion; also re-applies the Deployment/Service/PDB and still drops patched names, and needs a new label on the Role plus a TEST-1 item 33 assertion); keep the selector and label the Role (same drop problem).
DECISION: rename the CREDENTIAL_SECRET_RBAC_MISSING details keys (`missingSecrets`→`resourceNames`, `remediation`→`rbacPatch`) in CODE-7 step 8 — BECAUSE §15.1 is normative and names the §15.1 keys; the only consumers are credential_pools.go and credential_pool_rbac_probe_test.go (no CLI or SDK reads them) — ALTERNATIVES: point TEST-1 items 21/35 at `details.remediation` and file the drift in the defects list (keeps code off-spec, two edits instead of one).
FACT: the shipped 422 is built at pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go (writeError with `missingSecrets`/`remediation`); `tokenServiceRBACPatch` hardcodes `-n lenny-system` and appends the raw secretRef. The Kind install namespace is lenny-system. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:388-393, :437-446
FACT: the token-service ServiceAccount carries only `lenny.labels` (no `lenny.dev/component`), and no chart object carries `app.kubernetes.io/component=token-service`, so the shipped runbook Remediation Step 4 applies nothing. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/token-service-deployment.yaml:279-284, charts/lenny/templates/_helpers.tpl:6-14
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: SPEC-8 Edit (j) says "Leave the rest of the item unchanged." for §17.7 **Token Service outage**, but Edit (l) gives the §4.9 Role a non-empty `resourceNames`, so §17.7 *Diagnosis* (5) `kubectl auth can-i get secret -n lenny-system --as=...` answers `no` on a correct install. True instead: (5) checks `get secret/<name>` for a Secret a credential pool references. The spec loop should add that replacement to Edit (j).
OPEN: separate finding for a later round — §17.7 *Remediation* (4) `kubectl apply -f charts/lenny/templates/token-service-rbac.yaml` applies an unrendered Helm template (it was already wrong; the file did not exist before 0111) and would drop admin-appended names. It belongs with the DEFERRED Edit (j) addition above.
OPEN: separate finding — `tokenServiceRBACPatch` appends the whole secretRef as the resourceName, so a two- or three-segment ref (`ns/name`) appends `ns/name`, which never matches. CODE-7 step 8's Role takes the second segment as the name. It also hardcodes `-n lenny-system` while CODE-7 step 8 puts the Role in the release namespace. Either stage the fix in CODE-7 step 8 or file it in the summary defects list.
OPEN: separate finding — a `helm upgrade` that changes the seeded pool set re-renders the Role's `rules` and drops names the admin API appended (rbacPatch mutates a Helm-managed object). Nothing in the proposal states that interaction.
WATCHOUT: lookup was not used for this design; every search was grep or sed over pkg/, charts/, docs/runbooks, spec/17, spec/15, and the proposal files.

### [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30c]

- DECISION (orchestrator under the owner's standing delegation, 2026-10-30): the Token Service Secret access path is settled as follows. (1) Role ownership: the chart render is the single owner of the `lenny-token-service-secrets` Role's resourceNames. The admin API never patches the live Role; when a credential pool names a Secret the Role does not cover, the API answers with the values change and `helm upgrade` instruction, so a later `helm upgrade` cannot drop names. (2) secretRef parsing: one rule shared by the chart render, rule T3, the prober, and the admin API. `<namespace>/<name>` names both, and a one-segment ref names a Secret in the release namespace. (3) Read failure: a failed read of a credential Secret fails that call closed with the transient category, with no stale value served. A warm fill retries on the next cycle, and a session-time consumer returns its transient error.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §4.6.1 etcd bullet gets an exception that points at §4.9 **Token Service secret resolution.** (cause) and §4.3 **High availability and failure handling:** (effect), and its bold label drops "unaffected". The exception is placed in SPEC-8 Edit (j) — BECAUSE a client-go `Get` with no resourceVersion is a quorum read that needs etcd, so the per-call read fails during an etcd outage, and §4.3 already owns the outage effect — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's text enumerating VCS operations (rejected: restates §4.3 and misses any other Secret read that Edit (h) makes per call).
DECISION: the exception is worded on the API server being unable to read etcd. With that wording the §4.6.1 quota-exhaustion parenthetical ("existing sessions continue as described in 'etcd unavailability' below") needs no edit, because reads still succeed under a quota alarm — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:481.
DECISION: secret-reloads metric. In the Edit (h) paragraph, the §4.9 sentence becomes a pointer to §16.1 that binds the metric to each read. The §16.1 row is the one home of the outcome set, widened to success, not_found, denied, unavailable, and parse_error. CODE-7 step 7 T4 records the outcome — ALTERNATIVES: widen the set in both §4.9 and §16.1 (rejected: two copies of one closed set); delete the metric everywhere (rejected: removes the per-Secret diagnosis signal, and the cascade into catalog.go, catalog_test, promemit, and docs is larger than the finding).
FACT: in the tree, nothing increments `lenny_token_service_secret_reloads_total`. `promemit.IncSecretReload` has no production caller, its counter is labeled `outcome` only, and its comment describes it as a signing-secret (KEK) reload with outcomes success and failure. The only Token Service Secret `Get` in the tree is secretprobe — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit.go:60-65,173-179; pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go:82.
WATCHOUT: once T4 records the outcome, `IncSecretReload` needs a `secret_name` label to match the §16.1 "by secret name" row. A fixer who wires T4 to the existing one-label counter leaves spec and code disagreeing — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit.go:64.

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
- DECISION: filed no findings on SPEC-8 — BECAUSE every SPEC-8 edit anchor resolves in the current spec, and the read model (Edit (h)), outage effect (Edit (j)), Role rules 1 to 3 (Edit (l)), and the citers (Edit (m)) are each stated in one home. Each one traces end to end without a dead trigger or a bypassed gate — ALTERNATIVES: the foreign-namespace admin ref dead end (DENIED, then a 422 that points at rule 3, then a render failure) is the staged Option A of the OPEN in [redesign.token-service-secret-access.apply], so it was not filed.
- FACT: SPEC-8 anchors verified against the tree: §4.3 HA bullets (spec/04_system-components.md:224-228), the Role example and paragraph (spec/04:1682-1695), the live-probe (spec/04:1697), Tier 3 (spec/04:1699), the emergency runbook "(5) add a replacement credential" (spec/04:2164), initial setup step 2 (spec/04:2183), secret resolution and rotation (spec/04:2186-2199), §4.7.11 item 6 (spec/04:1377), §16.4 (spec/16_observability.md:389), §16.5 `TokenServiceUnavailable` (spec/16:430), §17.7 (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:806, 826-828), §24.5 add-credential (spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:90), the §28.7 tokenservice row (spec/28_communication-channels.md:2053), and the §13.2.4 Token Service row (spec/13_security-model.md:278).
- FACT: the Token Service has no Secret read in code today other than the probe (pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go:82), so Edit (h)'s per-call model conflicts with no existing informer. `lenny_token_service_secret_reloads_total` is wired only as a signing-secret reload counter (pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit.go:174-180). Its §16.1 outcome labels (spec/16:97) predate 0111. — EVIDENCE: grep of cmd/lenny-token-service and pkg/tokenservice

Edit (k) names `ReadCredentialSecret` explicitly as a Token Service RPC inside §4.7.11 item 6, so the item is not misattributed. — EVIDENCE: spec/04:1296 heading "Adapter-Agent Security Boundary"

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
- FACT: the shipped gateway already reads a VCS Secret from the kube-apiserver on every resolve (vcscred.StoreResolver -> connectorsecret.NewKubeResolver, cmd/lenny-gateway/auxstores.go:81), so the SPEC-8 Edit (h) per-call `get` moves that apiserver read rate into the Token Service and does not add to it. No informer exists in pkg/tokenservice; only secretprobe calls Secrets().Get. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/vcscred/vcscred.go:96-130, pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go:82
- FACT: the spec states no kube-apiserver read budget for the Token Service. The only apiserver QPS figures are the controller's (§4.6.1, spec/04:459). Tier 3 runs 4 Token Service replicas (spec/17:1246). EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:459, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1246
- WATCHOUT: before Edit (h), the informer cache let the Token Service ride out an etcd outage. With a per-call `get` it no longer does, which falsifies the §4.6.1 **etcd unavailability (degraded mode):** bullet "Existing sessions continue unaffected" (spec/04:504) for VCS credential operations. The bullet is not in SPEC-8 Edit (j), so this run files it. The §4.6.1 quota-exhaustion parenthetical (spec/04:481) only points at that bullet, so it needs no edit. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:504

EVIDENCE: summary.md:23,70-73; spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit (c)
DECISION: no plan-conformance findings in spec round 1 — BECAUSE nothing the plan entry requires is absent and unrecorded — ALTERNATIVES: filing the shallow-vs-full-clone fallback was rejected because Decision 8 records the divergence with a reason.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: In the current tree the Token Service has no Secret informer and no lease-path Secret read. Its only Secret `get` is the live probe (pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go:82). Removing the informer in SPEC-8 Edit (h) therefore drops no cache that code relies on. The gateway currently reads VCS Secrets directly, once per Resolve, with no cache (pkg/gateway/provisioning/vcscred/vcscred.go:96-131). — EVIDENCE: as cited
- FACT: When the Token Service is down, the private-entry credential resolution fails and `FetchGitPack` answers `PERMISSION_DENIED`, not `UNAVAILABLE`. That follows the §4.7.1 rule 4 refusal and the CODE-3 step 6 status table (non-spec-changes.md:104). §6.2 sends setup-window failures to the retryable STARTING_FAILED, so the adapter-side outcome is the same either way. Do not file it as a reliability defect. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:206, :232
- WATCHOUT: "marked `unavailable`" (spec/04:2184 initial setup step 3, :2199 Ordering bullet) has no persisted mechanism in code. Under the per-call `get` model, "re-resolved at the next read" heals trivially. Step 3's proactive "log errors ... and mark" at bootstrap is now loosely worded, but no recovery depends on it. — EVIDENCE: grep of pkg/credential and credentialpoolstore finds no unavailable state
- DECISION: returned no findings for SPEC-8 under the reliability lens — BECAUSE the outage semantics (no earlier value, retryable, shared breaker) are binding per [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30c], §4.3, §16.5, and §17.7 cite the §4.3 home consistently, and the staged spec adds no durable write, lease, or retry loop — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the breaker cascade, where apiserver-caused Unavailable reads open the shared token_service breaker that blocks Assign (non-spec item 39). Rejected it because it is a consequence of the binding "counts as Token Service unavailability" decision.
- USEFUL [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30c]: it settled the read model and the outage semantics up front, which closed most reliability questions on SPEC-8.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
- DECISION: no security findings on SPEC-8 (Edits (h), (i), (j), (l), (m)) — BECAUSE every changed control fails closed: the per-call `get` serves no earlier value on a failed read (Edit (h)); the Role's placeholder-first `resourceNames` avoids the empty-list-grants-all RBAC semantics (Edit (l) rule 2); a cross-namespace ref is denied by RBAC and fails the render; the admin probe still rejects on DENIED, NOT_FOUND, and transport failure; a Token Service outage fails VCS operations and private-mirror reads (Edit (j)) — ALTERNATIVES: (a) rule 4 letting `lenny/vcs_token` resolve without a `leaseScope`, rejected because the current tool already resolves by caller-chosen host and provider with no plan gating (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go:991-1003), so no control regresses; (b) long-lived git-https secret reaching the pod, rejected per earlier rounds (anthropic_direct precedent); (c) cross-tenant secretRef, pre-existing per [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30c]; (d) cross-namespace admin ref, an OPEN in [redesign.token-service-secret-access.apply].
- FACT: no other spec or docs site describes Token Service egress, so Edit (i) on the §13.2.4 row is the only network site. — EVIDENCE: lookup exact `Token Service.{0,200}egress` over spec and docs (only spec/13:185, :237 link-TLS mentions, docs/reference/configuration.md:289 KMS)
- USEFUL [standing context, Token Service Secret access]: the read-model, Role, and cross-tenant entries closed three candidate findings without re-derivation.

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
- FACT: the spec budgets no kube-apiserver read rate for any component. Only the warm pool controller's QPS buckets (§4.6.1, §17.8.2) are spec-stated, and the gateway's `--cluster-qps` flag help says the spec leaves gateway client throttling to operator tuning. So the SPEC-8 Edit (h) per-call Secret `get` exceeds no stated budget. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:456-459, cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:872
- FACT: client-go's default throttle (QPS 5, burst 10) would choke per-call reads at Tier 3 (4 Token Service replicas, ~30 claims/s plus a read for every VCS git operation). CODE-7 rule T3 already closes this with `--cluster-qps`/`--cluster-burst` (100/200) and a flags_test row. Do not re-file it as a spec defect. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:197, :348; cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go:84-88
- WATCHOUT: under Edit (h), an apiserver 429 or timeout on a Secret `get` counts against the shared `token_service` breaker, so apiserver load shedding can block LLM session starts. It also gives VCS credentials no grace period. Both follow from binding decisions ([orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30c] outage semantics, summary decision 23 shared breaker). Do not file them as performance findings. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:335, :346
- FACT: Tier 3 read estimate is roughly 30/s session claims plus 30/s warm refills plus two reads per private source per materialization (FetchGitPack plus the rule 2 check, SPEC-2 (c) rule 4) plus credential-helper operations. That is low hundreds of etcd-backed GETs per second, all reads, with no etcd writes, no new watch, and no informer memory (the shipped Secret informer is removed). — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:105 rule 4, spec/04_system-components.md:475

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 step 7 gains rule T5 as the one home of which ReadCredentialSecret request records which `lenny_token_service_secret_reloads_total` outcome, through a new `tokenservice.Metrics.IncSecretReload(secretName, outcome string)`; T2's `ParseSecretRef` rejection produces `parse_error` (labeled with the ref as written), so every §16.1 outcome SPEC-8 Edit (h) lists has a producer and spec-changes.md needs no edit — BECAUSE the handler reaches metrics only through the `tokenservice.Metrics` interface `GRPCServer.SetMetrics` wires — ALTERNATIVES: "T1/T2 refusal records nothing" (leaves parse_error without a producer); dropping parse_error from §16.1 (needs a spec edit); a second metrics setter or a direct promemit call (second surface, breaks the package's Prometheus-free split).
WATCHOUT: the types satisfying `tokenservice.Metrics` are `promemit.Emitter`, `tokenservice.NoMetrics`, and the `recordingMetrics` fake in pkg/tokenservice/audit_test.go (also used by revocation_propagation_metric_test.go); promemit_test.go calls `IncSecretReload("success")` directly and moves to two arguments. Do not wire T5 to the existing one-label counter. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/metrics.go:18-79, pkg/tokenservice/audit_test.go:58-106, pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit.go:61-64,174-181, promemit_test.go:27
WATCHOUT: T2's pool-absent, non-VCS, missing-credential, and revoked refusals involve no Secret and record no outcome; only the ref rejection inside T2 records (parse_error). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 step 7 rule T5
DECISION: the gateway ReadCredentialSecret client runs under `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), c.timeout)` — BECAUSE `Subsystem.Do` records every non-nil fn error as a breaker failure and `countsAgainstBreaker` lists Canceled and DeadlineExceeded, so caller cancellations would open the replica-wide breaker; `assign`/`revoke` already bound calls with `c.timeout` on a detached context — ALTERNATIVES: a carve-out returning the ctx error without recording a failure (needs plumbing past Subsystem.Do; never arises under WithoutCancel); `context.Background()` (drops trace values and discards the SecretReader ctx); classify by ctx.Err() (changes callTokenService for one caller). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/core/subsystem/subsystem.go:86-93, pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:176,284-296,390
CORRECTS [non-spec.1 FACT at review-log.md:2150]: "a ReadCredentialSecret cancelled by its caller maps to ErrTokenServiceUnavailable ... below the bar" no longer holds; under WithoutCancel a caller cancellation never reaches the RPC or the breaker.
FACT: DOCS-1's metrics.md bullet now covers the `lenny_token_service_secret_reloads_total` row (docs/reference/metrics.md:212), closing the DEFERRED at review-log.md:2190 and the OPEN/DEFERRED at :2188 via rule T5 and TEST-1 item 24.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
- DECISION: the secret-reloads counter is recorded by a new CODE-7 step 7 rule T5 through a new `tokenservice.Metrics` method `IncSecretReload(secretName, outcome string)`, and T2's `ParseSecretRef` rejection is the producer of `parse_error` (labeled with the ref as written) — BECAUSE the handler reaches metrics only through `GRPCServer.metrics` (`tokenservice.Metrics`, wired by `tsGRPC.SetMetrics(w.metricsEmitter)`), and mapping `parse_error` to the one parse the handler does gives every §16.1 outcome a producer with no spec edit — ALTERNATIVES: "T1 or T2 refusal records nothing" as the finding suggested (leaves `parse_error` in the §16.1 row with no producer, which would need a spec-changes edit this loop cannot make); a separate setter on `GRPCServer` for a secret-read callback (a second metrics surface beside the existing interface); calling the concrete `*promemit.Emitter` from the handler (breaks the package's interface split).
- DECISION: `ReadCredentialSecret` runs under `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), c.timeout)` — BECAUSE it matches `assign`/`revoke` (client.go:176, :390) so a caller cancellation never becomes a Canceled/DeadlineExceeded status and the existing "statuses `countsAgainstBreaker` lists" return rule stays true without a carve-out; `WithoutCancel` keeps ctx values (trace metadata) that `context.Background()` drops — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's "caller ctx ended → no breaker failure, return the context error" clause (an exception rule needing out-of-band plumbing through `Subsystem.Do`, which records every non-nil error from fn as a failure); dropping the ctx parameter to mirror `Assign` (vcscred.SecretReader.Read already takes ctx, and the method would discard trace context).
- FACT: implementers of `tokenservice.Metrics` are `promemit.Emitter`, `tokenservice.NoMetrics`, and the `recordingMetrics` fake in `pkg/tokenservice/audit_test.go`; `gatewaymetrics.Metrics` and `recordingRevocationObserver` implement other interfaces. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/metrics.go:19, pkg/tokenservice/audit_test.go:82-106, cmd/lenny-token-service/surfaces.go:72
- FACT: `promemit_test.go:27` calls `IncSecretReload("success")`, the only caller in the tree; the signature change breaks it, so it is in Files touched. `catalog.go:192` help ("Token Service secret reloads by outcome") is not compared to the promemit Help by any test and stays true. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit_test.go:27, pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:192
- WATCHOUT: with `WithoutCancel`, a FetchGitPack call cancelled by the S1 shutdown step still waits up to the client timeout (default 5s) for an in-flight read. That bound is intended; do not "fix" it by threading the caller's cancellation back in, which reopens the breaker defect. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:112, :284-296; pkg/gateway/core/subsystem/subsystem.go:86-91
- USEFUL [spec.1.fix-G1.1]: its DEFERRED stated the T4 metric and the `secret_name` label gap precisely; the WATCHOUT at review-log line ~2198 (one-label counter) is the trap this design closes.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: pool update (PUT /v1/admin/credential-pools/{name}) joins the 400 VALIDATION_ERROR handler list in CODE-7 step 8 and TEST-1 item 40 by inserting "pool update," and nothing more — BECAUSE PUT is a full replace, so the body's credential list is the new set, and the existing wording "a non-empty `secretRef`" already covers every credential in that body. Validation runs on every body ref, not only the ones `newSecretRefs` diffs out for the probe, which is the simple fail-closed reading — ALTERNATIVES: validate only the introduced refs (rejected: it adds a partial-update clause, and with no deployments there is no legacy invalid stored ref to protect); a sentence spelling out PUT semantics (rejected: the existing words already cover it, so it would be hair).
FACT: handleUpdateCredentialPool probes only `newSecretRefs(current.Credentials, body.Credentials)` and only when `r.secretProber != nil`, so the ParseSecretRef check has to sit ahead of that `if` block, or a nil prober persists an invalid ref — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:697-705
