### [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30d]

- DECISION (owner, 2026-10-30): credential Secrets live in a dedicated namespace that holds nothing else, readable namespace-wide by the Token Service. Both ways of adding a credential are supported: (A) someone with cluster access creates the Secret and registers it by secretRef, and (B) a tenant admin sends the credential value through the admin API and Lenny writes the Secret into that namespace. This supersedes [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30c] item 1 (chart-owned resourceNames). The whole Token Service Secret access path, covering the namespace, the Role, the write path, the secretRef rule, read-failure semantics, the admin-API remediation, and the §17.7 runbook, moves to a new prerequisite proposal 0112. 0111 keeps its git-specific use, cites 0112, and lands after it.


### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: §6.4 **Private repository stores.** rule 4 (relabelled **Mirror.**) is the spec home of the gateway mirror limits. One appended sentence states that each replica bounds its mirrors by the §17.8.1 disk budget and concurrency limits, deletes the least-recently-used mirror of any key to stay in budget, and that an operation that still does not fit fails the fill, materialization, or restore completion that needed it. Added an Edge-cases bullet citing rule 4, and deleted summary Decision 24's last sentence ("§6.4 gains no statement of the mirror limits ..."). BECAUSE CODE-3 step 7 rules M1/M2 and DOCS-1 published a session-visible failure and cross-tenant LRU deletion that had no spec basis, and the SPEC-11 row cited rule 4 for limits it did not state. ALTERNATIVES: re-pointing SPEC-11 at **Repository stores.** (states no limit either, and covers only the fill); a new rule 6 **Mirror limits.** (splits one concept that SPEC-5 and §14 rule 2 cite as rule 4); stating values, the measure, or RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED in spec (Decision 24 keeps the values out of the spec, and those details belong to CODE-3 step 7).
WATCHOUT: the rule 4 limit sentence uses "fails" and must never say "refuses". The §4.7.1 `FetchGitPack` row maps "a call that ... rule 4 or rule 5 refuses" to PERMISSION_DENIED, while CODE-3 step 6 maps budget exhaustion to RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED. Rewording to "refuses" silently moves the budget failure under PERMISSION_DENIED. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 `FetchGitPack` row; non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 6 table row "rule M2 leaves the mirror over `maxBytes`".
FACT: the §17.8.1 anchor is `17_deployment-topology.md#1781-operational-defaults--quick-reference` (double hyphen), as used in spec/29. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:899, spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:914
FACT: CODE-3 step 7's claim that it is "the one home of the mirror's keys, disk, concurrency, waiting, and memory" stays true as the implementation home. Rule M1 already cites §6.4 rule 4 for the key, and M2 implements the spec bound and outcome. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 7.
CORRECTS [summary Decision 24]: it said §6.4 gains no statement of the mirror limits and that every non-fill mirror refusal is an implementation bound. Rule 4 now states the bound and the outcome, and the limit values stay out of the spec.


### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §6.4 **Private repository stores.** rule 4 becomes the spec home of the per-replica mirror limits and of the exhausted-disk-budget outcome. One appended sentence cites §17.8.1 for the limits, states the LRU deletion across keys of any tenant, and states that an operation that still does not fit fails the fill, materialization, or restore completion that needed it. The rule 4 label shortens to **Mirror.**, and summary Decision 24's last sentence ("§6.4 gains no statement of the mirror limits ...") is deleted. BECAUSE the SPEC-11 §17.8.1 row cites rule 4, DOCS-1 publishes the RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED remedy, and cross-tenant deletion is a tenant-visible accepted behavior, so spec-first development needs a spec statement. ALTERNATIVES: re-point the SPEC-11 row at **Repository stores.** (rejected: that paragraph also states no limit, and materialization and restore failures would still have no spec basis); state the limits in a new rule 6 (rejected: rule 4 already defines the mirror for public and private keys, and a new rule adds a label and a number to cite).
WATCHOUT: the appended rule 4 sentence must use "fails" and never "refuses". SPEC-5's §4.7.1 FetchGitPack row maps "a call that ... rule 4 or rule 5 refuses" to PERMISSION_DENIED, while CODE-3 step 6 gives the budget failure RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED. Rule 4's credential clause keeps "refuses the operation". EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:106, :207; non-spec-changes.md:108
FACT: §17.8.1's anchor is `17_deployment-topology.md#1781-operational-defaults--quick-reference` (see spec/10_gateway-internals.md:158). EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:899
WATCHOUT: CODE-3 step 7 rule M2 stays the implementation home for the measure, the lock-without-waiting, the requested-key-last ordering, and the status. M1 already pairs "the key §6.4 rule 4 defines" with "one home of the mirror's keys", so the step 7 "one home" sentence means the implementation home and needs no edit. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:112-114

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no spec-scope performance or reliability findings in this round — BECAUSE the only net-new durable state is the Redis `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:git_lineage` sorted set (SPEC-10). It is written once per restore call of a session carrying `parent_workspace_ref` and read by `FetchGitPack` only for a commit-SHA want whose url the pod's Runtime lacks, so its rate is negligible at Tier 3 (30 claims/s). It is pod-scoped with no hot key, and it fails closed with reconstruction on the next bind attempt. A Redis reset that loses a written record yields a deny at FetchGitPack, which takes the same fail-and-retry path as the unavailable case. No etcd write is added, and Postgres reads (lineage pool, credential binding) sit on paths that already need Postgres — ALTERNATIVES: rejected the git-host outage, per-call ls-remote, gateway-drain-kills-fills, and per-call Secret GET leads (all refuted or rejected in earlier rounds, see review-log USEFUL at :1182 and :1191). Also rejected the 2-GETs-per-operation git-https amplification (about 180 Secret GETs/s at Tier 3 worst case), because no spec section states a Token Service apiserver read budget (grep `QPS` in spec/ finds only the §17.8.2 controller limiters).
FACT: 0112's staged **Token Service secret resolution.** rule 2 makes each keyed read one apiserver `get` with no cache, so 0111's **`git-https` credentials.** "reads both keys" means two GETs per git operation. EVIDENCE: proposals/0112_*/…spec-changes.md:213; 0111 spec-changes.md:289,43
WATCHOUT: rule 7's lineage expiry says "that call's bound" and gives only the resume example (300s). For a first bind the bound is the `finalizing` watchdog (`maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds`, 600s, which starts at entry to `finalizing`), or the `/start` window on concurrent-workspace pools. I judged this interpretable rather than a defect. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:255; spec-changes.md:157
USEFUL [review-log Traps "Do not re-file"]: the refuted perf leads list saved re-tracing outage and pack-cost math.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
- DECISION: no security findings on the staged spec edits after the 0112 rebase — BECAUSE the git_lineage record cannot be read across tenants (the tenant pin applies on every pool, spec/05:469, spec/04:1403-1409), the record is gateway-written and its Redis outage fails the fetch (SPEC-10 Edit (c)), the private-entry credential is always resolved for the pool's ownerTenantId (SPEC-2 (c) rule 4), and SPEC-8 (b2) reads the git-https keys through 0112's keyed read (0112 SPEC-3 (c) rules 3 and 4 support a named key) — ALTERNATIVES: filing a tenant-admin pool write that sets ownerTenantId on a pool pointing at another tenant's private entry; rejected because rule 4 then resolves the writer's own credential under the key (writer, url), so nothing leaks.
- FACT: a tenant-admin can update but not create Runtimes and pools granted to its tenant (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:289, :326), so the SPEC-2 (c) rule 2 tenant-admin Runtime-write clause and its §10.2 citation are accurate. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:326
- FACT: the §13.4 allowSymlinks rule confines symlink targets to `/workspace/slots/{sessionId}/current` (spec/13_security-model.md:761), so the rule 3 checkout at `path` cannot be redirected outside the build tree by an earlier source's symlink.
- USEFUL [review-log:870, :1197, :1281, :1812]: the long-lived git-https credential reaching the pod through `lenny/vcs_token` and the SPEC-8 (g) §26.3 "short-lived token scoped to the single repo" replacement were already settled as following the `anthropic_direct` precedent. Do not re-file them.
